# `landiskaux` — PASS 1: landisk's wdc alt-status register is mapped 0x1da bytes from where the guest writes, and the fix is one `case`

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this brief's author stated wrongly.
**Check the premises, not just the derivations.** Where I state something as fact, ask whether it
is true. A seat that re-derives a premise from something this brief did NOT give it is worth more
than a seat that agrees.

**THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`.** There are THREE pristine-2021-upstream copies of
GXemul under the project root (`./src/` and two under `_archive/audit-results-*/builds/`). They
are NOT this fork. If your line numbers disagree with mine by hundreds you are reading one of
them — say so rather than reconciling.

**If you cannot read files that is expected** — every fact you need is inlined. Name any premise
you cannot settle from this packet rather than guessing at it.

**Where this brief states an ARITHMETIC claim — an address, a span, an offset — ENUMERATE it
rather than reasoning about it.** A derivation that skips one multiplier reads exactly like a
correct one, and this round is *entirely* one multiplier.

**VERDICT LINE FIRST**, so your answer survives truncation.

---

## 1. PREMISES ALREADY SETTLED, RE-INLINED SO NOBODY SPENDS AN ANSWER RE-DERIVING THEM

Settled 2026-09-18 by opening each file (re-derive any of them; do not take them from me):

* **`dev_wdc.c:2098` switches on `devinit->machine->machine_type` with exactly THREE cases** —
  `MACHINE_MACPPC` (`+0x160`), `MACHINE_HPCMIPS` (a hardcoded `0x14000386`, itself guarded on
  `devinit->addr == 0x14000180` and carrying a `/* TODO: Fix */`), and `MACHINE_IQ80321`
  (`+0x402`). **There is no `MACHINE_LANDISK`.**
* **There is NO `default:` arm.** The switch ends at `:2110` with a bare `}`. The value comes from
  a PRE-SWITCH initialisation at `:2095`: `alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x206;`. *(The queue
  row said "falls through to the default"; the substance is right and the word is not, and it
  matters here because a `case` that assigns nothing simply keeps `:2095`'s value.)*
* **`alt_status_addr` is NOT inside the main device mapping.** `:2120` registers a SEPARATE
  **two-byte** device, `"wdc_altstatus"`, at that address. `:2122` registers the main device at
  `devinit->addr` for `DEV_WDC_LENGTH * devinit->addr_mult` bytes.
* **`DEV_WDC_LENGTH` is 8** (`dev_wdc.c:45`), and **landisk passes `addr_mult=2`** — read from the
  device string itself at `machine_landisk.c:87-88`:
  `"wdc irq=%s.cpu[%i].irq[0x%x] addr_mult=2 addr=0x14000000"`.
* **`devinit->addr_mult` exists** (`src/include/device.h:53`, `int addr_mult`) and `dev_wdc.c`
  already uses it at `:1174` (`relative_addr /= d->addr_mult`), `:2083` and `:2123`.
* **`MACHINE_LANDISK` is 6004** (`src/include/machine.h:268`).

Settled 2026-08-27 by a MEASURED BOOT CENSUS (`regress/artifacts/landiskaux/census_20260827/`),
which this brief's first draft did not carry — the omission it exists to avoid:

* **OpenBSD 7.6 issues exactly TWELVE byte writes to `0x1400002c` and ZERO reads**, reproduced on
  FOUR runs across TWO independent instruments (three pristine boots, one census-hook build).
* All twelve are `len=1`, all from `pc=0x8c15f6e8 <obio_iomem_write_1+0x8>`, all `no_exceptions=0`
  — **real guest stores, not debugger reads.**
* Six carry `0x08` (WDCTL_4BIT), five `0x02` (WDCTL_IDS), and **the FIRST carries `0x0c` =
  `WDCTL_RST | WDCTL_4BIT` — a real ATA SOFT RESET, silently discarded today.**
* **LIVENESS CONTROL from the same boot:** the command block at `0x14000000..0x1400000f` SUCCEEDS
  — 12,718 accesses, `wd0` attaches with correct geometry — so `devinit->addr` is RIGHT and the
  defect is a missing offset case, not a wrong base.
* `wdc_altstatus` at `0x14000206` receives **ZERO accesses for the entire boot.**

Settled 2026-09-11 by a codex FIX-DESIGN run (`codex_fixdesign_20260911.txt`), each re-checked here:

* `dev_wdc_altstatus_access` does **NOT** divide `relative_addr` by `addr_mult` — it tests
  `relative_addr != 0` and nothing else. **So the primary's two-byte stride does not require a
  two-byte auxiliary registration.**
* **Twelve one-byte writes at offset 0 cannot distinguish a length-1 from a length-2
  registration** — already filed as `wdcaltwidth`.
* **Direct calls to the auxiliary handler cannot test the missing machine case**, since they
  bypass init and address dispatch.
* Codex **declined to derive the replacement address from the permitted files**, calling it
  "Verified `A`". *That refusal is the live question this panel inherits — see Q2.*

**THE ARITHMETIC, enumerated rather than asserted:**

    main wdc device : 0x14000000 .. 0x14000000 + 8*2 - 1  =  0x14000000..0x1400000f   (16 bytes)
                      -- which is exactly what machine_landisk.c:86's own comment claims,
                         "wdc0 at obio0 port 0x14000000-0x1400000f irq 10"
    altstatus TODAY : 0x14000000 + 0x206                  =  0x14000206   (2 bytes)
    guest WRITES    : 0x1400002c                          (twelve measured Device Control writes)
    fix would give  : 0x14000000 + 0x16 * 2               =  0x1400002c
    collision check : 0x2c = 44 >= 16, so the two-byte altstatus lands BEYOND the main span.
                      NO COLLISION.  (Nobody had run this check; it is the one that could have
                      made the fix register a device inside an existing one.)

---

## 2. THE FULL ENCLOSING BLOCK (comments trimmed; every exit path shown)

```c
	alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x206;

	/*  Special hacks for individual machines:  */
	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
	case MACHINE_MACPPC:
		alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x160;
		break;
	case MACHINE_HPCMIPS:
		/*  TODO: Fix  */
		if (devinit->addr == 0x14000180)
			alt_status_addr = 0x14000386;
		break;
	case MACHINE_IQ80321:
		alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x402;
		break;
	}

	/*  Get disk geometries:  */
	for (i=0; i<2; i++)
		if (diskimage_exist(devinit->machine, d->base_drive +i, DISKIMAGE_IDE))
			diskimage_getchs(devinit->machine, d->base_drive + i,
			    DISKIMAGE_IDE, &d->cyls[i], &d->heads[i], &d->sectors_per_track[i]);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "wdc_altstatus",
	    alt_status_addr, 2, dev_wdc_altstatus_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
	    devinit->addr, DEV_WDC_LENGTH * devinit->addr_mult, dev_wdc_access,
	    d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
```

## 3. THE PROPOSED FIX — one `case`, and it is the whole diff

```c
	case MACHINE_LANDISK:
		alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x16 * devinit->addr_mult;
		break;
```

---

## 4. THE QUESTIONS

**Q1 — IS THE MULTIPLIER FORM RIGHT, OR SHOULD IT BE A LITERAL?** The three existing cases all use
a BARE offset (`+0x160`, `+0x402`) or a hardcoded absolute (`0x14000386`); **not one of them
multiplies by `addr_mult`.** So the prescribed line is the only case of its kind in the switch. Is
`0x16 * addr_mult` the right shape — it tracks the register-spacing the machine declares — or is
the house idiom here a plain `+ 0x2c`, with the multiplier an over-generalisation from a single
data point? *Both resolve to the same address today; they differ only if a future landisk variant
declares a different `addr_mult`.*

**Q2 — WHERE DOES 0x16 COME FROM, AND IS IT LOAD-BEARING?** The claim is that the OpenBSD 7.6
driver writes `0x1400002c`, and `0x2c / 2 == 0x16`. **A wrong multiplier maps the register
SILENTLY** — no crash, no diagnostic, just a control register nobody reads. What independent
evidence fixes 0x16 rather than 0x2c-as-a-literal? Is there an SH4/landisk source that states the
alt-status/Device-Control register's offset in *register units*?

**Q3 — THE WITNESS.** *The row recorded `witness: none` until today, and a measured rung-4 boot
census had been sitting in its own artifacts directory since 2026-08-27 — corrected before this
brief was fired.*  What remains genuinely open is clause (i): a rung-2..4 witness is INVALID
unless it shows the symptom pre-fix AND goes green post-fix **with the same script**.  Only the
pre-fix half can exist yet. **That is an argument about ONE instrument, not a demonstration that no guest-path
witness exists.** Per this project's 2026-08-27 lifecycle ruling, a round may not be recorded as
rung-1 until one seat has been asked to try a rung-3 probe — and the last time that ask was made, a
seat built a witness in a SINGLE invocation. **Can a rung-3 probe reach this?** A guest store to
`0x1400002c` on unmodified `-E landisk` should be swallowed today and answered by `wdc_altstatus`
after the fix; a store to `0x14000206` should show the mirror image. If you can name that probe,
say so concretely; if you are confident none exists, say why in terms that are not about the
offline differential.

**Q4 — THE OPTION SET.** This is an ADDITION to product code, and the ADDITION LICENCE says
additions are licensed by a MEASURED DELTA or a TRANSFERRED WARRANT, never by "it cannot hurt".
The claimed delta is twelve measured Device Control writes landing on unmapped memory. **Is that
delta real and is the addition the right member?** Name the members you would consider — including
ones this brief has not: is there a case for fixing `machine_landisk.c` instead (declare the
altstatus address at the machine, not in the device), or for the HPCMIPS-style absolute?

**Q5 — WHAT IS THE SMALLEST EDIT** to the block in §2 that breaks a property this fix claims and
still passes whatever detector ships with it? And — separately — **does adding a fourth `case`
create any hazard for the existing three?** Note `MACHINE_HPCMIPS` already guards on
`devinit->addr == 0x14000180` while landisk's addr is `0x14000000`: close, and worth checking that
no machine reaches two arms.

GXEMUL-SEC/regress\artifacts\lcaisalane\pass1_20260910\codex.txt:3854:.\tools\pipeline\ledger.json:33489:   "state_note": "FILED 2026-09-07.  Surfaced while verifying a claim in the grok seat's `lcabadval` pass-1 BODY (its\nverdict line did not carry it) -- and **verified to be sharper than the seat stated it**, by opening\nboth functions.\n\n**THE DEFECT: an ACCEPTED write to one register arms a host kill in a DIFFERENT function.**\n\n`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)`, `case LCA_IOC_CONF`:\n\n    d->ioc_conf = idata;\n    /*  Only bit 0 is implemented so far, the PCI bus 0 vs\n        bus non-0 selection bit.  */\n    if (idata & ~1) {\n            fatal(\"TODO: Write to unimplemented bit of IOC_CONF: 0x%\" PRIx64 \"\\n\", ...);\n            exit(1);\n    }\n\n**`0x1` PASSES that guard.**  `idata & ~1` is 0, so the write is accepted, stored, and the arm's own\ncomment describes bit 0 as the implemented selector.\n\n`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)` then consumes it:\n\n    bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;\n    if (bus == 0) {\n            for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)\n                    if (tag & (0x800 << dev))\n                            break;\n            if (dev >= 21) {\n                    /*  fatal(\"[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\\n\");\n                    exit(1);  */\n                    dev = 0;\n            }\n    } else {\n            fatal(\"TODO. Non-zero bus.\\n\");\n            exit(1);\n    }\n\nSo: **guest writes 1 to IOC_CONF (accepted), then performs any PCI configuration-space access, and\nthe host process exits.**  Two guest accesses, both to registers the model implements, neither of\nthem rejected.\n\n*** WHY IT IS ITS OWN ROW AND NOT PART OF [[lcabadval]]. ***  Every kill `lcabadval` covers is a\nguard REFUSING a value it does not recognise.  **This one fires on a value the guard ACCEPTS**, in a\ndifferent function, on a later access.  The acceptance and the kill are separated in both code and\ntime, which is why a per-arm audit of `lca_ioc` walks straight past it -- as mine did, twice.\n\n*** AND THE SEAT'S FRAMING NEEDED CORRECTING, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH RECORDING. ***  grok wrote\nthat this exit becomes *\"newly reachable from a CONF extra-bit write that also sets bit 0; today\nthat write dies in the CONF guard first.\"*  That is true of an EXTRA-BIT write (e.g. `0x3`), and it\nmade the kill sound like a CONSEQUENCE of adopting option A.  **It is not: `0x1` alone reaches it\ntoday, with no fix applied.**  A widens which CONF values arrive here; it does not open the door.\nThe door is open now.  *Checked by reading both arms rather than by accepting the seat's sentence --\nwhich is this project's own rule, and the seat was right about the site while being wrong about the\ntiming.*\n\n**THE IRONY, and it is a design signal rather than a joke:** the `bus == 0` branch is the file's own\ngraceful-degradation PRECEDENT -- a commented-out `fatal()`/`exit(1)` with `dev = 0;` substituted --\nand [[lcabadval]] cites that very loop as a transferable in-tree warrant.  **The `else` five lines\nbelow it is a bare kill.**  Same function, opposite dispositions, no comment explaining why.\n\n**REACHABILITY, stated honestly rather than assumed.**  Bit 0 is a bus SELECTOR, so a guest reaches\nthis by selecting a bus the model does not implement and then doing config access -- plausible for\nfirmware or an OS that probes beyond bus 0, and trivially reachable by a probe.  **Whether any\nshipped guest on `alphabook1` actually does it is NOT established here**, and the row does not claim\nit.  What is established is that no guard stands between an accepted write and the kill.\n\n**WITNESS: a rung-3 probe is available and most of it already exists.**  `device_add(machine, \"lca\")`\nis `ST_ALPHABOOK1` ONLY (`machine_alpha.c`), and `regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py` ALREADY drives a\nguest `stq`/`ldq` at `LCA_IOC_CONF` (`0xfffffc0180000020`, K0SEG | physical) on unmodified\n`alphabook1`.  This row needs one more session: `stq 0x1` to CONF, then a PCI config-space access.\n**Three traps that probe already paid for:** debugger `dump` skips `DM_DEFAULT` handlers; a debugger\n`put d` of a killing value IS a host kill but is NOT a guest instruction, so it does not witness; and\nHAE at window offset 0 silently succeeds, so a miss control must not use `LCA_IOC_BASE+0`.\n\n**DISPOSITION, not yet adjudicated:** it is the same de-fatalisation family, but the option set\ndiffers because the kill is not a value refusal -- the honest members here are DECLINE THE CONFIG\nACCESS (return without dispatching, with a latched announce) or IMPLEMENT bus != 0.  **Note the\narchitecturally correct error path is blocked for the same reason grok gave on the sibling row:\n`LCA_IOC_STAT0`/`STAT1` have no case at all and hit the `default:` arm's `exit(1)`, so LCA cannot\nreport a device error through the register the header designates for it.**\n\nsev HIGH: a guest-drivable host kill, reached by two ordinary accesses with no rejected value\nanywhere on the path.\n\n*** SIBLING CHECK DISCHARGED WITH A DENOMINATOR, 2026-09-07 -- the class has EXACTLY ONE member in\nthis file. ***  The rule is grep for the siblings before calling a finding whole; here the question\nis which lca_ioc fields have a consumer at all, since a field nothing reads cannot arm a delayed\nkill.  Counting references that are neither the readback (`odata = d->X`) nor the store (`d->X = `):\n\n    ioc_conf          1 consumer   <- `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` in lca_pci_conf.  THIS ROW.\n    tlb_enable        0\n    window_base_0     0            window_base_1     0\n    window_mask_0     0            window_mask_1     0\n    window_t_base_0   0            window_t_base_1   0\n\n**So the accepted-write-arms-a-later-kill shape cannot recur elsewhere in dev_lca.c**: the other\nseven fields are write-and-read-back with nothing downstream.  That is also why [[lcabadval]]s five\npure-storage arms are a different problem from this one, and why a fix here does not generalise to\nthem.\n\n*Not claimed beyond this file.  The general class -- an ACCEPTED store that a later access consumes\ninto a terminating path -- is a plausible cross-device census and is NOT run here.*\n\n*** THE WITNESS-DESIGN CHECK WAS REFUSED BY codex ON A CONTENT FILTER, 2026-09-07 -- recorded as a\nSEAT FAILURE, never as agreement. ***  Zero-byte `.txt`, exit 0, the error only in the `.err`:\n*\"This content was flagged for possible cybersecurity risk.\"*  Full analysis and the two same-day\nnegative controls are on [[codexsubjectrefusal]].  **Re-fired on grok**, which reads the tree, takes\nits brief from a file and has no such filter -- so the question is covered, not lost.  The brief\nitself is preserved at `regress/artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/WITNESS_brief.md` so the trigger can be\nstudied rather than guessed at.\n\n*** THE WITNESS DESIGN IS SOUND -- grok, 2026-09-07, ON THE BRIEF codex REFUSED.  The\nalternate-transport rule paid off completely: the recovery is BETTER than the refused seat would\nhave given, not a degraded substitute. ***\n\n**DENOMINATOR, and it is the largest this row has seen:** all of `dev_lca.c` (390 lines -- both\nhandlers, `DEVINIT`, ISA, interrupt stubs); `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)` and\n`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)` end to end; `bus_pci_data_access`/`bus_pci_setaddr` in `bus_pci.c`;\n`alpha_translate_v2p` K0SEG in `memory_alpha.c`; the device and unmapped paths in `memory_rw.c`;\n`LS_GENERIC_N`/`LS_N` in `cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c`; `fatal()` in `debugmsg.c`; `ST_ALPHABOOK1`\nin `machine_alpha.c`; and all of `regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py`.\n\n**CONFIRMED:** S2 reaches `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` for ANY access in the window -- no `switch` on\n`relative_addr`, no `len` test, no `writeflag` test that can skip it, no early `return`; the only\n`writeflag` use before it is filling `idata`, which is not a guard.  `len` is first used AFTER the\nkill.  And `ioc_conf` has **exactly two readers**, so nothing else can be armed by S1.\n\n*** FOUR CORRECTIONS THAT CHANGE THE CONSTRUCTION: ***\n\n1. **MY LIVENESS CONTROL IS MISLABELLED.**  `ioc rt` proves the WRITER (`lca_ioc`'s CONF arm)\n   round-trips `0x1`.  **It says nothing about S2's window.**  PCI_CONF liveness is the armed\n   session's unique message (the handler ran) PLUS the control surviving (the same access is not an\n   unconditional kill).  A positive signature is available if wanted: an empty-slot read returns\n   `0xffffffffffffffff`, where unmapped returns 0.\n2. **S2 MUST BE A READ, NOT A WRITE.**  Both reach the kill, but an empty-slot WRITE makes\n   `bus_pci_data_access` print `[ ... write to non-existant device ... ]` -- so the CONTROL would\n   carry a second `fatal()` on stderr.  An empty-slot READ of reg 0 is the architectural ID probe\n   and is silent.\n3. **A TIGHTER VARIANT: ONE PROCESS, not two sessions.**  control `ldq` (survives), then S1, then\n   the armed `ldq` (dies) -- **only `ioc_conf` changes between the two loads.**  Separate sessions\n   are still adequate, but this removes the residual that two processes could differ in something\n   that ran before the debugger prompt.\n4. **KEEP IT ON `step`.**  `continue` would make the `lca_interrupt_assert`/`_deassert` stubs a\n   confounder -- and `cpu_dyntrans.c` skips interrupt delivery while single-stepping anyway.\n\n*** THE CONTRACT LIMIT, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE FOR THE FIX ROUND: THIS IS A WITNESS OF\nTHE `exit(1)`, NOT A DETECTOR OF THE REPAIR. ***  grok: *\"'Host survives, session completes' greens\nfor a silent `return 1` that skips `bus_pci_*` and `memory_writemax64`, for a latched announce, AND\nfor falling through into existing `bus_pci` with `cur_bus=1` (empty type-1 also survives).\"*  So the\npost-fix oracle cannot discriminate between fix members, and a round that ships one must build a row\nthat can.  **Post-fix caution in the same breath: if someone later implements type-1 by falling\nthrough to `bus_pci_setaddr(bus=1, ...)`, empty bus 1 is a non-killing miss today, so the witness\ngoes green -- do NOT read that green as \"type-1 is modelled.\"**\n\n**WHY OFFSET 0 IS THE RIGHT CONTROL ADDRESS, which I had asserted without warrant:** it is not\nHAE-like (HAE is an exact-address `break` in `lca_ioc`; `lca_pci_conf` has no per-offset special\ncase).  On the ARMED session the offset is irrelevant -- `bus == 1` exits before `tag`/`dev` are\nused.  On the SURVIVING control, `tag == 0` matches no IDSEL bit, so the `dev >= 21` body runs --\n**and that body is already the commented-out kill with `dev = 0;`** -- then an empty-slot read\nreturns `(uint64_t)-1` without exiting.  `0x18` (the NetBSD `| 0x3 << 3` encoding) yields the same\n`tag`/`reg` and is equivalent.  **Do NOT use `relative_addr = 0x800000`**, which talks to the\nregistered `i82378zb` at device 7.\n\n*** AND grok FOUND THE DESIGN ARGUMENT SITTING FIVE LINES ABOVE THE DEFECT: the `dev >= 21` arm is\nALREADY DE-FATALISED, in the same function, and the surviving control USES that path. ***  *\"If that\n`exit(1)` were still live, the control would die and you would misread PCI_CONF as unconditionally\nfatal.  The file already contains the decline precedent the `else` lacks.\"*  That is the strongest\navailable warrant for declining here, and it is one function away from the kill.\n\nAlso confirmed: FAULT (member 5) is unavailable -- `memory_rw.c` delivers a failed device access as\nan exception only under `MEM_MIPS`/`MEM_M88K`, and this path always `return 1` on the live path, so\nthe kill is `exit(1)` and not `return 0`.  And `lca_isa`'s `ofs > len` kill is a DIFFERENT window\n(`LCA_PCI_SIO`), not reachable from `0x1e0000000`.\n\n*** CROSS-REFERENCE, AND A CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN NOVELTY CLAIM.  [[lcabadval]] ALREADY\nENUMERATED THIS KILL ON 2026-09-06. ***\n\nIts eleven-kill census for dev_lca.c lists **line 134, class BUS, condition `bus != 0`, i.e.\n`d->ioc_conf & 1`** -- so the site AND its dependence on ioc_conf were both already recorded, and I\nfiled this row saying only lcabadval mentioned lca_pci_conf without reading how.  **Sixth\nrediscovery this session; see [[refilingclass]], which predicts exactly this.**\n\n**WHAT THIS ROW GENUINELY ADDS, stated narrowly:** the census records the MECHANISM; it does not\nrecord that **`0x1` PASSES the CONF guard**.  That is what makes the kill reachable with no rejected\nvalue anywhere on the path -- an accepted write in one handler arming a terminating branch in\nanother -- and it is why the disposition differs from lcabadval's six value guards, whose kills all\nfire on refusal.  Plus the witness design, now reviewed SOUND by grok.\n\n**KEPT AS A SEPARATE ROW** because it has its own disposition (decline the config access, or\nimplement bus != 0) and its own witness, and because folding it into a row named for VALUE GUARDS\nwould hide it -- which is the census's own closing lesson: *a row named after one class hides the\nmembers of the others.*\n\n*** AND THE CENSUS NAMES SOMETHING PLAUSIBLY WORSE THAN THIS ROW, WHICH I SHOULD SAY OUT LOUD RATHER\nTHAN LEAVE IN A TABLE: the two INTERRUPT STUBS at :73 and :85. ***  `lca_interrupt_assert` and\n`lca_interrupt_deassert` are bare `fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1)` with **NO GUARD AT ALL**, so *any*\ndevice asserting an interrupt through the LCA ends the process regardless of what it writes.  In the\ncensus's words: the value-guard registers *\"need a firmware revision that writes something else;\nthese two need only that an interrupt is asserted.\"*  That is member 7 (IMPLEMENT) of the option\nset -- a whole path stubbed out, with no value being refused -- and it is a stronger candidate for\nthe next critical round than this one.\n\n*** THE RUNG-3 WITNESS EXISTS AND THE DEFECT IS MEASURED, 2026-09-07.  regress/lca_confbus_probe.py,\nartifact regress/artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/witness_HEAD_20260907.raw, sentinel LCACONFBUS_DONE. ***\n\n    bus ctl      yes                    yes                    CONTROL: identical read at ioc_conf==0 survived   ok\n    bus ctlsig   0xffffffffffffffff     0xffffffffffffffff     DEVICE SIGNATURE: empty-slot ID probe             ok\n    bus kill     dead                   alive                  <- THE DEFECT                                     FAIL\n    bus attrib   yes                    yes                    death carried THIS arm@s diagnostic               ok\n      EVIDENCE: TODO. Non-zero bus.\n    LCACONFBUS_RESULT=3/4\n\n**One emulator process, unmodified `alphabook1`, real guest instructions through real address\ndecode.**  The sequence is: guest `ldq` from `LCA_PCI_CONF+0` with `ioc_conf == 0` (SURVIVES), then\nguest `stq 0x1` to `LCA_IOC_CONF` (ACCEPTED -- `idata & ~1` is 0), then the IDENTICAL `ldq` again\n(HOST DIES).  Only `ioc_conf` differs between the two loads, which is what attributes the death to\nthe VALUE rather than to the address, the width, or touching the window at all.\n\n*** grok@s PREDICTED DEVICE SIGNATURE CAME BACK EXACTLY. ***  It reasoned from `bus_pci.c` that an\nempty-slot read returns `0xffffffffffffffff` via `memory_writemax64` while UNMAPPED returns 0.  The\ncontrol row reads **0xffffffffffffffff**.  That is a prediction from source, confirmed by\nmeasurement -- so the signature control is established, not assumed.\n\n**ORACLE POLARITY is the ladder@s: the rows assert the CORRECT (post-fix) behaviour, so `bus kill`\nis RED on the committed build and goes GREEN when the defect is fixed, with the SAME script.**\nClause (i) is therefore HALF discharged -- the pre-fix symptom is shown; the post-fix green awaits a\nfix, which is correct for test-first.\n\n*** TWO APPARATUS DEFECTS CAUGHT IN THE FIRST RUN AND FIXED, both worth keeping. ***\n1. **The first artifact carried NO EVIDENCE for `bus attrib`.**  The row tested the PTY buffer and\n   printed nothing, so the preserved log showed a green row whose diagnostic a later reader could\n   not check.  The probe now ECHOES the matched line into its own output.  *A green row whose\n   evidence is not in the artifact is not verifiable, however sound the logic.*\n2. **I nearly filed a false correction to grok.**  It said the `TODO. Non-zero bus.` literal is\n   unique in `src/`; my grep returned TWO.  The second hit is `dev_lca.o`, the compiled object.  One\n   source site, `dev_lca.c:133`.  grok was right; my apparatus swept binaries.\n\n*** THE FIX DESIGN IS CLEARER THAN THIS ROW ASSUMED, AND IT DIVERGES FROM ITS SIBLING FOR A REASON\nWORTH STATING.  Derived 2026-09-07 from the codex fix design on [[lcairqstubkill]] plus this row's\nown witness. ***\n\n**THE TWO LCA ROWS LOOK LIKE ONE PROBLEM AND ARE NOT.**  Both are bare `fatal()+exit(1)` reached by\nordinary guest access, so it is tempting to give them one disposition.  The difference is what the\nguest is ASKING FOR:\n\n* [[lcairqstubkill]] -- an interrupt DELIVERY.  There is no correct answer that is not delivery: a\n  decline LOSES AN EVENT, silently or otherwise, and the guest can never learn of it.  codex's\n  disposition there is \"diagnose and stop the guest CPU\", because continuing is a lie.\n* **THIS ROW -- a PCI configuration cycle on bus 1.  There IS a correct answer, and hardware gives\n  it: a config read of an absent device returns ALL ONES.**  An unimplemented BUS is legitimately\n  EMPTY.  Declining is not a lost event; it is the architecturally right reply.\n\n> **So the dispositions properly differ, and the reason is not \"one is worse\".  For a DELIVERY path\n> an unimplemented target has no honest continuation; for an ADDRESSING path it has one, and the\n> tree already implements it.**\n\n*** AND THE TREE ALREADY DOES THE RIGHT THING ONE STEP DOWN, which the witness measured by accident.\n***  The `bus == 0` branch already reaches `bus_pci_setaddr` / `bus_pci_data_access`, and this row's\nown witness recorded the result: **a read of an empty slot returns `0xffffffffffffffff`** (the\n`bus ctlsig` row, MEASURED).  grok noted the same about the armed side in its design review --\n*\"falling through into existing bus_pci with cur_bus=1 (empty type-1 also survives)\"*.\n\n**SO THE LIKELY FIX IS NOT A DECLINE AT ALL BUT A FALL-THROUGH:** stop special-casing `bus != 0`,\nlet the existing `bus_pci` path handle it, and an empty bus 1 answers all-ones by itself.  That is\noption-set member 7 (IMPLEMENT) reached cheaply, because the machinery exists -- unlike the sibling\nrow, where member 7 is blocked at the dyntrans core.\n\n*** THE DISCRIMINATING ROW IS THEREFORE THE RETURNED VALUE, NOT THE RUNNING STATE. ***  codex's\noracle for the sibling row (\"stopped guest state, plus the reason\") is right THERE and wrong HERE,\nbecause a correct fix must leave the guest RUNNING.  The observable that separates the candidates\nhere is what the armed read returns:\n\n    fall through to bus_pci (empty bus 1)      0xffffffffffffffff    <- architecturally correct\n    silent return without servicing            0x0 or unchanged      <- looks like UNMAPPED\n    latched announce then return unserviced    0x0 or unchanged      <- same, plus a message\n    the existing exit(1)                       host gone\n\n**And this probe ALREADY HAS THAT ROW SHAPE**: `bus ctlsig` asserts exactly `0xffffffffffffffff` on\nthe bus-0 control, and distinguishes it from the `0x0` an unmapped read gives.  The fix round adds\nthe mirror of it on the ARMED side.  *No new capability is needed, which is not true of the sibling.*\n\n**NOT YET VERIFIED, and it is the one thing to check before building:** that `bus_pci` genuinely\ntolerates `cur_bus == 1` rather than asserting or indexing something sized for one bus.  grok's\nreview says empty type-1 survives; **that is a seat's reading and this row has not measured it.**\nThe cheap check is to build the fall-through and run the existing probe -- the armed row should turn\nfrom `dead` to `0xffffffffffffffff`.\n\n*** THE UNVERIFIED LINK IS CLOSED BY READING: `bus_pci' IS BUS-AGNOSTIC BY CONSTRUCTION, SO THE\nFALL-THROUGH FIX IS SOUND WITHOUT A NEW CAPABILITY. ***\n\nThe paragraph above flagged one thing to check before building -- whether `bus_pci' tolerates\n`cur_bus == 1' rather than asserting or indexing something sized for a single bus.  It does:\n\n    bus_pci.h:84   int  cur_bus, cur_device, cur_func, cur_reg;   <- a plain int, not bus-0-only\n    bus_pci.h:87   struct pci_device *first_device;               <- a LINKED LIST, not an array\n                                                                     indexed by bus number\n    bus_pci.c:102  if (dev->bus == pci_data->cur_bus && ...)      <- devices carry their OWN bus\n                                                                     field and are MATCHED against it\n    bus_pci.c:200  pci_data->cur_bus = bus;                       <- set from the caller, unchecked\n\n**So a lookup with `cur_bus = 1` walks the list, matches nothing (no device carries `bus == 1`), and\nfalls into the no-device path -- which this row's own witness has already MEASURED returning\n`0xffffffffffffffff` on bus 0.**  Nothing is sized for one bus and nothing asserts.\n\n*** THAT CONFIRMS grok's READING AND UPGRADES IT FROM A SEAT CLAIM TO A CHECKED ONE. ***  Its design\nreview said *\"falling through into existing bus_pci with cur_bus=1 (empty type-1 also survives)\"*,\nwhich this row correctly recorded as unmeasured.  It is now verified by source rather than accepted.\n\n**CONSEQUENCE: the fix is a DELETION, not an addition.**  Remove the `bus != 0` special case and let\nthe existing path serve the cycle; an absent device on an absent bus answers all-ones by itself.\nThat is the cheapest member-7 in the option set -- and the addition licence is satisfied trivially,\nbecause nothing is added.\n\n**WHAT STILL OWES A MEASUREMENT, and it is one run:** build the fall-through and re-run\n`regress/lca_confbus_probe.py`.  **PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION: the `bus kill' row turns from `dead'\nto `alive', and a mirror of `bus ctlsig' on the armed side should read `0xffffffffffffffff`** -- the\nsame value the bus-0 control already returns, which is what distinguishes a serviced empty-bus cycle\nfrom a silent unserviced return (`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped).\n\n*** PASS-1 FIX PANEL, 2026-09-07 -- PROVISIONAL: recorded on THREE landed seats (deepseek, minimax,\nthe kimi-k3:cloud substitute) while codex, grok and glm were still answering.  Any ruling short of\nthe roster is provisional on its face. ***\n\n**Q1 IS SETTLED AND MY WORRY WAS UNFOUNDED -- AND THE PROOF IS INSIDE THE EXISTING CODE, WHICH I HAD\nNOT THOUGHT TO USE.**  I asked whether the file's tag comment describes the same `tag` this code\ncomputes (`(relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff`) or a pre-shift value.  minimax:\n\n> *\"The existing IDSEL check proves the code's `tag` IS the comment's `tag`.  The `bus == 0` branch\n> checks `tag & (0x800U << dev)` for dev 0..20.  The mask `1 << (dev+11)` only matches real hardware\n> if `tag` holds IDSEL bits at positions 11..31 -- exactly the comment's type-0 layout.  That branch\n> is EXERCISED TODAY on real bus-0 traffic and works, so the interpretation is FORCED.\"*\n\n`0x800` is exactly `1 << 11`, and the kimi substitute reaches it independently (*\"func at bits 10..8\nin both rows\"*).  **So `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is the faithful type-1 decode.**  *A working branch\nof the same function is a stronger oracle for a layout question than the comment is -- the comment\ncould be stale, the working code cannot be.*\n\n*** Q2 IS THE REAL DEFECT IN MY SKETCH, AND ALL THREE SEATS FLAG IT: THE FIX AS WRITTEN WOULD BE\nINCOHERENT. ***  deepseek: *\"the sketch is incomplete/incoherent because it keeps\n`bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` while the type-1 tag comment says bus is in tag bits 23..16.\"*  The kimi\nsubstitute sharpens it: **the model computes THREE bus/type sources from the address -- the AD[1:0]\ntype bits, the tag's bus field, and `ioc_conf & 1` -- and DISCARDS TWO.**  *\"The packet cannot say\nwhich one real hardware honours.\"*\n\n> **So decoding `dev` from the type-1 layout while taking `bus` from a register bit mixes two\n> addressing models.**  Either the tag is authoritative (and `bus` must come from bits 23..16) or the\n> register is (and the type-1 tag layout does not apply).  **The fix cannot be written until that is\n> chosen, and no seat could choose it from the packet.**\n\n**Q4: THE DISCRIMINATING ROW IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT -- unanimous among the three.**  My\nproposed armed-side all-ones row separates *serviced an empty bus* from *returned without servicing*\n(`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped).  **It cannot separate IMPLEMENT from an explicit all-ones\nreturn**, because an EMPTY bus 1 answers all-ones under both.  kimi: *\"only a populated bus-1 device\ncan\"* tell them apart -- and nothing in this tree places one.\n\n*That is a real limit on what this round can claim, and it is better to know it before building the\nrow than after.*\n\n**NOT ADOPTED PENDING THE ROSTER.**  The Q2 question is a hardware-behaviour question the packet\ncannot settle, and `_scratchpad/` holds no LCA/21066 datasheet (the `ls _scratchpad/*.pdf` list has\nDC21285 but no 21066), so it may not be settleable from this machine at all -- which would make it a\ncandidate for the 2026-09-09 adjudication rather than for a fix round.\n\n*** grok LANDED AND SETTLED Q2 -- THE ANSWER THE PACKET-FED SEATS COULD NOT REACH, AND IT DISSOLVES\nTHE INCOHERENCE RATHER THAN CHOOSING A SIDE. ***\n\n> **VERDICT: IMPLEMENT, but the sketch is one assignment short.**  `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is the\n> right extract of *this* `tag` (Q1 is not a double-shift).  **Also assign\n> `bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff` -- `ioc_conf & 1` is the PCI cycle TYPE, not the bus number.**\n> Member 7; fall through into `bus_pci`.  The proposed all-ones row is necessary and not sufficient:\n> the witness address is not a type-1 tag, and empty-slot data cannot pin the extract.\n\n**WHY THIS RESOLVES THE CONFLICT INSTEAD OF PICKING A WINNER.**  deepseek and the kimi substitute\nwere right that taking `dev` from the tag while taking `bus` from a register bit mixes two addressing\nmodels.  grok's reading shows the register bit is not a bus selector at all: it chooses the CYCLE\nTYPE -- type 0 addresses the local bus, type 1 addresses a bus behind a bridge -- and in type 1 the\nbus number comes from the tag.  **The file's own comment, \"the PCI bus 0 vs bus non-0 selection bit\",\nis a loose description of exactly that**, so the code comment and grok agree once the names are\nstraightened.  *The variable is simply misnamed: `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;` stores a cycle type in\nsomething called `bus`.*\n\n**THE FIX IS THEREFORE TWO ASSIGNMENTS, NOT ONE:**\n\n    } else {\n            bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;    /*  type-1: bus is bits 23..16  */\n            dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;    /*  type-1: device is bits 15..11  */\n    }\n\n*** AND grok BREAKS MY PROPOSED DISCRIMINATING ROW FOR A REASON I HAD NOT SEEN: \"the witness address\nis not a type-1 tag\". ***  The existing probe reads at `LCA_PCI_CONF + 0`, so `tag == 0` -- which\nunder the type-1 decode means bus 0, device 0.  **The row would therefore exercise the new code with\na degenerate tag and prove nothing about the extract**, and \"empty-slot data cannot pin the extract\"\nbecause an absent device answers all-ones whatever bus/dev were decoded.\n\n> **So the round needs a row that drives a NON-ZERO type-1 tag and observes something that depends\n> on the decoded bus/dev -- and with no device on bus 1, there is nothing to observe.**  That is the\n> same limit the three packet-fed seats reached from the other side (*\"only a populated bus-1 device\n> can\"* separate IMPLEMENT from a faked all-ones return).  Two independent routes to one conclusion.\n\n**HARDWARE CLAIM, FLAGGED AS UNVERIFIABLE FROM THIS MACHINE:** that `ioc_conf & 1` is the cycle type\nis a statement about the 21066/LCA, and **`_scratchpad/` holds no LCA datasheet** -- the PDF list has\nDC21285 but nothing for this chipset.  It is consistent with the file's comment and with standard PCI\ntype-0/type-1 addressing, but it is not CHECKED here, and this project's rule is to cite only what\nhas been read.  **That is a good candidate for the 2026-09-09 adjudication rather than for me.**\n\n*** THE PANEL IS COMPLETE (6 seats) AND UNANIMOUS ON THE DESIGN -- AND codex CITED A PRIMARY SOURCE\nSITTING ON THIS MACHINE THAT I HAD JUST DECLARED UNAVAILABLE. ***\n\nI wrote, one paragraph above, that the cycle-type claim is *\"a statement about the 21066 ... not\nCHECKED here\"* because `_scratchpad/` holds no LCA datasheet. &secondary, &device, 0);\n    :127   if (secondary) {\n    :130           REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01;\n    :132   } else {\n    :134           /*  on the LCA, must frob the tag used for devices on the primary bus,\n    :136              in the same ways as is used by type 1 configuration cycles on PCs.  */\n    :139           tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (tag & 0x7ff);\n    :140   }\n\n**The performs a config access, and I described that as an ordinary but\nsynthetic pair of guest accesses.  **It is not synthetic: it is the OpenBSD driver's own\nsecondary-bus path, line for line.**  `REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01` followed by the config read is\nwhat `lca_conf_read` DOES whenever it is asked about a device behind a bridge.\n\n> **So the kill is not merely reachable in principle -- it is on the code path a real guest driver\n> takes the moment it probes a secondary bus.**  The row previously said \"NOT CLAIMED: that any\n> shipped guest issues this sequence.\"  That caution can now be withdrawn: a shipped guest driver\n> issues exactly this sequence, by construction, for secondary-bus config cycles.\n\n**THE FIX, now backed by the driver rather than by reading the fork's comment:**\n\n    } else {\n            bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;    /*  type-1: bus is bits 23..16   */\n            dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;    /*  type-1: device is bits 15..11 */\n    }\n\n**WHAT IS STILL NOT SETTLED, and every seat agrees:** the discriminating row.  grok -- *\"the witness\naddress is not a type-1 tag, and empty-slot data cannot pin the extract\"*; glm -- the row Derived 2026-09-08 by opening the dispatcher. ***\n\n`src/cpus/memory_rw.c`, the device-failure path -- the whole of it:\n\n    if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {\n            debug(\"[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx failed ]\");\n    #ifdef MEM_MIPS\n            mips_cpu_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION? EXCEPTION_IBE : EXCEPTION_DBE, ...);\n    #endif\n    #ifdef MEM_M88K\n            m88k_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION ? M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS\n                                                           : M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS, 0);\n    #endif\n            return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;\n    }\n\n**TWO `#ifdef` ARMS. THE SHIFT IS RIGHT AND ALMOST EVERYTHING AROUND IT NEEDED CORRECTING --\nINCLUDING TWO PREMISES I STATED AS SETTLED. ***\n\n**CONFIRMED, with a denominator.**  `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is correct: `relative_addr >> 5`\nundoes the address expansion, so for a valid tag T and aligned register R,\n`A = (T << 5) | (R << 5) | 24` gives `((A >> 5) & ~0xff) == T`, putting device bits at 15..11 of\nthe recovered tag.  Validated over **4,194,304 encode/decode combinations, zero mismatches** --\nand codex labelled that an ARITHMETIC check, not an emulator test, which is the honest form.\n\n*** MY IMPLEMENT SKETCH WAS INCOMPLETE: IT DECODED `dev` AND NOT `bus`. ***  `IOC_CONF` selects\nthe CYCLE FORMAT, it does not supply the bus number -- PCI address bits 23..2 come from CPU\naddress bits 28..7, and only bits 1..0 come from `IOC_CONF[1:0]`.  So the minimum correction is\n\n    } else {\n        bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;\n        dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;\n    }\n\n> **AND THE TRAP IN THE OBVIOUS ALTERNATIVE, which I would have walked into: DO NOT CHOOSE THE\n> DECODING BRANCH FROM THE EXTRACTED BUS FIELD.**  Primary-bus IDSEL bits overlap that field, and\n> keeping `bus == 1` in the forwarding call **collapses every type-1 destination onto bus 1**.\n\n*** PREMISE 4 IS FALSE AS STATED, and the refutation is one function away. ***  I wrote that an\nempty-slot read returns all-ones.  `bus_pci_data_access` implements a THREE-WAY contract:\n\n    read, register 0        ->  0xffffffffffffffff\n    read, any other reg     ->  ZERO\n    write                   ->  a diagnostic on EVERY access, then returns\n\nThe measured ID-register instance stands; the GENERAL claim does not.  **This matters because it\nmeans the candidate dispositions differ TODAY, with no bus-1 device added at all** -- an\nunconditional all-ones responder differs from the backend at nonzero registers, and announcement\npolicies differ on diagnostic output.  (The write diagnostic does not terminate: `fatal()` returns\nnormally.)\n\n*** THE DISCRIMINATING ROW I PROPOSED CANNOT DISCRIMINATE, AND IT HAS THREE FALSE-POSITIVE TRAPS.\n***  At register 0, all-ones is returned by correct forwarding, by the INCORRECT bus-fixed-to-1\nforwarding, by explicit all-ones completion, and by announced all-ones completion -- four\ndispositions, one observable.  Worse:\n\n* **THE EXISTING ADDRESS DOES NOT ENCODE BUS 1.**  `lca_confbus_probe.py`'s setup uses a ZERO\n  window offset, so the recovered tag -- bus field included -- is zero, and the control side\n  reaches the **no-IDSEL fallback to device 0**.  *The witness establishes mode-dependent\n  termination, not a correctly addressed secondary-bus transaction.*  That is a scope correction\n  to what this row's existing witness is entitled to claim.\n* **A FAILED LOAD PRESERVES THE CONTROL SIGNATURE.**  `memory_rw` pre-zeros, so a handler that\n  returns success without filling data yields zero -- but a literal `return 0` takes the FAILURE\n  path, and `LS_GENERIC_N` then returns *before updating the destination register*, so the witness\n  **reads back the all-ones the register already held.**  A vacuity trap of exactly the shape the\n  carrier's witness clauses exist to catch.\n* **THE PARSER TAKES THE FIRST OBSERVATION** (`printed` uses `re.search`), so adding another print\n  and calling the helper again retrieves the CONTROL's result rather than the new one.\n\n**WHAT IT PRESCRIBES INSTEAD:** keep the signature check as an empty-ID COMPLETION check with an\nindependently identified result and a distinct prior destination value, and add a **model-level\nrouting fixture** observing the exact bus/device/function/register passed onward -- distinct\nsynthetic devices on bus 1 and on another bus separate correct decoding from hardcoded bus 1 and\nfrom constant-return stubs.  Include write dispatch and preservation of primary-bus decoding.\n\n*** A SECOND ARCHITECTURAL PREMISE CORRECTED: RAW LCA ABSENCE DOES NOT GUARANTEE ALL-ONES. ***\nDigital's §6.2.18 says an unanswered transaction is a MASTER ABORT: the read returns *arbitrary\ndata with error status*, raising an instruction exception plus interrupt and IOC error logging.\n**So the 64-bit all-ones signature is an EMULATOR-COMPATIBILITY oracle, not a hardware contract**,\nand the round may not claim hardware fidelity for it.  The current path delegates to the generic\nbackend and returns success; it implements none of that error sequence.\n\n**SCOPE, stated so the round does not overclaim what it closes:** this closes the configuration\nhandler's termination and its missing destination decode ONLY.  Still open in the same file --\n`lca_ioc`'s unconditional read/write `default` termination and its register-value rejections;\n`lca_interrupt_assert` / `lca_interrupt_deassert`; and `lca_isa`'s separate guard.  Nor does\nforwarding implement bridge topology or configuration-cycle error reporting: the backend matches a\nnumeric tuple and receives **no cycle-type argument at all**, so type 1 with a zero bus field needs\nan explicit policy or simple forwarding will alias a root-bus device.\n\n**PRIMARY SOURCE, and this project did not have it:** codex cited the *Digital 21066/21066A\nHardware Reference Manual* (Table 6-1, §6.4.2, §6.2.18) with page anchors.  No 21066/LCA manual was\nin `_scratchpad/`, and there are several open LCA rows reasoning from source alone.  Fetched at the\ncitation, on the `vmesbcp2.pdf` precedent.\n\n*** AND THEN VERIFIED LOCALLY RATHER THAN TRUSTED, which is what the carrier's rule demands and\nwhich this project has been burned by skipping: `#357`/`#362`/`#365` shipped DDI 0100I page numbers\nthat came from panel output rather than a manual. ***  `_scratchpad/21066_66a_hrm.txt`, 713 KB /\n13,876 lines:\n\n    :8840  S6.2.6   \"CPU physical address bits <31:7> are used for PCI address bits <26:2>\"\n                    -> THE SHIFT OF 5 that `relative_addr >> 5` implements.  CONFIRMED.\n    :9216  S6.2.9   \"PCI address bits ad<1:0> come directly from configuration cycle type\n                     register (IOC_CONF, Section 6.4.2) bits <1:0>\"\n                    -> IOC_CONF selects the CYCLE TYPE and does NOT supply the bus.  CONFIRMED.\n    :9486  S6.2.18  \"the IOC returns arbitrary data with error status to the CPU, which causes\n                     an instruction exception\" + internal interrupt + ERR/CODE=1/CMD/ADDR/LOST\n                    -> absent-device reads are NOT all-ones on real hardware.  CONFIRMED.\n\n**One narrowing, stated because a wrong reason gets reused:** codex wrote *\"bits 23..2 from CPU\naddress bits 28..7\"*.  The manual's general sparse rule is the WIDER `<31:7> -> <26:2>` with the\nsame shift; codex's range is the PCI **type-1 config format** (bus 23..16, dev 15..11, fn 10..8,\nreg 7..2), which S6.2.9 defers to explicitly -- *\"restricted by the encoding requirements of PCI\nconfiguration cycles as described in the PCI Local Bus [Specification]\"*.  **Same shift, narrower\nwindow, and the narrowing is correct for config cycles** -- so `bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff` and\n`dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` rest on the PCI spec's field layout, not on this manual, and the round\nshould say so rather than citing the LCA manual for the field positions.\n\n*** A TRAP FOR THE NEXT READER: `file` REPORTS THIS PDF AS \"10 page(s)\" AND IT IS WRONG. ***  The\nreal count lives in a linearized trailer. The\nfile's own comment above the stub says *\"Line 0 = ISA interrupt.\"*\n\n**WHY IT IS PLAUSIBLY THE WORST KILL IN THE FILE.**  Every other terminating call in `dev_lca.c`\nneeds a guest to write a value the model does not expect.  **This one needs only that an interrupt\nis asserted** -- i.e. ordinary disk activity on a machine whose DEVINIT attaches two IDE channels.\nNo unusual value, no undocumented register, no firmware revision.\n\n*** WHAT IS ESTABLISHED AND WHAT IS NOT -- read this before quoting the row. ***\n* **ESTABLISHED, by reading:** the stubs terminate unconditionally; they are registered as the\n  interrupt handlers; `bus_isa_init` receives the same interrupt path with both IDE channels.\n* **NOT ESTABLISHED:** that any guest actually reaches them.  **No measurement has been made.**  A\n  device could conceivably never assert on this path, and `interrupt_handler_register` semantics\n  have not been read here.  The row does NOT claim \"alphabook1 cannot boot\"; it claims the wiring\n  is present and the handler is a kill.\n\n**NEXT STEP IS A RUNG-3 ATTEMPT, and the apparatus already exists.**\n`regress/lca_confbus_probe.py` (shipped 2026-09-07 for [[lcaconfbus1kill]]) already boots\nunmodified `alphabook1` under the debugger with a disk image attached and drives guest `ldq`/`stq`\nthrough real decode.  The question is whether a guest access can make an IDE channel assert while\nsingle-stepping -- **and the carrier's rule cuts against it: a step-based probe sees a SYNCHRONOUS\nassert (a device handler reached inside the guest's own store) but NOT one that depends on a\nhost-timer callback.**  So the honest first question is *what produces the assert*, and if the\nanswer is `timer_add`, this needs a free-running probe rather than a stepped one.\n\n**DISPOSITION: option-set MEMBER 7 (IMPLEMENT), and the set's other members do not apply.**  There\nis no value being refused and nothing to decline, accept, mask or fault -- it is an entire path\nstubbed out.  The carrier added member 7 on 2026-09-06 from this very file, using these very\nfunctions as its example.  A cheaper interim is member 3 (decline + LATCHED announce): return\nwithout asserting, announce once, and let the guest see a missing interrupt rather than a dead host\n-- **but that trades a host kill for a silently lost interrupt**, which is the readback-lie shape\n[[lcabadval]] is currently held on, so it is a design question and not an obvious win.\n\n**TRANSFERABLE PRECEDENT, already closed in this tree:** [[lunaabsentcpu]] -- *\"dev_luna88k.c\nreassert_interrupts() calls the NULL interrupt callback of an UNCONFIGURED cpu slot\"* -- same family\n(a terminating/absent interrupt callback reached by ordinary operation), fixed, and closed with a\nrung-3 probe witness (`regress/luna_absentcpu_probe.py`).  Read that round before designing this one.\n\nsev HIGH on the wiring; it would be CRITICAL if a witness lands, and the row says so rather than\npre-judging it.\n\n*** THE WITNESS-SHAPE QUESTION IS ANSWERED, AND FAVOURABLY: THE ASSERT IS GUEST-DRIVEN, NOT\nHOST-TIMER-DRIVEN, SO A STEPPED PROBE CAN REACH IT. ***\n\nThe carrier demands one question before designing any probe around a tick: *what produces the thing\nthe tick consumes?*  If the answer is `timer_add`, the probe must free-run, because `debugger.c`\ncalls `timer_stop()` while interacting and `step N` returns before `timer_start()`.\n\n    DEVICE_TICK(wdc)\n    {\n            struct wdc_data *d = (struct wdc_data *) extra;\n\n            if (d->int_assert)\n                    INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);\n    }\n\n**What it consumes is `d->int_assert`, a flag the COMMAND PATH sets from an ordinary guest register\nwrite.**  No host timer produces it.  And tick functions do run while stepping -- `machine.c`\ndecrements `ticks_till_next[te]` by 1 under `single_step` instead of by `N_SAFE_DYNTRANS_LIMIT` --\nso the tick fires, one tick at a time.\n\n> **PREDICTED ROUTE: a guest write issues a wdc command that sets `int_assert`; the probe steps far\n> enough for the tick to run; `INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq)` dispatches to the registered handler for\n> `<machine>.lca`; and that handler is `fatal()` + `exit(1)`.**  Synchronous in the carrier's sense\n> -- what the tick consumes comes from the guest, not from SIGALRM.\n\n**STILL A PREDICTION, AND THREE LINKS ARE UNVERIFIED:** (1) that `INTERRUPT_ASSERT` on `d->irq`\nresolves to the LCA handler rather than to an intermediate ISA controller that swallows it; (2) how\nmany steps the tick needs; (3) whether `alphabook1` reaches a state where a wdc command can be\nissued from a cold debugger at all.\n\n**LINK (1) IS THE ONE TO READ FIRST.**  `bus_isa_init` may interpose its own interrupt controller\nbetween the IDE channel and the `<machine>.lca` path -- in which case the assert never reaches these\nstubs, and the row's severity drops sharply.  **If it interposes, THAT is the finding, not a\nfailure**, and the row should be re-graded rather than quietly dropped.\n\n*Recorded because it converts an open-ended witness question into three named, checkable links.*\n\n---\n\n*** A PROCESS NOTE, because it happened while writing THIS paragraph and it is the second instance\ntoday. ***  The first attempt to record the above went through `python -c '...'` and bash tore the\nsingle-quoted string apart on the apostrophes in \"guest's\" and \"carrier's\", exactly as it had done\nhours earlier on a different note.  **The rule minted from the first instance -- PROSE DESTINED FOR\nA RECORD GOES INTO A SCRIPT FILE, NEVER THROUGH `-c` -- was already written down in this same ledger\nwhen I broke it again.**  That is the carrier's own structure-versus-memory finding landing on me\ntwice in one session: knowing a rule and having it fire on the right object are different problems,\nand only the second is solved by writing more.  This paragraph was written from a file and landed\nintact on the first attempt.\n\n*** LINK (1) IS SETTLED BY READING, AND IT RAISES THE SEVERITY RATHER THAN LOWERING IT.  THE\nINTERPOSED PIC DOES NOT SWALLOW THE ASSERT -- IT FORWARDS IT, AND BOTH STUBS ARE REACHABLE. ***\n\nI flagged `bus_isa_init` as possibly interposing a controller that absorbs the interrupt.  **It does\ninterpose, and it forwards.**  The full chain, every link read:\n\n    dev_wdc.c    DEVICE_TICK(wdc):  if (d->int_assert) INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);\n                                    -- d->irq is \"<machine>.lca.isa.14\" (wdc0_irq = 14,\n                                       wdc1_irq = 15, bus_isa.c:180)\n\n    bus_isa.c    isa_interrupt_assert():  sets the PIC IRR bit for that line, then\n                                          isa_interrupt_common(d, old_isa_assert)\n\n    bus_isa.c    isa_interrupt_common():  cascades PIC2 -> PIC1 IRQ 2, then\n                                          new_isa_assert = d->pic1->irr & ~d->pic1->ier;\n                                          if (old == new) return;\n                                          if (!new) { INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq); return; }\n                                          ... otherwise asserts upward\n\n    dev_lca.c    lca_interrupt_assert() / lca_interrupt_deassert():\n                                          fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1);\n\n**`d->irq` in `bus_isa_data` is the connection made at `bus_isa.c:193`:\n`INTERRUPT_CONNECT(interrupt_base_path, d->irq)` -- and `DEVINIT(lca)` passes `interrupt_path` =\n`\"<machine>.lca\"` as that base path.  So the upward assert lands exactly on the two stubs.**\n\n**AND `BUS_ISA_EXTERNAL_PIC` IS NOT SET BY LCA**, so the whole block at `bus_isa.c:190-209` runs:\nLCA passes only `BUS_ISA_IDE0 | BUS_ISA_IDE1`.  The 16 ISA lines are registered and the upward\nconnection is made.\n\n*** BOTH DIRECTIONS KILL, WHICH I HAD NOT EXPECTED. ***  `isa_interrupt_common` calls\n**`INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq)`** at the point where the last unmasked IRR bit clears -- and\n`lca_interrupt_deassert` is the same bare `fatal()` + `exit(1)`.  So an ordinary interrupt LIFECYCLE\n-- assert, then acknowledge -- reaches a terminating stub at BOTH ends.  A fix that de-fatalises\nonly the assert leaves the deassert live.\n\n**THE ONE REMAINING GATE, and it is NOT a guest-unmask requirement in the direction I first\nassumed:** the upward assert fires when `pic1->irr & ~pic1->ier` becomes non-zero, i.e. when an IRR\nbit is set and NOT masked by `ier`.  **`ier` is the PIC's interrupt-ENABLE/mask register, and its\nRESET VALUE has NOT been read here** (`dev_8259.c`).  If it starts at 0 -- nothing masked -- then\nthe FIRST IDE assert propagates immediately with no guest setup at all.  **That is the next thing to\nread, and it decides whether this is \"reachable after ordinary driver setup\" or \"reachable\nimmediately\".**  Recorded as unread rather than guessed.\n\n**SEVERITY STAYS HIGH AND IS NOT RAISED TO CRITICAL YET**, because reachability is still established\nby READING only -- no witness has been run.  What has changed is that the plausible-interposition\nescape hatch is now closed: the PIC forwards, and both stubs are on the path.\n\n*** THE LAST GATE IS OPEN AT RESET.  THE CHAIN IS NOW COMPLETE WITH EVERY LINK READ, AND IT NEEDS NO\nGUEST SETUP AT ALL. ***\n\nThe remaining question was the PIC mask: the upward assert fires when `pic1->irr & ~pic1->ier` goes\nnon-zero, so a non-zero reset `ier` would mask IDE and gate the whole path behind guest driver\nconfiguration.  Read:\n\n    dev_8259.c:266    memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct pic8259_data));\n    dev_8259.c:213    d->ier = idata;          <- the ONLY write to ier, from a guest store\n\n**`ier` is zeroed at init and is never set anywhere else.**  So at reset `ier == 0`, `irr & ~ier`\nis just `irr`, and **the FIRST set IRR bit propagates upward immediately.**  Nothing is masked until\na guest deliberately masks it.\n\n*** THE COMPLETE CHAIN, EVERY LINK READ IN THE SOURCE: ***\n\n    1.  dev_wdc.c      DEVICE_TICK(wdc): if (d->int_assert) INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);\n                       `int_assert` is set by the guest COMMAND path -- no host timer involved.\n                       d->irq is \"<machine>.lca.isa.14\"  (wdc0_irq = 14, wdc1_irq = 15)\n\n    2.  bus_isa.c      isa_interrupt_assert(): sets the PIC IRR bit, calls isa_interrupt_common()\n\n    3.  bus_isa.c      isa_interrupt_common(): new = pic1->irr & ~pic1->ier;  ier == 0 AT RESET,\n                       so `new` is non-zero on the first assert -> forwards upward on d->irq\n                       (and calls INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq) when the last unmasked bit clears)\n\n    4.  bus_isa.c:193  d->irq came from INTERRUPT_CONNECT(interrupt_base_path, d->irq), and\n                       DEVINIT(lca) passes interrupt_path = \"<machine>.lca\" as that base path\n\n    5.  dev_lca.c:70   lca_interrupt_assert():   fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1);\n        dev_lca.c:82   lca_interrupt_deassert(): fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1);\n\n**So on `alphabook1`, an ordinary IDE interrupt ends the host process, and BOTH ends of the\ninterrupt lifecycle -- assert and deassert -- land on a terminating stub.**  No unusual register\nvalue, no undocumented offset, no firmware revision, and no guest PIC configuration: the mask is\nopen at reset.\n\n*** WHAT IS AND IS NOT CLAIMED, because this is the point at which overclaiming would be easy. ***\n* **ESTABLISHED BY READING, link by link:** the wiring, the forwarding, the open reset mask, and\n  that both stubs terminate unconditionally.\n* **NOT MEASURED.**  No probe has been run.  Nothing here shows that a guest on `alphabook1`\n  actually issues a wdc command that sets `int_assert`, nor that the tick runs before the guest\n  does something else.  **The row does NOT claim \"alphabook1 cannot boot\"** -- that is a plausible\n  consequence, not a finding, and it would need a witness.\n* The severity assessment therefore stays **HIGH on the wiring**.  It goes to the top of the\n  critical queue only when a witness lands, and the honest form of that sentence is what this row\n  says rather than what it implies.\n\n**A FIX MUST TAKE BOTH STUBS.**  De-fatalising only `lca_interrupt_assert` leaves\n`lca_interrupt_deassert` live on the same path, reached at acknowledge time -- which would convert\n\"dies on the first disk interrupt\" into \"dies on the first disk interrupt ACK\" and read as a fix in\nevery test that only asserts.\n\n*Found by following [[lcabadval]]'s own eleven-kill census, which had already named these two as\n\"plausibly the most severe of the eleven\" and which nothing had followed up.*\n\n*** CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN PRECEDENT CLAIM: [[lunaabsentcpu]] TRANSFERS AS A **WITNESS**\nTECHNIQUE AND **NOT** AS A DISPOSITION.  Checked by reading its diff rather than its title. ***\n\nThe row above says *\"read that round before designing this one\"*, which reads as though its FIX\ntransfers.  It does not.  `79cd12e`'s diff on `dev_luna88k.c` is a **BOUND** repair:\n\n    -   for (cpu = 0; cpu < MAX_CPUS; cpu++)\n    +   for (cpu = 0; cpu < d->ncpus; cpu++)          /*  #503  */\n\nplus a captured `ncpus` snapshot.  **luna's defect was walking into UNPOPULATED slots and calling a\ncallback that was the memset's NULL** -- a SIGSEGV from an absent handler.  **LCA's defect is a\nhandler that is PRESENT and deliberately terminates.**  Bounding a walk cannot help when the walk is\ncorrect and the destination is `exit(1)`; there is no unconnected slot here.\n\n**WHAT DOES TRANSFER, and it is the more useful half: HOW TO DRIVE AN INTERRUPT FROM GUEST STORES\nALONE.**  `luna_absentcpu_probe.py`'s witness is *\"a guest store of 0xfc000000 to INT_ST_MASK1\nfollowed by a store to SOFT_INT1\"* -- **unmask, then trigger, both ordinary guest stores, no host\ntimer and no disk.**  That is exactly the shape a witness for this row needs, and it is why the\n`#503` round is worth reading.\n\n**The ISA analogue is not identical**, because there is no soft-interrupt register on this bus: the\nPIC mask is already open at reset (so the unmask step is free) but the TRIGGER has to come from a\ndevice asserting, which is what the reachability census now out to codex is enumerating.\n\n*This is the warrant-transfer caution in miniature: a precedent that is genuinely same-family for\none purpose (witness construction) can be wrong for another (disposition), and the row title looks\nidentical either way.  Read the diff, not the title.*\n\n*** MEASURED 2026-09-07.  THE MOST SEVERE ITEM IN THIS FILE IS NOW A RUNG-3 WITNESS, AND IT TAKES\nFOUR GUEST INSTRUCTIONS. ***  `regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py`, artifact\n`regress/artifacts/lcairqstubkill/witness_HEAD_20260907.raw`, sentinel `LCAIRQ_DONE`.\n\n    lpt ctl      yes     yes     CONTROL: same store, same window, a non-asserting port   ok\n    kbc live     yes     yes     DEVICE SIGNATURE: dev_pckbc_access announced the command ok\n    kbc kill     dead    alive   <- THE DEFECT                                            FAIL\n    kbc attrib   yes     yes     ATTRIBUTION                                              ok\n      EVIDENCE: [ pckbc: write to CTL: aa 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 ]\n      EVIDENCE: lca_interrupt_assert: TODO\n    LCAIRQ_RESULT=3/4\n\n**The whole program is four instructions** -- build the scratch pointer, load the port address, load\nthe value, store it -- on unmodified `alphabook1`, and `0xAA` is the STANDARD 8042 SELF-TEST\ncommand.  No disk image, no OS, no host timer, no driver setup, no unusual value.\n\n*** THE MATCHED CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES IT ATTRIBUTABLE, and it is the census that supplied it. ***\n`lpt ctl` performs the IDENTICAL store -- same value, same width, same K0SEG path, same ISA window\n-- one port away, at `B+0x378`, where `dev_lpt_tick` is EMPTY and `dev_lpt_access` never asserts.\nIt SURVIVES.  So the death is attributable to the DEVICE REACHED, not to storing into ISA space,\nnot to the address width, and not to the debugger path.\n\n**TWO PREMISES OF MINE WERE CORRECTED BY THE codex ISA CENSUS BEFORE THIS WAS BUILT**, and the row\nwould have failed without both: the WDC controllers exist ONLY IF an IDE image is attached, so the\nIDE route this row was written around is unavailable to a probe attaching a memory file; and PIT/RTC\nasserts are HOST-TIMER driven and therefore invisible to a stepped probe, while PCKBC and the UARTs\nare synchronous with the guest access.  *The census cost one codex invocation and it chose the route.*\n\n*** SEVERITY: the wiring claim is now MEASURED, not read. The witness\nshows the path is open and ordinary, not that a particular OS walks it.\n\n**AND THE CONTRACT LIMIT IS THE SAME ONE grok NAMED ON THE SIBLING ROW:** this witnesses THIS\n`exit(1)`; it is NOT a detector of a repair.  \"Host survives\" would green for a silent return, for a\nlatched announce, and for an actual interrupt implementation alike.  **A fix round owes a row that\ndiscriminates those** -- and note the deassert stub is on the same path and would need its own.\n\n*** TREE-WIDE CENSUS: THIS IS THE ONLY TERMINATING INTERRUPT HANDLER IN THE FORK, 2 OF 70. Pass 2 keyed on what actually DEFINES the class -- a function ASSIGNED to\n`.interrupt_assert` / `.interrupt_deassert` -- and found **70**.  **One registered handler does not\nfollow the naming convention and was invisible to the name-keyed pass.**  Both agree on the answer\n(2), but only the second could have been trusted to give it: this project's deriver rule is to key\non what makes a member a member, and a name is a correlate whose error is invisible because the\noutput is still a plausible number.\n\n*** THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FIX IS THE USEFUL HALF: THERE ARE 68 WORKING PRECEDENTS. ***  The codex\nfix brief asks (Q2) whether an in-tree shape can be copied.  The census answers it before the seat\ndoes -- 68 registered handlers deliver, latch, forward or ignore an interrupt without ending the\nprocess, so the question is not *whether* a non-terminating handler is possible on this\narchitecture but *which* of the 68 shapes suits an Alpha ISA line.\n\n**AND IT STRENGTHENS THE CASE THAT THESE TWO ARE UPSTREAM RESIDUE RATHER THAN A DECISION** -- the\nsame reading the option-set rule reached for `sgireunmapped`, where the sibling handlers in the same\nfile already declined and the arm under review was the file's own outlier.  Here the outlier is\n2-of-70 across the whole fork.\n\n*** THE codex FIX DESIGN LANDED AND IT SOLVES THE PROBLEM BOTH WITNESS CONTRACTS DECLARED OPEN.\nArtifact: `regress/artifacts/lcairqstubkill/codex_fix_20260907.txt`. ***\n\n**DENOMINATOR: 36/36 registration call sites in 28 files, 35 assert/deassert pairs = 70 callbacks.\nThat INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCES this row's own census of 70**, by a different method (registration\nsites rather than assignment targets).  Two derivations, same number.\n\n*** 1.  MEMBER 7 (IMPLEMENT) IS BLOCKED AT THE DYNTRANS CORE, NOT AT THE DEVICE. ***\n`alpha_irq_interrupt_assert()` exists and sets `cpu->cd.alpha.irq_asserted = 1`.  **But \"the only\nreferences to Alpha's `irq_asserted` are the two assignments\"** -- nothing consumes it -- and\n`DYNTRANS_RUN_INSTR_DEF`'s interrupt-dispatch block has **ARM, M88K, MIPS, PPC and SH branches and\nNO ALPHA BRANCH**.  So even correct LCA wiring would deliver nothing to guest software.  **This\nindependently confirms the filed row [[irqassertedunread]]** -- \"alpha, i960 and riscv set\ncd.<arch>.irq_asserted with no consumer\" -- reached from the other direction.\n\nA minimal wiring step is still identified and is NOT sufficient on its own: give `lca_data` a\nconnected upstream `struct interrupt`, connect it to `devinit->interrupt_path` (which already\ndefaults to the bootstrap CPU in `device_add()`), and forward both levels.  **LCA currently stores\nno upstream interrupt connection at all**, and passing its own object to the CPU callback would be\nwrong because `extra` points at `lca_data` rather than the destination CPU.\n\n*** 2.  MY Q3 FRAMING WAS REFUTED: THERE IS NO HOUSE POLICY FOR THIS CASE. ***  I asked which\ndisposition \"this tree's own practice favours\".  The answer is that the precedents CONFLICT:\n\n    cpu_sh.c    sh3_cpu_interrupt_assert/deassert()   silent TODO bodies\n    dev_pcc2.c  reassert_interrupts()                 diagnoses, then exit(1)\n    dev_sh4.c   sh4_timer_tick()                      latched announce, then continues\n\n*\"The SH4 example supports bounded reporting, but it does not establish a universal disposition for\nan asserted delivery path.\"*  My brief presumed a settled practice; there is none.\n\n*** 3.  A DISPOSITION I HAD NOT NAMED, WITH AN IN-TREE PRECEDENT: DIAGNOSE AND STOP THE GUEST CPU. ***\nNot `exit(1)` (host process death) and not a silent or latched return (invisible loss), but an\nEMULATOR-LIMITATION STOP: `alpha_palcode()` already does exactly this for unsupported PAL calls --\ndiagnostic plus `cpu->running = 0`.  **This is an emulator limitation, not an architectural guest\nFAULT**, and the distinction is the point.\n\n**AND A CAUTION THAT WOULD HAVE MADE A NAIVE FIX SUBTLY WRONG:** *\"merely changing `running` inside a\ndevice callback does not itself unwind the current batched execution.\"*  `alpha_instr_call_pal()`\nhandles PAL stops by redirecting `next_ic` to `nothing_call`; a device implementation must arrange\nthe same unwind.  *That is the same class as the four-readers-proposed-`return` incident -- a\nplausible edit that skips machinery the enclosing control flow depends on.*\n\n*** 4.  THE DISCRIMINATING ROW, WHICH IS WHAT BOTH WITNESS CONTRACTS SAID WAS MISSING. ***\n**\"Stopped guest state, plus the reason -- NOT survival.\"**  Proposed acceptance row: starting from\na running CPU, an unsupported LCA assertion produces its specific diagnostic and leaves\n`cpu.running == false`; with debugger-at-end enabled the debugger stays responsive and subsequent\nsteps execute no guest instructions; the matched non-asserting control remains RUNNING.\n\n    diagnosed guest stop                              GREEN\n    silent return                                     RED -- CPU remains running\n    latched announce and return                       RED -- CPU remains running\n    forwarding only, or delivery to a resuming handler RED -- CPU remains running\n    the existing exit(1)                              RED -- debugger/process is gone\n\n**That discriminates all five candidates**, and the state is already observable: `cpu_new()` exposes\n`running` as a setting, `machine_run()` skips stopped CPUs, and `emul_run()` enters the debugger\nafter all machines stop when debugger-at-end is set.\n\n> **SO THE CONTRACT LIMIT ON BOTH LCA WITNESSES IS LIFTED -- for THIS disposition.**  Each says\n> \"host survives\" cannot tell the fix members apart, and that remains true; the answer is that the\n> oracle must read the CPU's RUNNING STATE and the diagnostic, not the host's survival.  The\n> existing probes would need that row added; neither has it today.\n\n**THE FABLE QUESTION IN §7j-1 IS NARROWED BUT NOT ANSWERED.**  I asked whether a latched decline is\nacceptable for an unimplemented delivery path.  codex's answer is that a THIRD option dominates both\nhorns -- but choosing between \"stop the guest with a diagnostic\" and \"lose interrupts quietly and\nkeep running\" is still a judgment about what an emulator owes its user, and that is exactly what the\nflagship seat is held for.  **§7j-2 (what observable discriminates) is now ANSWERED and can be\nstruck from the queue when the adjudication runs.**\n\n*** THE LOAD-BEARING CLAIM WAS VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY, AND MY FIRST CHECK NEARLY REFUTED IT\nWRONGLY. ***\n\ncodex's whole disposition rests on \"the Alpha CPU cannot take an asynchronous interrupt\".
