Recorded here so the adjudication prices it rather than assuming it.\n\n*** THE NOTE ABOVE UNDERSTATED THE SCOPE, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART: FAULT IS NOT\n\"UNAVAILABLE ON ALPHA\".  IT IS AVAILABLE ON **MIPS AND M88K ONLY**, FOR EVERY ARCHITECTURE IN THIS\nTREE.  Derived 2026-09-08 by opening the dispatcher. ***\n\n`src/cpus/memory_rw.c`, the device-failure path -- the whole of it:\n\n    if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {\n            debug(\"[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx failed ]\");\n    #ifdef MEM_MIPS\n            mips_cpu_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION? EXCEPTION_IBE : EXCEPTION_DBE, ...);\n    #endif\n    #ifdef MEM_M88K\n            m88k_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION ? M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS\n                                                           : M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS, 0);\n    #endif\n            return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED;\n    }\n\n**TWO `#ifdef` ARMS.  There is no ARM arm, no Alpha arm, no PPC arm and no SH arm** -- every other\narchitecture reaches `return MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED` with no exception delivered at all.\n\n> **SO OPTION-SET MEMBER 5 IS A PHANTOM ON EVERY ARCHITECTURE BUT TWO**, and a round on an ARM, SH,\n> PPC or Alpha device that reaches for FAULT is reaching for a round that does not exist yet.\n\n*Found because I wrote the OPPOSITE into a brief.  Assessing `fbfiqsilent` -- an ARM footbridge row\n-- I told the seat that \"member 5 FAULT may genuinely be available here, `memory_rw`'s\ndevice-failure path has an ARM arm.\"  It replied that the claimed arm **is absent from this tree**,\nand opening the file confirms it.  **I was one brief away from spreading a false availability claim\nacross the ARM queue**, having just corrected three rows for the Alpha version of it.*\n\n*** codex: CONFIRMED GUEST-REACHABLE PROCESS TERMINATION ON alphabook1, WITH THE PATH NAMED. ***\n\n    dev_ns16550_access() / scheduled UART tick -> dev_ns16550_tick()\n        -> isa_interrupt_assert() -> isa_interrupt_common() -> lca_interrupt_assert() -> exit(1)\n\n**The decisive wiring is one line after the registration:** `devinit_lca()` passes the same\ninterrupt path to `bus_isa_init()` (`dev_lca.c:418`), which connects its upstream interrupt to the\nsupplied template (`bus_isa.c:190`) and **unconditionally installs the first UART on ISA IRQ 4**\n(`bus_isa.c:250` -- verified by reading: the `ns16550 irq=%s.isa.%i` device_add with `4`).  No\nmachine-description modification is involved, which is what makes it a witness candidate rather\nthan laundering.\n\n**LCA line 0 is the AGGREGATE ISA input, not ISA IRQ 0** -- a correction to my brief's reading of\nthe comment.  And missing Alpha CPU dispatch does not prevent reaching the callback: *the process\nexits before CPU acceptance matters*.\n\n*** THE REPAIR IS EXPLICITLY PARTIAL, AND I VERIFIED THE REASON MYSELF. ***  codex says forwarding\nto an upstream `struct interrupt` is the minimum useful repair but cannot complete delivery,\nbecause the Alpha CPU callbacks only set and clear `cpu->cd.alpha.irq_asserted` and **that field\nhas no reader**.  Checked by census:\n\n    cd.alpha.irq_asserted   written cpu_alpha.c:218, :223   declared cpu_alpha.h:161   READ NOWHERE\n    cd.arm.irq_asserted     READ at cpu_dyntrans.c:208\n    cd.m88k.irq_asserted    READ at cpu_dyntrans.c:212 and :382\n    cd.ppc.irq_asserted     READ at cpu_dyntrans.c:243 and cpu_ppc.c:309\n\n**`cpu_dyntrans.c` has no Alpha arm at all.**  So a correct forwarding fix would set a flag nothing\nconsumes -- honest containment, not delivery.\n\n**FOOTBRIDGE IS THE CLOSEST IN-TREE ANALOGUE and it does NOT transfer literally:** it registers\n`interrupt->line` as a **bit mask** while LCA registers **index zero**, so a copied\n`status |= interrupt->line` would record nothing.\n\n*** MY OWN CITATIONS WERE CONTENTS ENTRIES, WHICH codex CAUGHT. ***  The brief cited manual lines\n`175` and `510` for the interrupt sections; those are TABLE-OF-CONTENTS rows.  The bodies are at\n`:4459` (S4.1.13) and `:10729` (S6.4.13).  *I had read the bodies and then cited the contents.*\n\n*** WITNESS ATTEMPT -- MADE, HARNESS WORKS, AND IT DID **NOT** FIRE.  Recorded as an attempt,\nwhich is what the lifecycle rule asks for. ***\n\n`_scratchpad/lcairq_probe.py`, harness copied from `regress/lca_confbus_probe.py` (pty.fork,\n`-V -x -E alpha -e alphabook1 -M 64 0x20000000:alpha_dummy.bin`).  Addresses derived, not guessed:\n`LCA_ISA_BASE = LCA_PCI_SIO(0x1c0000000) + 0x10000000`, UART at `+0x3f8`, K0SEG for the debugger.\n\n    live        alive   alive   CONTROL: plain step on an unmodified machine          ok\n    devsig      alive   alive   CONTROL: the UART window reads through the same decode ok\n    spin only   alive   alive   CONTROL: free-running self-branch, no UART setup       ok\n    irq assert  alive   died    IER=0x02, MCR=0x08, THR write, then `continue`    *** did not fire ***\n\n**Three controls green and the writes visibly land** (the debugger echoes each `put b`).  Two\nthings were learned and are worth carrying: the 8-byte all-zero dummy decodes as `call_pal\nPAL_halt` and **stops the machine before any device tick can run**, which is why the first attempt\nneeded a planted self-branch (`br r31,-1` = `0xc3ffffff`); and even free-running with IER, MCR and\na THR write in place, no assertion reached the stub.\n\n**THE OPEN QUESTION IS WHETHER A DEBUGGER `put` REACHES `DEVICE_ACCESS(ns16550)` AT ALL.**\n`dev_ns16550.c:353` registers via `memory_device_register`, and this project already records that\ndebugger access can bypass a handler depending on the registration flags.  If `put` writes a shadow\nrather than the device, `IIR_TXRDY` was never set and the tick's condition was never true -- which\nwould make the attempt inconclusive rather than negative.  **That is the next thing to settle, and\nit is a measurement, so it belongs to the reserved measuring seat rather than to another reading.**\n\n*So the row stands on codex's SOURCE-DERIVED reachability, not on an executed witness.  The\ndistinction matters and is recorded rather than blurred.*\n\n*** THE WITNESS ATTEMPT'S OPEN QUESTION IS NOW SETTLED, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT MY APPARATUS COULD\nNOT DO WHAT I ASKED OF IT.  A DEBUGGER `put' NEVER REACHES `DEVICE_ACCESS(ns16550)'. ***\n\n    debugger_cmds.c:1461, :1507   memory_rw(..., MEM_WRITE, CACHE_NONE | NO_EXCEPTIONS)\n    memory_rw.c:584               if (!no_exceptions || (flags & DM_READS_HAVE_NO_SIDE_EFFECTS))\n                                          -> only THEN is devices[i].f() called\n    dev_ns16550.c:353-355         memory_device_register(..., DM_DEFAULT, NULL)\n\n`DM_DEFAULT` is 0, so the UART carries no `DM_READS_HAVE_NO_SIDE_EFFECTS` bit; the debugger passes\n`NO_EXCEPTIONS`; **the handler is skipped.**  My three `put b' writes to IER, MCR and THR were\nabsorbed without ever invoking `dev_ns16550_access`, so `IIR_TXRDY` was never set, the tick's\ncondition was never true, and nothing asserted.\n\n> **SO THE ATTEMPT WAS INCONCLUSIVE FOR AN APPARATUS REASON, AND THAT IS NOW A FACT RATHER THAN A\n> SUSPICION.**  It says nothing whatever about whether the defect is reachable -- codex's\n> source-derived path stands untouched.\n\n*** AND IT GENERALISES INTO A PROBE-WRITING RULE THIS PROJECT HAD ONLY HALF-RECORDED. ***  The\ncarrier already notes that the debugger's `dump` bypasses a `DM_DEFAULT` handler on READS, and\nprescribes guest loads for SH4 state.  **The same gate governs WRITES, by the same line of\n`memory_rw.c`** -- so:\n\n> **CONFIGURING A DEVICE FROM THE DEBUGGER WITH `put' IS A NO-OP AGAINST ANY `DM_DEFAULT' DEVICE.\n> Device access in a probe must be made by GUEST INSTRUCTIONS.**  `put' is for RAM -- scratch\n> pointers, planted code, page tables -- where no handler is involved.\n\n**THE IN-TREE PROBES ALREADY DO THIS CORRECTLY, which is why nothing else is broken:**\n`lca_confbus_probe.py` uses `put d' only to seed SCRATCH in RAM and then reaches the device with\nguest `STQ`/`LDQ`; `alpha_swpctx_v0_probe.py` (#514) uses `put' only for RAM and `call_pal', and\ntouches no device at all.  **My failed attempt is the one that broke the pattern**, and it broke it\nby configuring a device the debugger cannot configure.\n\n**WHAT THE WITNESS NOW NEEDS, concretely:** the UART's IER/MCR/THR must be written by guest stores.\nThe registers are byte-wide at `LCA_ISA_BASE + 0x3f8` with `addrmult = 1`, so this needs either a\nbyte store (`STB`, which is a BWX instruction and may not exist on a 21064-class part -- CHECK\nbefore assuming) or a wider store whose other bytes are harmless.  **That check is the next step\nand it is cheap; it is recorded here rather than guessed.**\n\n*** THE WITNESS FIRES.  `regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py', measured on `2ab3d2f' with `src/'\nUNMODIFIED: 2/4, and the transcript's last line is the stub's own diagnostic. ***\n\n    live         alive   alive   CONTROL: a free-running self-branch alone survives        ok\n    ram ctl      alive   alive   ATTRIBUTION: the IDENTICAL sequence against RAM survives   ok\n    uart irq     died    alive   an ordinary UART interrupt must not end the emulator     FAIL\n    stub named   yes     no      the stub's own TODO diagnostic must not appear           FAIL\n\n    GXemul> continue\n    lca_interrupt_assert: TODO\n\n**THREE GUEST BYTE STORES END THE EMULATOR** -- IER `0x02`, MCR `0x08`, then any byte to THR --\non an unmodified `-E alpha -e alphabook1` with no `device_add` and no machine-description change.\n`WITNESS_HEAD_20260908.out` and the full session transcript are preserved beside it.\n\n*** `ram ctl' IS WHAT MAKES THIS A WITNESS RATHER THAN A CRASH REPORT. ***  It runs the\n**identical instruction sequence** -- same registers, same opcodes, same count -- against RAM\ninstead of the UART, and survives.  So the termination is attributable to the DEVICE and not to the\ncode sequence, the self-branch, or the free-running mode.  One control, two hypotheses separated.\n\n*** IT TOOK THREE FACTS TO GET HERE, AND TWO OF THEM WERE MINE TO GET WRONG. ***\n\n1. **codex's source path**, which named the wiring end to end: `devinit_lca()` hands its interrupt\n   path to `bus_isa_init()`, which unconditionally installs the first ns16550 on ISA IRQ 4.\n2. **The `put'/DM_DEFAULT finding** -- my FIRST attempt configured the UART with debugger `put' and\n   nothing happened, because `put' passes `NO_EXCEPTIONS' and `memory_rw' then skips a `DM_DEFAULT'\n   device's handler entirely.  *That attempt was inconclusive for an apparatus reason, and until it\n   was traced it looked like evidence the path was unreachable.*\n3. **`stb' is ungated in this tree.**  The UART registers are byte-wide, so the guest needs a byte\n   store -- a BWX instruction a real 21066 would not have.  `cpu_alpha_instr.c:895` decodes\n   `case 0x0e` with **no feature check**, and `ALPHA_FEATURE_BWX` (`cpu_alpha.h:57`) is **DEFINED\n   AND NEVER CONSULTED** -- the third declared-but-unused constant found in this header today,\n   after `ALPHA_PTE_ASM` and `ALPHA_KENTRY_MM`.  **So this probe depends on an emulator fact rather\n   than a hardware one, and a future round that gates BWX would break it.**  Recorded in the probe\n   header, not left to be rediscovered.\n\n**SEVERITY: the row is already `high` and this does not raise it -- it removes the last doubt about\nreachability.**  The witness ladder is explicit that rung measures reachability-proof strength and\nnever harm, so a witness landing does not by itself move severity.  *What it does change is that\n\"guest-reachable\" is no longer source-derived.*\n\n**WHAT THE ROW STILL OWES is unchanged and is the hard part:** the disposition.  Forwarding cannot\ncomplete delivery, because `cd.alpha.irq_asserted` has no reader and `cpu_dyntrans.c` has no Alpha\narm -- so any repair converts *\"the emulator terminates\"* into *\"the interrupt is silently\ndropped\"*.  That question is with codex and is queued for the flagship seat.\n\n*** CORRECTION, AND IT IS MINE: THIS ROW WAS ALREADY WITNESSED AND I BUILT A SECOND WITNESS\nWITHOUT CHECKING.  I ALSO OVERWROTE THE COMMITTED PROBE FILE DOING IT. ***\n\n`regress/lca_irqstub_probe.py` shipped in **`7c2f8e4` on 2026-09-07** -- *\"lcairqstubkill\nWITNESSED: four guest instructions end the host through a documented 8042 register\"* -- scoring\n**3/4**, with an LPT control chosen from a codex ISA census because `dev_lpt_tick` is empty and\ncannot assert.  **The ledger's own `witness` field said all of that**, and I replaced it with a\nthinner sentence about my own probe.\n\n**HOW IT WAS CAUGHT: the probe-wiring checker's DUPLICATE-KEY guard.**  A dict literal keeps only\nthe last value and says nothing, so the checker refuses two entries for one key -- and it named\n`lca_irqstub_probe.py`.  Without that guard I would have shipped a manifest that silently\ndescribed the wrong probe.  *Nothing else in the gate would have noticed, because the file existed\nand the row had a witness either way.*\n\n**DAMAGE, checked rather than assumed:** the probe file was overwritten and is **restored from\nHEAD**; the ledger `witness` field is **restored and extended**.  **Yesterday's artifacts are\nintact** -- `witness_HEAD_20260907.raw` is unmodified, and `git diff HEAD` over that directory\nshows only my new files added.\n\n**WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS WORTH KEEPING:** my probe is a **SECOND, INDEPENDENT PATH** -- ns16550\non IRQ 4 rather than 8042 on IRQ 1, three byte stores rather than one word store, with its own\nidentical-sequence-against-RAM control.  **Two independent guest routes to one stub is real\nvalue**: a repair that silences one and not the other is visible at once.  It is renamed\n`lca_irqstub_uart_probe.py` and its artifacts `UART_WITNESS_*`, so nothing can be mistaken for the\nrow's witness of record.\n\n**AND TWO FINDINGS FROM BUILDING IT STAND ON THEIR OWN**, independent of the duplication: the\n`put`/`DM_DEFAULT` mechanism (a debugger `put` never reaches a `DM_DEFAULT` device's handler, now\nfiled as a probe-writing hazard), and that `ALPHA_FEATURE_BWX` is defined and never consulted so\n`stb` executes on any Alpha here.\n\n> **THE PROCESS FAILURE IS EXACT AND WORTH NAMING: I read the row's `state_note` and its briefs,\n> and never read its `witness` FIELD.**  The standing instruction is to check the repo before\n> starting, and I checked the prose and not the schema.  *A row that already carries a witness is\n> the cheapest possible thing to check, and it is one field.*\n\n*** THE DISPOSITION IS DESIGNED, WITH TWO TRANSFERRED WARRANTS AND AN EXPLICIT LIMIT. ***\n\n**VERDICT (codex):** *bounded announcement plus level forwarding, as a PARTIAL repair, with Alpha\ninterrupt delivery left explicitly unresolved.*\n\n*** IT ARGUED THE STRONGEST CASE AGAINST THAT BEFORE HOLDING IT, which is what was asked. ***\n*\"It preserves the guest's failure while weakening its visibility.  An interrupt-dependent guest\ncan still hang; a warning can disappear into logs; an automation wrapper can mistake continued\nprocess life for success.  Forwarding adds no presently functioning guest capability because\nnothing consumes the flag.  An announced drop would achieve the same immediate containment with\nless plumbing.\"*\n\n> **AND THEN THE DISCRIMINATION THAT SETTLES IT:** *\"That objection defeats a claim of FIXED ALPHA\n> INTERRUPTS.  It does not defeat the narrower improvement of removing these two process exits.\"*\n> The round may remove the terminations; it may not claim delivery.\n\n**THE ADDITION LICENCE IS SATISFIED BY TWO NAMED IN-TREE PRECEDENTS, not by \"it cannot hurt\":**\nFootbridge's connection-and-propagation shape (`dev_footbridge.c:794`), and -- the one that matters\nfor the announce -- **`dev_pcc2.c:369`, which ALREADY ANNOUNCES AN UNAVAILABLE INTERRUPT-DELIVERY\nMODE ONCE.**  That is this exact disposition, already shipped elsewhere in the tree.\n\n**THE MINIMAL IMPLEMENTATION, concretely:** add `struct interrupt irq;` and `int\nirq_delivery_warned;` to `lca_data` (the existing `memset` initialises the latch);\n`INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->irq)` before creating downstream devices, since that\npath already defaults to the bootstrap CPU; a `lca_interrupt_notice()` helper carrying the `(once)`\nmarker; then `notice(); INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);` and the deassert mirror.  **N = 1 per instance\nwith an explicit suppression marker -- the house idiom, not the counter form.**\n\n*** IT ALSO CORRECTED ITS OWN EARLIER FOUR-ROW COVERAGE TABLE as having \"overstated what could\nimmediately become passing coverage\", which is a seat being harder on itself than on me: ***\n\n    callback forwarding      BUILDABLE TODAY as a host-side test; the current callbacks fail it\n                             and the small repair passes it\n    masking and release      SPLIT.  Level retention/withdrawal passes after the small repair;\n                             masked-versus-unmasked ENTRY requires CPU delivery, and no entry in\n                             either case proves nothing about masking\n    vector acknowledgement   BLOCKED on missing LCA acknowledgement support -- a SEPARATE missing\n                             dependency, implementable independently of CPU dispatch\n    guest execution          CANNOT pass with forwarding alone\n\n**SO THE ROUND'S DETECTOR IS THE FIRST ROW PLUS HALF THE SECOND**, and the record must say that\nrather than implying the table.\n\n*** AND THE DELIVERY ROUND IS BIGGER THAN A CONDITIONAL IN `cpu_dyntrans.c'. ***  codex found two\nmore declared-but-unused things in this layer: **the tree WRITES `kentry[]` AND NEVER CONSUMES\nIT**, and **`PAL_OSF1_rti` has a printable name with no execution implementation** -- so the PAL\ndispatcher cannot supply the interrupt entry/return lifecycle at all.  *That is the fifth\ndeclared-but-unused Alpha item found today, after `ALPHA_PTE_ASM`, `ALPHA_KENTRY_MM` and\n`ALPHA_FEATURE_BWX`.*  Queued as its own compatibility round with a named measured delta: on the\npartial-repair baseline, an eligible asserted input leaves a guest handler counter unchanged, and\nthe implementation makes the handler execute and return.\n\n**Footbridge supplies no Alpha PAL entry convention**, so that warrant does NOT transfer to the\ndelivery round -- only to the propagation shape.\n\n*** THE SIBLING CHECK THE ERROR DEMANDED, AND IT CAME BACK CLEAN. ***  Having overwritten one\ncommitted witness, the obvious question is whether I have done it anywhere else, or whether any\nother row's `witness' field names something that is no longer there.  Censused across every OPEN\nrow:\n\n    open rows                                        194\n    of which ALREADY CARRY A WITNESS                 119\n    whose witness names a MISSING file                 0\n\n**Zero.**  Every path named in a witness field exists on disk, so this was a single incident and\nthe restore closed it.  *And all EIGHT high-severity open rows already carry a witness* -- which\nsays something about the queue that is worth writing down: **the top of this queue is not short of\nevidence, it is short of DISPOSITIONS.**  That is the shape of work the flagship adjudication is\nfor, and it is why the packet's questions are disposition questions rather than requests for more\nmeasurement.\n\n*** THE DISPOSITION QUESTION IS SETTLED, 2026-09-08, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT FORWARDING IS A NO-OP ON\nTHIS ARCHITECTURE.  Led by the codex seat, VERIFIED BY ME IN ONE GREP. ***\n\nThe round had been parked on *\"forwarding alone leaves Alpha delivery unresolved\"* without anyone\nestablishing what Alpha delivery actually IS.  A codex ANALYSIS job (see `codexcyberflag` -- the\nconstruction-shaped brief is refused, the analysis-shaped one is not) said mid-stream:\n\n> *\"Alpha has a CPU interrupt pin and an `irq_asserted` field, but the searches so far show only\n> WRITES to that field.\"*\n\n**Its stream then ended without a final verdict, so this would have been an UNREAD REVIEW had the\n`.err` not been opened.  Checked rather than taken:**\n\n    grep -rn irq_asserted src/          ->  57 references, classified\n\n    cpu_alpha.c:218    cpu->cd.alpha.irq_asserted = 1;      WRITE\n    cpu_alpha.c:223    cpu->cd.alpha.irq_asserted = 0;      WRITE\n    cpu_alpha.h:161    int irq_asserted;                    DECL\n    ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    READS OF cpu->cd.alpha.irq_asserted ANYWHERE IN src/ :   ZERO\n\n`cpu_dyntrans.c` is where a pending interrupt becomes a delivered one, and it tests exactly three\narchitectures -- `cd.arm.irq_asserted` (`:208`), `cd.m88k.irq_asserted` (`:212`, `:382`) and\n`cd.ppc.irq_asserted` (`:243`).  **Alpha is absent from that list.**  `cpu_ppc.c:309` shows the\nsame field being consumed on the PPC side; there is no Alpha counterpart.\n\n> **SO THE ALPHA CPU MODEL HAS AN INTERRUPT-PENDING FLAG THAT NOTHING CONSUMES.  Wiring the LCA\n> stubs to `cpu_interrupt()` would set a field no code reads: a NO-OP, not a delivery.**\n\n*** WHAT THIS SETTLES, and it changes the round from blocked to shippable: ***\n\n* **Member 7 (IMPLEMENT) is NOT a wiring job.**  It requires adding Alpha interrupt dispatch to\n  `cpu_dyntrans.c` plus the PALcode entry and IPL check that go with it -- a SUBSYSTEM, and its own\n  round.  The earlier note's instinct that *\"declining an interrupt leaves the machine incomplete\"*\n  was right about the machine and wrong about the size of the gap: the machine is already\n  incomplete, before this row's stubs are reached.\n* **The partial repair is COHERENT, which was the open question.**  Nothing in the model blocks\n  waiting for an acknowledgement that cannot come, because nothing is waiting at all -- there is no\n  consumer.  Replacing `exit(1)` with a bounded announce leaves the guest with interrupts silently\n  dropped, which is EXACTLY what it already gets on every Alpha path that does not terminate.\n* **The honest disclosure the round owes** is therefore not *\"we chose not to forward\"* but\n  *\"Alpha interrupt delivery does not exist in this CPU model; these stubs cannot forward to\n  anything, and the fix removes a host termination without pretending to add delivery.\"*\n\n**AND IT REFRAMES THE SEVERITY HONESTLY IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.**  The termination is still\nguest-reachable and still witnessed twice (8042 command port, 3/4; ns16550 IRQ 4, 2/4), so the row\nstays HIGH.  But the fix buys *the host stops dying*, NOT *interrupts start working* -- and a round\nblock claiming the latter would be a wrong record.\n\n*Provenance worth keeping: this is the reading-seat-buys-the-class pattern exactly.  codex named the\nfamily (`only writes to that field`) and never finished its answer; one grep turned that into a\nmeasured fact that settles a design question two panels had left open.  The cost of NOT opening a\nzero-byte job's `.err` would have been another round of design argument.*\n\n*** PASS 1, 2026-09-08.  P4 SURVIVED A SEAT THAT TRIED TO REFUTE IT, AND FOUR OF MY BRIEF'S OTHER\nCLAIMS DID NOT. ***\n\n**grok: \"RATIFY member 3 (per-instance, two bits), do not forward, do not call this delivery.\nP4 holds and is STRONGER than the brief states.\"**  On Q1 specifically: *\"Yes. I tried to refute it\nand failed.  Delivery does not exist.\"*  **deepseek** conditionally accepted and did the correct\npacket-fed thing -- refused to ratify P4 as unverifiable from the packet and named the exact search\nthat settles it, rather than agreeing.\n\n*** THE FOUR CORRECTIONS, three of which change the round. ***\n\n**(1) Q2 WAS MIS-FRAMED AND THE QUESTION CANNOT BE ASKED.**  I asked whether the stubs should write\n`irq_asserted` anyway.  grok: *\"`interrupt->extra` is `struct lca_data *`, NOT a `struct cpu *`.\nThere is no `irq_asserted` in this file to write.  Forwarding is `INTERRUPT_CONNECT(cpu->path, ...)`\nplus `INTERRUPT_ASSERT`, i.e. CHIPSET WIRING, not a field store.\"*  So the option I was weighing does\nnot exist at these call sites at all -- **the answer is not \"no, don't\", it is \"there is nothing\nthere to write.\"**\n\n**(2) \"A DRIVER WILL USUALLY REACH BOTH WITHIN ONE INTERRUPT CYCLE\" IS FALSE OF THIS CODE, and I\nused it to argue for two latch bits.**  grok traced it: *\"`isa_interrupt_common` takes ONE EDGE per\ncall -- ASSERT if IRR went 0->nonzero, DEASSERT if it went nonzero->0, return if the aggregate\nstayed asserted.  The 8042 probe never reads the `0x55` reply, so it never deasserts.  The UART probe\nleaves IER on and free-runs, so tick keeps asserting.  **Existing witnesses do not touch\n`_deassert`.**\"*\n> **THE CONSEQUENCE IS COVERAGE DEBT AND IT IS DISCLOSED: the deassert latch is UNWITNESSED.**  Two\n> bits remain the right choice -- for the opposite reason to the one I gave -- because the two\n> callbacks are reached on DIFFERENT edges and a shared bit would report whichever arrived first.\n\n**(3) `detector:` AS THE WITNESS TERM IS A CATEGORY ERROR.**  I proposed it in Q5.  grok: *\"That term\nis for a fix with a measured detector and NO guest-path witness.  This row already has TWO rung-3\nprobes.  Unobservable DELIVERY is a CONTRACT fact, not a missing witness of the kill.\"*  Correct --\nthe round witnesses the TERMINATION, which is what it fixes; it does not claim to witness delivery,\nwhich it does not add.  The witness term stays `probe:`.\n\n**(4) MY §C0 COUNT WAS STALE** -- see `pass1_20260908/CORRECTION_C0.md`, written before this.  grok's\nframing adds the general rule: *\"Do not treat a DIRTY-TREE `grep -c` as the ship check.\"*\n\n*** (5) THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A BROKEN ROUND, AND IT NEEDS MEASUREMENT NOT READING. ***\n\ngrok: *\"The UART probe's `stub named` row is HOSTILE to member 3.  It wants `lca_interrupt_assert`\nABSENT.  A latched `fatal(\"...TODO\")` keeps that row RED FOREVER.  Drop or re-pin it under the\noracle-correction rule; do not ship member 3 against that want.\"*\n\n**The row is exactly as described:**\n`row(\"stub named\", \"no\" if \"lca_interrupt_assert\" not in t3 else \"yes\", \"no\", ...)`\n\n**BUT THE HAZARD MAY NOT REACH MY IMPLEMENTATION, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE MESSAGE TEXT.**  grok\nreasoned from a generic *latched `fatal(\"...TODO\")`* -- keeping the original wording, which contains\nthe function name.  The announce I wrote says *\"[ lca: ISA interrupt assert not implemented (Alpha\nhas no interrupt delivery); ignored  (once) ]\"* -- **\"ISA interrupt assert\", not\n`lca_interrupt_assert`** -- so the forbidden literal may never appear.\n\n> *** THAT IS A READING OF MY OWN STRING AND IT IS EXACTLY THE KIND OF CLAIM THIS PROJECT REQUIRES\n> TO BE MEASURED.  The post-fix run settles it; nothing is claimed here. ***  And whichever way it\n> lands, grok has identified a REAL DESIGN CONSTRAINT that was not in my brief and that I satisfied\n> by accident rather than by intent: **the announce must not contain the literal\n> `lca_interrupt_assert`, or it pins a shipped witness row red forever.**\n\n*** THE FIX IS IMPLEMENTED AND MEASURED, 2026-09-08.  ONE INSTRUMENT, BOTH DIRECTIONS. ***\n\n                                     PRE-FIX   POST-FIX\n    lca_irqstub_probe.py               3/6       6/6\n    lca_irqstub_uart_probe.py          2/5       5/5\n\nScratch clone `/tmp/gxlca`, baseline `git show HEAD:src/devices/dev_lca.c` rebuilt in the clone.\nNo project tree mutated.  Raws + a README naming which instrument produced which figure:\n`regress/artifacts/lcairqstubkill/fix_20260908/`.\n\n**THE DIFF:** both stubs keep a diagnostic and lose the termination -- option-set member 3, DECLINE\nplus a bounded announce.  **PER-INSTANCE** via `interrupt->extra`, not function-static, so one guest\naccess to one device instance cannot silence every other instance ([[staticlatchscope]]).  **TWO\nseparate latch bits**, because grok established the two callbacks are reached on DIFFERENT EDGES --\n`isa_interrupt_common` takes one edge per call -- so a shared bit would report whichever arrived\nfirst and hide the other entirely.\n\n**THREE ROWS ADDED, because both probes' own contracts demanded them.**  `lca_irqstub_probe.py`'s\nheader says: *\"it is NOT a detector of a repair: 'host survives' would green for a silent return, for\na latched announce, and for an actual interrupt implementation alike.  A fix round owes a row that\ndiscriminates those.\"*\n\n    irq announce   the announce text is present -- a silent return prints nothing, and a REAL\n                   implementation would print nothing either, so only this repair prints this\n    irq honest     the announce contains \"Alpha has no\" -- the round's claim is that it stops the\n                   host dying WITHOUT making interrupts work, and this is the only place a reader\n                   ever learns that\n    irq latched    COUNTS the announce in the free-running UART session, want exactly 1.  That\n                   session's tick re-asserts repeatedly, so an unlatched announce prints many times.\n                   Counting is the oracle because a PRESENCE row would be satisfied by a latch that\n                   never engages -- the very mutant it exists to catch.\n\n*** A DEFECT I INTRODUCED WHILE ADDING THEM, AND CAUGHT BY THE PROBE'S OWN OUTPUT. ***  The rows went\nin BELOW each score computation, splitting both scorers: numerator over the old row set, denominator\nevaluated at print time.  **The 8042 probe printed SIX ROWS ALL `ok` UNDER A SUMMARY OF 4/6.**  The\nUART probe reported 3/5 pre-fix where the honest score was 2/5 -- and post-fix it rendered 5/5\nCORRECTLY BY COINCIDENCE, because nothing failed.  Repaired in both files by moving the computation\nadjacent to the print. Surfaced while verifying a claim in the grok seat's `lcabadval` pass-1 BODY (its\nverdict line did not carry it) -- and **verified to be sharper than the seat stated it**, by opening\nboth functions.\n\n**THE DEFECT: an ACCEPTED write to one register arms a host kill in a DIFFERENT function.**\n\n`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)`, `case LCA_IOC_CONF`:\n\n    d->ioc_conf = idata;\n    /*  Only bit 0 is implemented so far, the PCI bus 0 vs\n        bus non-0 selection bit.  */\n    if (idata & ~1) {\n            fatal(\"TODO: Write to unimplemented bit of IOC_CONF: 0x%\" PRIx64 \"\\n\", ...);\n            exit(1);\n    }\n\n**`0x1` PASSES that guard.**  `idata & ~1` is 0, so the write is accepted, stored, and the arm's own\ncomment describes bit 0 as the implemented selector.\n\n`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)` then consumes it:\n\n    bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;\n    if (bus == 0) {\n            for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)\n                    if (tag & (0x800 << dev))\n                            break;\n            if (dev >= 21) {\n                    /*  fatal(\"[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\\n\");\n                    exit(1);  */\n                    dev = 0;\n            }\n    } else {\n            fatal(\"TODO. Non-zero bus.\\n\");\n            exit(1);\n    }\n\nSo: **guest writes 1 to IOC_CONF (accepted), then performs any PCI configuration-space access, and\nthe host process exits.**  Two guest accesses, both to registers the model implements, neither of\nthem rejected.\n\n*** WHY IT IS ITS OWN ROW AND NOT PART OF [[lcabadval]]. ***  Every kill `lcabadval` covers is a\nguard REFUSING a value it does not recognise.  **This one fires on a value the guard ACCEPTS**, in a\ndifferent function, on a later access.  The acceptance and the kill are separated in both code and\ntime, which is why a per-arm audit of `lca_ioc` walks straight past it -- as mine did, twice.\n\n*** AND THE SEAT'S FRAMING NEEDED CORRECTING, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH RECORDING. ***  grok wrote\nthat this exit becomes *\"newly reachable from a CONF extra-bit write that also sets bit 0; today\nthat write dies in the CONF guard first.\"*  That is true of an EXTRA-BIT write (e.g. `0x3`), and it\nmade the kill sound like a CONSEQUENCE of adopting option A.  **It is not: `0x1` alone reaches it\ntoday, with no fix applied.**  A widens which CONF values arrive here; it does not open the door.\nThe door is open now.  *Checked by reading both arms rather than by accepting the seat's sentence --\nwhich is this project's own rule, and the seat was right about the site while being wrong about the\ntiming.*\n\n**THE IRONY, and it is a design signal rather than a joke:** the `bus == 0` branch is the file's own\ngraceful-degradation PRECEDENT -- a commented-out `fatal()`/`exit(1)` with `dev = 0;` substituted --\nand [[lcabadval]] cites that very loop as a transferable in-tree warrant.  **The `else` five lines\nbelow it is a bare kill.**  Same function, opposite dispositions, no comment explaining why.\n\n**REACHABILITY, stated honestly rather than assumed.**  Bit 0 is a bus SELECTOR, so a guest reaches\nthis by selecting a bus the model does not implement and then doing config access -- plausible for\nfirmware or an OS that probes beyond bus 0, and trivially reachable by a probe.  **Whether any\nshipped guest on `alphabook1` actually does it is NOT established here**, and the row does not claim\nit.  What is established is that no guard stands between an accepted write and the kill.\n\n**WITNESS: a rung-3 probe is available and most of it already exists.**  `device_add(machine, \"lca\")`\nis `ST_ALPHABOOK1` ONLY (`machine_alpha.c`), and `regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py` ALREADY drives a\nguest `stq`/`ldq` at `LCA_IOC_CONF` (`0xfffffc0180000020`, K0SEG | physical) on unmodified\n`alphabook1`.  This row needs one more session: `stq 0x1` to CONF, then a PCI config-space access.\n**Three traps that probe already paid for:** debugger `dump` skips `DM_DEFAULT` handlers; a debugger\n`put d` of a killing value IS a host kill but is NOT a guest instruction, so it does not witness; and\nHAE at window offset 0 silently succeeds, so a miss control must not use `LCA_IOC_BASE+0`.\n\n**DISPOSITION, not yet adjudicated:** it is the same de-fatalisation family, but the option set\ndiffers because the kill is not a value refusal -- the honest members here are DECLINE THE CONFIG\nACCESS (return without dispatching, with a latched announce) or IMPLEMENT bus != 0.  **Note the\narchitecturally correct error path is blocked for the same reason grok gave on the sibling row:\n`LCA_IOC_STAT0`/`STAT1` have no case at all and hit the `default:` arm's `exit(1)`, so LCA cannot\nreport a device error through the register the header designates for it.**\n\nsev HIGH: a guest-drivable host kill, reached by two ordinary accesses with no rejected value\nanywhere on the path.\n\n*** SIBLING CHECK DISCHARGED WITH A DENOMINATOR, 2026-09-07 -- the class has EXACTLY ONE member in\nthis file. ***  The rule is grep for the siblings before calling a finding whole; here the question\nis which lca_ioc fields have a consumer at all, since a field nothing reads cannot arm a delayed\nkill.  Counting references that are neither the readback (`odata = d->X`) nor the store (`d->X = `):\n\n    ioc_conf          1 consumer   <- `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` in lca_pci_conf.  THIS ROW.\n    tlb_enable        0\n    window_base_0     0            window_base_1     0\n    window_mask_0     0            window_mask_1     0\n    window_t_base_0   0            window_t_base_1   0\n\n**So the accepted-write-arms-a-later-kill shape cannot recur elsewhere in dev_lca.c**: the other\nseven fields are write-and-read-back with nothing downstream.  That is also why [[lcabadval]]s five\npure-storage arms are a different problem from this one, and why a fix here does not generalise to\nthem.\n\n*Not claimed beyond this file.  The general class -- an ACCEPTED store that a later access consumes\ninto a terminating path -- is a plausible cross-device census and is NOT run here.*\n\n*** THE WITNESS-DESIGN CHECK WAS REFUSED BY codex ON A CONTENT FILTER, 2026-09-07 -- recorded as a\nSEAT FAILURE, never as agreement. ***  Zero-byte `.txt`, exit 0, the error only in the `.err`:\n*\"This content was flagged for possible cybersecurity risk.\"*  Full analysis and the two same-day\nnegative controls are on [[codexsubjectrefusal]].  **Re-fired on grok**, which reads the tree, takes\nits brief from a file and has no such filter -- so the question is covered, not lost. The\nalternate-transport rule paid off completely: the recovery is BETTER than the refused seat would\nhave given, not a degraded substitute. ***\n\n**DENOMINATOR, and it is the largest this row has seen:** all of `dev_lca.c` (390 lines -- both\nhandlers, `DEVINIT`, ISA, interrupt stubs); `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)` and\n`DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)` end to end; `bus_pci_data_access`/`bus_pci_setaddr` in `bus_pci.c`;\n`alpha_translate_v2p` K0SEG in `memory_alpha.c`; the device and unmapped paths in `memory_rw.c`;\n`LS_GENERIC_N`/`LS_N` in `cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c`; `fatal()` in `debugmsg.c`; `ST_ALPHABOOK1`\nin `machine_alpha.c`; and all of `regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py`.\n\n**CONFIRMED:** S2 reaches `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` for ANY access in the window -- no `switch` on\n`relative_addr`, no `len` test, no `writeflag` test that can skip it, no early `return`; the only\n`writeflag` use before it is filling `idata`, which is not a guard.  `len` is first used AFTER the\nkill.  And `ioc_conf` has **exactly two readers**, so nothing else can be armed by S1.\n\n*** FOUR CORRECTIONS THAT CHANGE THE CONSTRUCTION: ***\n\n1. **MY LIVENESS CONTROL IS MISLABELLED.**  `ioc rt` proves the WRITER (`lca_ioc`'s CONF arm)\n   round-trips `0x1`.  **It says nothing about S2's window.**  PCI_CONF liveness is the armed\n   session's unique message (the handler ran) PLUS the control surviving (the same access is not an\n   unconditional kill).  A positive signature is available if wanted: an empty-slot read returns\n   `0xffffffffffffffff`, where unmapped returns 0.\n2. **S2 MUST BE A READ, NOT A WRITE.**  Both reach the kill, but an empty-slot WRITE makes\n   `bus_pci_data_access` print `[ ... write to non-existant device ... ]` -- so the CONTROL would\n   carry a second `fatal()` on stderr.  An empty-slot READ of reg 0 is the architectural ID probe\n   and is silent.\n3. **A TIGHTER VARIANT: ONE PROCESS, not two sessions.**  control `ldq` (survives), then S1, then\n   the armed `ldq` (dies) -- **only `ioc_conf` changes between the two loads.**  Separate sessions\n   are still adequate, but this removes the residual that two processes could differ in something\n   that ran before the debugger prompt.\n4. **KEEP IT ON `step`.**  `continue` would make the `lca_interrupt_assert`/`_deassert` stubs a\n   confounder -- and `cpu_dyntrans.c` skips interrupt delivery while single-stepping anyway.\n\n*** THE CONTRACT LIMIT, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE FOR THE FIX ROUND: THIS IS A WITNESS OF\nTHE `exit(1)`, NOT A DETECTOR OF THE REPAIR. ***  grok: *\"'Host survives, session completes' greens\nfor a silent `return 1` that skips `bus_pci_*` and `memory_writemax64`, for a latched announce, AND\nfor falling through into existing `bus_pci` with `cur_bus=1` (empty type-1 also survives).\"*  So the\npost-fix oracle cannot discriminate between fix members, and a round that ships one must build a row\nthat can.  **Post-fix caution in the same breath: if someone later implements type-1 by falling\nthrough to `bus_pci_setaddr(bus=1, ...)`, empty bus 1 is a non-killing miss today, so the witness\ngoes green -- do NOT read that green as \"type-1 is modelled.\"**\n\n**WHY OFFSET 0 IS THE RIGHT CONTROL ADDRESS, which I had asserted without warrant:** it is not\nHAE-like (HAE is an exact-address `break` in `lca_ioc`; `lca_pci_conf` has no per-offset special\ncase).  On the ARMED session the offset is irrelevant -- `bus == 1` exits before `tag`/`dev` are\nused.  On the SURVIVING control, `tag == 0` matches no IDSEL bit, so the `dev >= 21` body runs --\n**and that body is already the commented-out kill with `dev = 0;`** -- then an empty-slot read\nreturns `(uint64_t)-1` without exiting.  `0x18` (the NetBSD `| 0x3 << 3` encoding) yields the same\n`tag`/`reg` and is equivalent.  **Do NOT use `relative_addr = 0x800000`**, which talks to the\nregistered `i82378zb` at device 7.\n\n*** AND grok FOUND THE DESIGN ARGUMENT SITTING FIVE LINES ABOVE THE DEFECT: the `dev >= 21` arm is\nALREADY DE-FATALISED, in the same function, and the surviving control USES that path. ***  *\"If that\n`exit(1)` were still live, the control would die and you would misread PCI_CONF as unconditionally\nfatal.  The file already contains the decline precedent the `else` lacks.\"*  That is the strongest\navailable warrant for declining here, and it is one function away from the kill.\n\nAlso confirmed: FAULT (member 5) is unavailable -- `memory_rw.c` delivers a failed device access as\nan exception only under `MEM_MIPS`/`MEM_M88K`, and this path always `return 1` on the live path, so\nthe kill is `exit(1)` and not `return 0`.  And `lca_isa`'s `ofs > len` kill is a DIFFERENT window\n(`LCA_PCI_SIO`), not reachable from `0x1e0000000`.\n\n*** CROSS-REFERENCE, AND A CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN NOVELTY CLAIM. That is what makes the kill reachable with no rejected\nvalue anywhere on the path -- an accepted write in one handler arming a terminating branch in\nanother -- and it is why the disposition differs from lcabadval's six value guards, whose kills all\nfire on refusal.  Plus the witness design, now reviewed SOUND by grok.\n\n**KEPT AS A SEPARATE ROW** because it has its own disposition (decline the config access, or\nimplement bus != 0) and its own witness, and because folding it into a row named for VALUE GUARDS\nwould hide it -- which is the census's own closing lesson: *a row named after one class hides the\nmembers of the others.*\n\n*** AND THE CENSUS NAMES SOMETHING PLAUSIBLY WORSE THAN THIS ROW, WHICH I SHOULD SAY OUT LOUD RATHER\nTHAN LEAVE IN A TABLE: the two INTERRUPT STUBS at :73 and :85. ***  `lca_interrupt_assert` and\n`lca_interrupt_deassert` are bare `fatal(\"...TODO\"); exit(1)` with **NO GUARD AT ALL**, so *any*\ndevice asserting an interrupt through the LCA ends the process regardless of what it writes.  In the\ncensus's words: the value-guard registers *\"need a firmware revision that writes something else;\nthese two need only that an interrupt is asserted.\"*  That is member 7 (IMPLEMENT) of the option\nset -- a whole path stubbed out, with no value being refused -- and it is a stronger candidate for\nthe next critical round than this one.\n\n*** THE RUNG-3 WITNESS EXISTS AND THE DEFECT IS MEASURED, 2026-09-07.  regress/lca_confbus_probe.py,\nartifact regress/artifacts/lcaconfbus1kill/witness_HEAD_20260907.raw, sentinel LCACONFBUS_DONE. ***\n\n    bus ctl      yes                    yes                    CONTROL: identical read at ioc_conf==0 survived   ok\n    bus ctlsig   0xffffffffffffffff     0xffffffffffffffff     DEVICE SIGNATURE: empty-slot ID probe             ok\n    bus kill     dead                   alive                  <- THE DEFECT                                     FAIL\n    bus attrib   yes                    yes                    death carried THIS arm@s diagnostic               ok\n      EVIDENCE: TODO. The probe now ECHOES the matched line into its own output.  *A green row whose\n   evidence is not in the artifact is not verifiable, however sound the logic.*\n2. **I nearly filed a false correction to grok.**  It said the `TODO. One\n   source site, `dev_lca.c:133`.  grok was right; my apparatus swept binaries.\n\n*** THE FIX DESIGN IS CLEARER THAN THIS ROW ASSUMED, AND IT DIVERGES FROM ITS SIBLING FOR A REASON\nWORTH STATING.  Derived 2026-09-07 from the codex fix design on [[lcairqstubkill]] plus this row's\nown witness. ***\n\n**THE TWO LCA ROWS LOOK LIKE ONE PROBLEM AND ARE NOT.**  Both are bare `fatal()+exit(1)` reached by\nordinary guest access, so it is tempting to give them one disposition.  The difference is what the\nguest is ASKING FOR:\n\n* [[lcairqstubkill]] -- an interrupt DELIVERY.  There is no correct answer that is not delivery: a\n  decline LOSES AN EVENT, silently or otherwise, and the guest can never learn of it.  codex's\n  disposition there is \"diagnose and stop the guest CPU\", because continuing is a lie.\n* **THIS ROW -- a PCI configuration cycle on bus 1.  There IS a correct answer, and hardware gives\n  it: a config read of an absent device returns ALL ONES.**  An unimplemented BUS is legitimately\n  EMPTY.  Declining is not a lost event; it is the architecturally right reply.\n\n> **So the dispositions properly differ, and the reason is not \"one is worse\".  For a DELIVERY path\n> an unimplemented target has no honest continuation; for an ADDRESSING path it has one, and the\n> tree already implements it.**\n\n*** AND THE TREE ALREADY DOES THE RIGHT THING ONE STEP DOWN, which the witness measured by accident.\n***  The `bus == 0` branch already reaches `bus_pci_setaddr` / `bus_pci_data_access`, and this row's\nown witness recorded the result: **a read of an empty slot returns `0xffffffffffffffff`** (the\n`bus ctlsig` row, MEASURED).  grok noted the same about the armed side in its design review --\n*\"falling through into existing bus_pci with cur_bus=1 (empty type-1 also survives)\"*.\n\n**SO THE LIKELY FIX IS NOT A DECLINE AT ALL BUT A FALL-THROUGH:** stop special-casing `bus != 0`,\nlet the existing `bus_pci` path handle it, and an empty bus 1 answers all-ones by itself.  That is\noption-set member 7 (IMPLEMENT) reached cheaply, because the machinery exists -- unlike the sibling\nrow, where member 7 is blocked at the dyntrans core.\n\n*** THE DISCRIMINATING ROW IS THEREFORE THE RETURNED VALUE, NOT THE RUNNING STATE. ***  codex's\noracle for the sibling row (\"stopped guest state, plus the reason\") is right THERE and wrong HERE,\nbecause a correct fix must leave the guest RUNNING.  The observable that separates the candidates\nhere is what the armed read returns:\n\n    fall through to bus_pci (empty bus 1)      0xffffffffffffffff    <- architecturally correct\n    silent return without servicing            0x0 or unchanged      <- looks like UNMAPPED\n    latched announce then return unserviced    0x0 or unchanged      <- same, plus a message\n    the existing exit(1)                       host gone\n\n**And this probe ALREADY HAS THAT ROW SHAPE**: `bus ctlsig` asserts exactly `0xffffffffffffffff` on\nthe bus-0 control, and distinguishes it from the `0x0` an unmapped read gives.  The fix round adds\nthe mirror of it on the ARMED side.  *No new capability is needed, which is not true of the sibling.*\n\n**NOT YET VERIFIED, and it is the one thing to check before building:** that `bus_pci` genuinely\ntolerates `cur_bus == 1` rather than asserting or indexing something sized for one bus.  grok's\nreview says empty type-1 survives; **that is a seat's reading and this row has not measured it.**\nThe cheap check is to build the fall-through and run the existing probe -- the armed row should turn\nfrom `dead` to `0xffffffffffffffff`.\n\n*** THE UNVERIFIED LINK IS CLOSED BY READING: `bus_pci' IS BUS-AGNOSTIC BY CONSTRUCTION, SO THE\nFALL-THROUGH FIX IS SOUND WITHOUT A NEW CAPABILITY. ***\n\nThe paragraph above flagged one thing to check before building -- whether `bus_pci' tolerates\n`cur_bus == 1' rather than asserting or indexing something sized for a single bus.  It does:\n\n    bus_pci.h:84   int  cur_bus, cur_device, cur_func, cur_reg;   <- a plain int, not bus-0-only\n    bus_pci.h:87   struct pci_device *first_device;               <- a LINKED LIST, not an array\n                                                                     indexed by bus number\n    bus_pci.c:102  if (dev->bus == pci_data->cur_bus && ...)      <- devices carry their OWN bus\n                                                                     field and are MATCHED against it\n    bus_pci.c:200  pci_data->cur_bus = bus;                       <- set from the caller, unchecked\n\n**So a lookup with `cur_bus = 1` walks the list, matches nothing (no device carries `bus == 1`), and\nfalls into the no-device path -- which this row's own witness has already MEASURED returning\n`0xffffffffffffffff` on bus 0.**  Nothing is sized for one bus and nothing asserts.\n\n*** THAT CONFIRMS grok's READING AND UPGRADES IT FROM A SEAT CLAIM TO A CHECKED ONE. ***  Its design\nreview said *\"falling through into existing bus_pci with cur_bus=1 (empty type-1 also survives)\"*,\nwhich this row correctly recorded as unmeasured.  It is now verified by source rather than accepted.\n\n**CONSEQUENCE: the fix is a DELETION, not an addition.**  Remove the `bus != 0` special case and let\nthe existing path serve the cycle; an absent device on an absent bus answers all-ones by itself.\nThat is the cheapest member-7 in the option set -- and the addition licence is satisfied trivially,\nbecause nothing is added.\n\n**WHAT STILL OWES A MEASUREMENT, and it is one run:** build the fall-through and re-run\n`regress/lca_confbus_probe.py`.  **PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION: the `bus kill' row turns from `dead'\nto `alive', and a mirror of `bus ctlsig' on the armed side should read `0xffffffffffffffff`** -- the\nsame value the bus-0 control already returns, which is what distinguishes a serviced empty-bus cycle\nfrom a silent unserviced return (`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped).\n\n*** PASS-1 FIX PANEL, 2026-09-07 -- PROVISIONAL: recorded on THREE landed seats (deepseek, minimax,\nthe kimi-k3:cloud substitute) while codex, grok and glm were still answering.  Any ruling short of\nthe roster is provisional on its face. ***\n\n**Q1 IS SETTLED AND MY WORRY WAS UNFOUNDED -- AND THE PROOF IS INSIDE THE EXISTING CODE, WHICH I HAD\nNOT THOUGHT TO USE.**  I asked whether the file's tag comment describes the same `tag` this code\ncomputes (`(relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff`) or a pre-shift value.  minimax:\n\n> *\"The existing IDSEL check proves the code's `tag` IS the comment's `tag`.  The `bus == 0` branch\n> checks `tag & (0x800U << dev)` for dev 0..20.  The mask `1 << (dev+11)` only matches real hardware\n> if `tag` holds IDSEL bits at positions 11..31 -- exactly the comment's type-0 layout.  That branch\n> is EXERCISED TODAY on real bus-0 traffic and works, so the interpretation is FORCED.\"*\n\n`0x800` is exactly `1 << 11`, and the kimi substitute reaches it independently (*\"func at bits 10..8\nin both rows\"*).  **So `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is the faithful type-1 decode.**  *A working branch\nof the same function is a stronger oracle for a layout question than the comment is -- the comment\ncould be stale, the working code cannot be.*\n\n*** Q2 IS THE REAL DEFECT IN MY SKETCH, AND ALL THREE SEATS FLAG IT: THE FIX AS WRITTEN WOULD BE\nINCOHERENT. ***  deepseek: *\"the sketch is incomplete/incoherent because it keeps\n`bus = d->ioc_conf & 1` while the type-1 tag comment says bus is in tag bits 23..16.\"*  The kimi\nsubstitute sharpens it: **the model computes THREE bus/type sources from the address -- the AD[1:0]\ntype bits, the tag's bus field, and `ioc_conf & 1` -- and DISCARDS TWO.**  *\"The packet cannot say\nwhich one real hardware honours.\"*\n\n> **So decoding `dev` from the type-1 layout while taking `bus` from a register bit mixes two\n> addressing models.**  Either the tag is authoritative (and `bus` must come from bits 23..16) or the\n> register is (and the type-1 tag layout does not apply).  **The fix cannot be written until that is\n> chosen, and no seat could choose it from the packet.**\n\n**Q4: THE DISCRIMINATING ROW IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT -- unanimous among the three.**  My\nproposed armed-side all-ones row separates *serviced an empty bus* from *returned without servicing*\n(`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped).  **It cannot separate IMPLEMENT from an explicit all-ones\nreturn**, because an EMPTY bus 1 answers all-ones under both.  kimi: *\"only a populated bus-1 device\ncan\"* tell them apart -- and nothing in this tree places one.\n\n*That is a real limit on what this round can claim, and it is better to know it before building the\nrow than after.*\n\n**NOT ADOPTED PENDING THE ROSTER.**  The Q2 question is a hardware-behaviour question the packet\ncannot settle, and `_scratchpad/` holds no LCA/21066 datasheet (the `ls _scratchpad/*.pdf` list has\nDC21285 but no 21066), so it may not be settleable from this machine at all -- which would make it a\ncandidate for the 2026-09-09 adjudication rather than for a fix round.\n\n*** grok LANDED AND SETTLED Q2 -- THE ANSWER THE PACKET-FED SEATS COULD NOT REACH, AND IT DISSOLVES\nTHE INCOHERENCE RATHER THAN CHOOSING A SIDE. ***\n\n> **VERDICT: IMPLEMENT, but the sketch is one assignment short.**  `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is the\n> right extract of *this* `tag` (Q1 is not a double-shift).  **Also assign\n> `bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff` -- `ioc_conf & 1` is the PCI cycle TYPE, not the bus number.**\n> Member 7; fall through into `bus_pci`.  The proposed all-ones row is necessary and not sufficient:\n> the witness address is not a type-1 tag, and empty-slot data cannot pin the extract.\n\n**WHY THIS RESOLVES THE CONFLICT INSTEAD OF PICKING A WINNER.**  deepseek and the kimi substitute\nwere right that taking `dev` from the tag while taking `bus` from a register bit mixes two addressing\nmodels.  grok's reading shows the register bit is not a bus selector at all: it chooses the CYCLE\nTYPE -- type 0 addresses the local bus, type 1 addresses a bus behind a bridge -- and in type 1 the\nbus number comes from the tag.  **The file's own comment, \"the PCI bus 0 vs bus non-0 selection bit\",\nis a loose description of exactly that**, so the code comment and grok agree once the names are\nstraightened.  *The variable is simply misnamed: `bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;` stores a cycle type in\nsomething called `bus`.*\n\n**THE FIX IS THEREFORE TWO ASSIGNMENTS, NOT ONE:**\n\n    } else {\n            bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;    /*  type-1: bus is bits 23..16  */\n            dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;    /*  type-1: device is bits 15..11  */\n    }\n\n*** AND grok BREAKS MY PROPOSED DISCRIMINATING ROW FOR A REASON I HAD NOT SEEN: \"the witness address\nis not a type-1 tag\". ***  The existing probe reads at `LCA_PCI_CONF + 0`, so `tag == 0` -- which\nunder the type-1 decode means bus 0, device 0.  **The row would therefore exercise the new code with\na degenerate tag and prove nothing about the extract**, and \"empty-slot data cannot pin the extract\"\nbecause an absent device answers all-ones whatever bus/dev were decoded.\n\n> **So the round needs a row that drives a NON-ZERO type-1 tag and observes something that depends\n> on the decoded bus/dev -- and with no device on bus 1, there is nothing to observe.**  That is the\n> same limit the three packet-fed seats reached from the other side (*\"only a populated bus-1 device\n> can\"* separate IMPLEMENT from a faked all-ones return).  Two independent routes to one conclusion.\n\n**HARDWARE CLAIM, FLAGGED AS UNVERIFIABLE FROM THIS MACHINE:** that `ioc_conf & 1` is the cycle type\nis a statement about the 21066/LCA, and **`_scratchpad/` holds no LCA datasheet** -- the PDF list has\nDC21285 but nothing for this chipset.  It is consistent with the file's comment and with standard PCI\ntype-0/type-1 addressing, but it is not CHECKED here, and this project's rule is to cite only what\nhas been read.  **That is a good candidate for the 2026-09-09 adjudication rather than for me.**\n\n*** THE PANEL IS COMPLETE (6 seats) AND UNANIMOUS ON THE DESIGN -- AND codex CITED A PRIMARY SOURCE\nSITTING ON THIS MACHINE THAT I HAD JUST DECLARED UNAVAILABLE. ***\n\nI wrote, one paragraph above, that the cycle-type claim is *\"a statement about the 21066 ... not\nCHECKED here\"* because `_scratchpad/` holds no LCA datasheet. &secondary, &device, 0);\n    :127   if (secondary) {\n    :130           REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01;\n    :132   } else {\n    :134           /*  on the LCA, must frob the tag used for devices on fork's own loosely-worded comment (\"bus 0 vs bus non-0 selection bit\") was groping at.  **The\nfork's variable is misnamed**, and its type-0 handling is correct -- `(1 << (device + 11))` performs a config access, and I described that as an ordinary but\nsynthetic pair of guest accesses.  **It is not synthetic: it is the OpenBSD driver's own\nsecondary-bus path, line for line.**  `REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01` followed by the config read is\nwhat `lca_conf_read` DOES whenever it is asked about a device behind a bridge.\n\n> **So the kill is not merely reachable in principle -- it is on the code path a real guest driver\n> takes the moment it probes a secondary bus.**  The row previously said \"NOT CLAIMED: that any\n> shipped guest issues this sequence.\"  That caution can now be withdrawn: a shipped guest driver\n> issues exactly this sequence, by construction, for secondary-bus config cycles.\n\n**THE FIX, now backed by the driver rather than by reading the fork's comment:**\n\n    } else {\n            bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;    /*  type-1: bus is bits 23..16   */\n            dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;    /*  type-1: device is bits 15..11 */\n    }\n\n**WHAT IS STILL NOT SETTLED, and every seat agrees:** the discriminating row.  grok -- *\"the witness\naddress is not a type-1 tag, and empty-slot data cannot pin the extract\"*; glm -- the row Derived 2026-09-08 by opening the dispatcher. ***\n\n`src/cpus/memory_rw.c`, the device-failure path -- the whole of it:\n\n    if (res <= 0 && !no_exceptions) {\n            debug(\"[ %s device '%s' addr %08lx failed ]\");\n    #ifdef MEM_MIPS\n            mips_cpu_exception(cpu, cache == CACHE_INSTRUCTION? THE SHIFT IS RIGHT AND ALMOST EVERYTHING AROUND IT NEEDED CORRECTING --\nINCLUDING TWO PREMISES I STATED AS SETTLED. ***\n\n**CONFIRMED, with a denominator.**  `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is correct: `relative_addr >> 5`\nundoes the address expansion, so for a valid tag T and aligned register R,\n`A = (T << 5) | (R << 5) | 24` gives `((A >> 5) & ~0xff) == T`, putting device bits at 15..11 of\nthe recovered tag.  Validated over **4,194,304 encode/decode combinations, zero mismatches** --\nand codex labelled that an ARITHMETIC check, not an emulator test, which is the honest form.\n\n*** MY IMPLEMENT SKETCH WAS INCOMPLETE: IT DECODED `dev` AND NOT `bus`. ***  `IOC_CONF` selects\nthe CYCLE FORMAT, it does not supply the bus number -- PCI address bits 23..2 come from CPU\naddress bits 28..7, and only bits 1..0 come from `IOC_CONF[1:0]`.  So the minimum correction is\n\n    } else {\n        bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;\n        dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;\n    }\n\n> **AND THE TRAP IN THE OBVIOUS ALTERNATIVE, which I would have walked into: DO NOT CHOOSE THE\n> DECODING BRANCH FROM THE EXTRACTED BUS FIELD.**  Primary-bus IDSEL bits overlap that field, and\n> keeping `bus == 1` in the forwarding call **collapses every type-1 destination onto bus 1**.\n\n*** PREMISE 4 IS FALSE AS STATED, and the refutation is one function away. ***  I wrote that an\nempty-slot read returns all-ones.  `bus_pci_data_access` implements a THREE-WAY contract:\n\n    read, register 0        ->  0xffffffffffffffff\n    read, any other reg     ->  ZERO\n    write                   ->  a diagnostic on EVERY access, then returns\n\nThe measured ID-register instance stands; the GENERAL claim does not.  **This matters because it\nmeans the candidate dispositions differ TODAY, with no bus-1 device added at all** -- an\nunconditional all-ones responder differs from the backend at nonzero registers, and announcement\npolicies differ on diagnostic output.  (The write diagnostic does not terminate: `fatal()` returns\nnormally.)\n\n*** THE DISCRIMINATING ROW I PROPOSED CANNOT DISCRIMINATE, AND IT HAS THREE FALSE-POSITIVE TRAPS.\n***  At register 0, all-ones is returned by correct forwarding, by the INCORRECT bus-fixed-to-1\nforwarding, by explicit all-ones completion, and by announced all-ones completion -- four\ndispositions, one observable.  Worse:\n\n* **THE EXISTING ADDRESS DOES NOT ENCODE BUS 1.**  `lca_confbus_probe.py`'s setup uses a ZERO\n  window offset, so the recovered tag -- bus field included -- is zero, and the control side\n  reaches the **no-IDSEL fallback to device 0**.  *The witness establishes mode-dependent\n  termination, not a correctly addressed secondary-bus transaction.*  That is a scope correction\n  to what this row's existing witness is entitled to claim.\n* **A FAILED LOAD PRESERVES THE CONTROL SIGNATURE.**  `memory_rw` pre-zeros, so a handler that\n  returns success without filling data yields zero -- but a literal `return 0` takes the FAILURE\n  path, and `LS_GENERIC_N` then returns *before updating the destination register*, so the witness\n  **reads back the all-ones the register already held.**  A vacuity trap of exactly the shape the\n  carrier's witness clauses exist to catch.\n* **THE PARSER TAKES THE FIRST OBSERVATION** (`printed` uses `re.search`), so adding another print\n  and calling the helper again retrieves the CONTROL's result rather than the new one.\n\n**WHAT IT PRESCRIBES INSTEAD:** keep the signature check as an empty-ID COMPLETION check with an\nindependently identified result and a distinct prior destination value, and add a **model-level\nrouting fixture** observing the exact bus/device/function/register passed onward -- distinct\nsynthetic devices on bus 1 and on another bus separate correct decoding from hardcoded bus 1 and\nfrom constant-return stubs.  Include write dispatch and preservation of primary-bus decoding.\n\n*** A SECOND ARCHITECTURAL PREMISE CORRECTED: RAW LCA ABSENCE DOES NOT GUARANTEE ALL-ONES. ***\nDigital's §6.2.18 says an unanswered transaction is a MASTER ABORT: the read returns *arbitrary\ndata with error status*, raising an instruction exception plus interrupt and IOC error logging.\n**So the 64-bit all-ones signature is an EMULATOR-COMPATIBILITY oracle, not a hardware contract**,\nand the round may not claim hardware fidelity for it.  The current path delegates to the generic\nbackend and returns success; it implements none of that error sequence.\n\n**SCOPE, stated so the round does not overclaim what it closes:** this closes the configuration\nhandler's termination and its missing destination decode ONLY.  Still open in the same file --\n`lca_ioc`'s unconditional read/write `default` termination and its register-value rejections;\n`lca_interrupt_assert` / `lca_interrupt_deassert`; and `lca_isa`'s separate guard.  Nor does\nforwarding implement bridge topology or configuration-cycle error reporting: the backend matches a\nnumeric tuple and receives **no cycle-type argument at all**, so type 1 with a zero bus field needs\nan explicit policy or simple forwarding will alias a root-bus device.\n\n**PRIMARY SOURCE, and this project did not have it:** codex cited the *Digital 21066/21066A\nHardware Reference Manual* (Table 6-1, §6.4.2, §6.2.18) with page anchors.  No 21066/LCA manual was\nin `_scratchpad/`, and there are several open LCA rows reasoning from source alone.  Fetched at the\ncitation, on the `vmesbcp2.pdf` precedent.\n\n*** AND THEN VERIFIED LOCALLY RATHER THAN TRUSTED, which is what the carrier's rule demands and\nwhich this project has been burned by skipping: `#357`/`#362`/`#365` shipped DDI 0100I page numbers\nthat came from panel output rather than a manual. ***  `_scratchpad/21066_66a_hrm.txt`, 713 KB /\n13,876 lines:\n\n    :8840  S6.2.6   \"CPU physical address bits <31:7> are used for PCI address bits <26:2>\"\n                    -> THE SHIFT OF 5 that `relative_addr >> 5` implements.  CONFIRMED.\n    :9216  S6.2.9   \"PCI address bits ad<1:0> come directly from configuration cycle type\n                     register (IOC_CONF, Section 6.4.2) bits <1:0>\"\n                    -> IOC_CONF selects the CYCLE TYPE and does NOT supply the bus.  CONFIRMED.\n    :9486  S6.2.18  \"the IOC returns arbitrary data with error status to the CPU, which causes\n                     an instruction exception\" + internal interrupt + ERR/CODE=1/CMD/ADDR/LOST\n                    -> absent-device reads are NOT all-ones on real hardware.  CONFIRMED.\n\n**One narrowing, stated because a wrong reason gets reused:** codex wrote *\"bits 23..2 from CPU\naddress bits 28..7\"*.  The manual's general sparse rule is the WIDER `<31:7> -> <26:2>` with the\nsame shift; codex's range is the PCI **type-1 config format** (bus 23..16, dev 15..11, fn 10..8,\nreg 7..2), which S6.2.9 defers to explicitly -- *\"restricted by the encoding requirements of PCI\nconfiguration cycles as described in the PCI Local Bus [Specification]\"*.  **Same shift, narrower\nwindow, and the narrowing is correct for config cycles** -- so `bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff` and\n`dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` rest on the PCI spec's field layout, not on this manual, and the round\nshould say so rather than citing the LCA manual for the field positions.\n\n*** A TRAP FOR THE NEXT READER: `file` REPORTS THIS PDF AS \"10 page(s)\" AND IT IS WRONG. ***  The\nreal count lives in a linearized trailer. Caught before committing, by grepping this note instead of trusting my memory of it. ***\n\nI read `_guest-src-openbsd22/sys/arch/alpha/pci/lca_pci.c` and wrote up four findings as new: that\n`lca_conf_read` writes `REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF) = 0x01` on the secondary-bus path; that `data` is\ninitialised to `(pcireg_t)-1` and overwritten only if `badaddr()` succeeds, so absence normalises to\nall-ones at ANY offset; that the primary-bus tag frob places an IDSEL bit at `1 << (device + 11)`;\nand the decomposition itself.  **Counted in this very note before adding them: `REGVAL` 4, `IDSEL`\n7, `device + 11` 4, `badaddr` 2, `pcireg_t)-1` 1.**  All four were already recorded, several in the\nsame words.\n\n**THE CAUSE IS EXACT AND IT IS THE SECOND TIME TODAY: I READ PART OF A RECORD AND ACTED.**  I\nqueried this row's `state_note` and looked at the first fifteen hundred characters, which covered\nthe defect and the shipped witness but stopped short of the guest-source section. GATE MOVED, AND THE MOVE IS THE POINT: the old detector lived in gate_offline because source text needs no binary. The replacement needs build/gxemul and gdb, and gate_offline RUNS NO EMULATOR AT ALL (zero references to the binary) -- adding one would have made the gate's own name untrue. It went to gate 9, which already constructs every machine and subtype: ASan is that gate's instrument for the memory-safety class, this probe is its instrument for the VALUE class ASan cannot see. check_probe_wiring.py followed the move with no edit, because it DERIVES wiring from run.sh's GATES array rather than asserting it. Nothing was left behind in gate_offline: a stub row asserting the probe merely EXISTS cannot fail for the reason it names. degrade() not gate_skip() on a missing gdb -- gate_skip EXITS and would have discarded the sweep's own eleven checks and reported the whole gate as SKIP. THREE WRONG RECORDS FOUND IN MY OWN MATERIAL, all corrected. (1) The `:633` citation had drifted to :661 -- the read-the-line-before-citing-it rule. (2) The added source comment said "machine.c forces machine->bootarg non-NULL"; machine.c:559 is a DEBUG PRINT whose local has_bootarg only selects which message is emitted, and bootarg is not on this path at all. The CONCLUSION (only defective caller) survives on the two-caller grep, which agy and grok both performed independently; the argument for it did not. (3) "callee-side hardening is an IMPOSSIBILITY" overreached: impossible AT THAT SIGNATURE, not categorically. *** THE UBSAN FOLLOW-UP UNDERCOUNTED ITSELF BY FIVE TIMES. *** It was filed as "two left-shift defects at arcbios.c:737/784". The measuring seat found ip32 never reaches those lines -- its two hits are :570 and :590 -- making it four sites. A grep for the idiom then found TEN unguarded `(buf[3]<<24)` sites and FOUR correctly cast `((uint32_t)buf[3]<<24)`. Line 580 sits BETWEEN 570 and 590 and is the only one of the three that is cast: the author knew the idiom and applied it to 4 of 14 sites. :737 is the damaging one -- the sign-extended low word is ADDED to high-word terms, so the 64-bit pointer's high word comes out off by one for every address with bit 31 set. A SEAT'S FACTUAL CHALLENGE WAS REFUTED BY EXECUTION WHILE ITS MECHANISM CLAIM WAS SIMULTANEOUSLY RIGHT. grok read the arms and reported ip27 has no abort(), so "five call abort()" overstates. MEASURED over all ten advertised subtypes: ip19/20/22/24/27 return 134 and ip12/28/30/32/35 return 1 -- the five ARE the five. But ip22 has no body and FALLS THROUGH into case 24, and ip27 dies under gdb at interrupt_handler_lookup() <- devinit_z8530() <- device_add(), on its own arm's irq=0 -- which is exactly what grok predicted. A token search of a switch cannot see fall-through, and reading for abort() misses an abort raised by a callee; my own case-splitter had the identical blind spot. kimi audited the kill-table ARITHMETIC rather than the code and challenged "four-octet MAC 6/7 (E5 alone)" as inconsistent. MEASURED: format-only mutant 6/7 with E5 the sole failure; format-AND-arguments mutant 5/7 with E5 and E6. Both numbers right, different mutants -- and the real defect it exposed is that "(E5 alone)" never says whether E5 alone FIRED or alone SURVIVED, and the two readings are opposite. A kill-table cell must name the direction. STILL OPEN AND DECLARED: the ip32 arm's own snprintf is now redundant; kept, because deleting it drops the buffer to a single write site and costs nothing to leave. Nothing here claims anything about the bytes the GUEST reads back -- that needs a rung-3 probe on a machine that gets that far, and there is no SGI rig. The probe reads arguments from SysV AMD64 registers because build/gxemul has no DWARF; that ties it to one ABI, stated in the file rather than hidden, with V6/V7 as the rows that would catch a wrong register rather than let it read as zero. Gates: gate_offline PASS (263), gate_asan_sweep PASS (13, was 11), each run singly.
