VERDICT: CONFIRMED, AND THE PREVIOUS SEAT'S NUMBERS WERE RIGHT.  OpenBSD 7.6
issues exactly TWELVE byte writes to paddr 0x1400002c and ZERO reads, reproduced
on four independent runs.  Six carry 0x08 (WDCTL_4BIT), five carry 0x02
(WDCTL_IDS), and the FIRST carries 0x0c = WDCTL_RST | WDCTL_4BIT -- so a real ATA
SOFT RESET is among them and is silently discarded today.  The command block at
0x14000000..0x1400000f SUCCEEDS: 12,718 accesses across all eight registers, and
wd0 attaches with correct geometry, so devinit->addr is RIGHT and the defect is a
missing offset case, not a wrong base.  Nothing else in the constructed machine
claims 0x1400002c; the registered wdc_altstatus window at 0x14000206 receives
ZERO accesses for the entire boot.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q1.  IS IT TWELVE WRITES, AND ARE SIX OF THEM 0x08?      YES to both.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Four runs, two independent instruments, all agreeing:

  A1_pristine_boot.log         12 writes   0 reads     (unmodified build)
  A2_pristine_boot.log         12 writes   0 reads     (unmodified build)
  A3_pristine_boot.log         12 writes   0 reads     (unmodified build)
  B1_hook_full_window.log      12 writes   0 reads     (census-hook build)

All twelve are len=1, all from pc=0x8c15f6e8 <obio_iomem_write_1+0x8>, all with
no_exceptions=0 (real guest stores, not debugger reads).

  *** THE COUNT WAS TAKEN AS OCCURRENCES, NOT LINES, ON PURPOSE.  The emulator's
  warning interleaves with the guest console stream mid-word -- the transcript
  literally reads "wd0 at wdc0 chan[ memory WRITE ... ]nel 0 drive 0".  In these
  four runs both forms happened to give 12, so this is a precaution and NOT a
  measured correction; tally_runs.sh prints both so a divergence would show. ***

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q2.  WHAT ARE THE ACTUAL VALUES?   0x0c once, then 0x08/0x02 alternating.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
In order (bit names from the tree's own src/include/thirdparty/wdcreg.h --
WDCTL_4BIT 0x08, WDCTL_RST 0x04, WDCTL_IDS 0x02):

   1  0x0c   WDCTL_4BIT | WDCTL_RST      <-- SRST ASSERTED
   2  0x08   WDCTL_4BIT                  <-- SRST released
   3  0x02   WDCTL_IDS
   4  0x08   WDCTL_4BIT
   5  0x02   WDCTL_IDS
   6  0x08   WDCTL_4BIT
   7  0x02   WDCTL_IDS
   8  0x08   WDCTL_4BIT
   9  0x02   WDCTL_IDS
  10  0x08   WDCTL_4BIT
  11  0x02   WDCTL_IDS
  12  0x08   WDCTL_4BIT

  histogram:  0x0c x1   0x08 x6   0x02 x5      carrying WDCTL_RST: 1

Shape: one assert/release soft-reset pair at the head of the channel probe, then
five disable-interrupts / enable-interrupts pairs bracketing PIO commands.
Window context around the 0x0c (from the hook log):

      W  sdh              0xb0     (select drive 1)
      R  status           0x01
      W  sdh              0xa0     (select drive 0)
      W  UNMAPPED +0x2c   0x0c     <<< SRST asserted, DISCARDED
      W  UNMAPPED +0x2c   0x08     <<< SRST released, DISCARDED
      W  sdh              0xa0
      R  status           0x51

CORROBORATED ON THE UNMODIFIED BINARY, with no instrumentation of any kind, by
the tree's own committed write-watchpoint (#250, the debugger watchpoint
command).  It reported paddr 0x1400002c, len 1, value 0x0c first and then the
same {0x08, 0x02} set, from the same pc.  See A4_pristine_watchpoint.log.

  *** THE WATCHPOINT RUN SAW 9 OF THE 12, AND THAT IS A PROPERTY OF THE METHOD,
  NOT A DISAGREEMENT.  The check in MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE is guarded by
  !single_step && !about_to_enter_single_step, and the hit itself SETS
  about_to_enter_single_step -- so a write arriving before the debugger has been
  entered and resumed is not reported.  It is a LOWER BOUND by construction.  It
  confirms the VALUES; the COUNT of 12 rests on the other two instruments. ***

WHAT MAPPING THE REGISTER WOULD THEN DO (read from DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus)):
  * on the 0x0c write, idata & WDCTL_RST is true, so the handler sets
    d->cur_command = COMMAND_RESET and calls wdc_new_command_reset(), which
    clears atapi_cmd_in_progress, atapi_phase and write_in_progress and purges
    the inbuf ring (inbuf_head = inbuf_tail = 0).  The guard is LEVEL-triggered
    by deliberate #481 design.
  * the six 0x08 and five 0x02 writes reach no WDCTL_RST branch; they would
    produce only a debug() line, invisible without -v.
  * reads would return status_byte(), which is PURE (verified by reading it: it
    derives from diskimage_exist, the ring pointers, write_in_progress, d->error
    and the atapi phase, and assigns nothing).  Today they return 0 instead --
    but the guest performs ZERO reads there in this boot, so that half is not
    observable on this rig.
  * cur_command = COMMAND_RESET (0x100) is a LATCH.  Its only two consumers are
    the cyl_lo and cyl_hi read arms, and BOTH are gated on
    diskimage_is_a_cdrom(), so on this IDE-disk rig they do not fire.

  NOT SETTLED: whether delivering the reset changes the boot outcome, and what a
  CD-ROM rig (-d ic:) would do -- both consumers of the latch are cdrom-gated and
  this census used a plain IDE disk.  Those are fix-side measurements the round
  still owes; this census does not answer them.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q3.  DO THE COMMAND-BLOCK ACCESSES SUCCEED?    YES -- decisively.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THIS IS THE DISCRIMINATING CONTROL AND IT COMES OUT ON THE "MISSING OFFSET CASE"
SIDE.  Full window census from B1_hook_full_window.log (landisk uses addr_mult=2,
so device register N sits at paddr 0x14000000 + 2N):

  paddr        wdc register           dir  len  count
  0x14000000   0 data                 R    2    2560
  0x14000002   1 error/features       R    1       6
  0x14000002   1 error/features       W    1      12
  0x14000004   2 seccnt               R    1       2
  0x14000004   2 seccnt               W    1      13
  0x14000006   3 sector               R    1       2
  0x14000006   3 sector               W    1      12
  0x14000008   4 cyl_lo               R    1       3
  0x14000008   4 cyl_lo               W    1      13
  0x1400000a   5 cyl_hi               R    1       2
  0x1400000a   5 cyl_hi               W    1      13
  0x1400000c   6 sdh                  W    1      33
  0x1400000e   7 status/command       R    1   10033
  0x1400000e   7 status/command       W    1      14
  0x1400002c   *** NOT MAPPED ***     W    1      12
                                              -------
                                               12730   (12718 mapped + 12 not)

Nothing at all in 0x14000010..0x1400002b or 0x1400002d..0x1400003f.

DEVICE-SIGNATURE CONTROL (the value could only have come from the emulated wdc,
not from RAM): the guest reads back the IDENTIFY data and prints

    wd0 at wdc0 channel 0 drive 0: <wdc-scratch-64m.img>
    wd0: 128-sector PIO, CHS, 64MB, 131 cyl, 16 head, 63 sec, 132048 sectors

-- the model string is the host image's filename and the geometry is the one the
emulator computed for it.  RAM cannot produce that.

SECOND, INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION: the pristine build run with -v prints the
device's own DATA-register debug line, and it counts 2560 "wdc: read from DATA"
-- byte-for-byte the same number as the hook's 2560 reads at 0x14000000.  Two
instruments, one modified and one not, agreeing exactly.  Also from that run:
0 "wdc: WARNING" of any kind (no flood), and 0 accesses to ALTSTATUS.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q4.  DOES ANYTHING ELSE CLAIM 0x1400002c?      NO.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Answered on the CONSTRUCTED machine, not just by grep, via the debugger's
device-list command (A5_device_list.log).  Complete map:

   0:            ram @ 0x0c000000  len 0x4000000
   1:            wdc @ 0x14000000  len 0x10        <-- 0x14000000..0x1400000f
   2:  wdc_altstatus @ 0x14000206  len 0x2         <-- 0x14000206..0x14000207
   3:            ram @ 0x1e000000  len 0x8000
   4:         sh4_sq @ 0xe0000000  len 0x4000000
   5..12:  ram / sh4_itlb_aa / sh4_itlb_da1 / sh4_utlb_aa / sh4_utlb_da1
  13:       sh4_pcic @ 0xfe200000  len 0x224
  14:            sh4 @ 0xff000000  len 0x1000000
  15:        rs5c313 @ 0x110000000 len 0x10

0x1400002c falls in no entry.  The command block ends 0x1c bytes below it and
wdc_altstatus begins 0x1da bytes above it.  Static grep agrees: the only source
reference to this range is machine_landisk.c's single device_add of
"wdc ... addr_mult=2 addr=0x14000000", and DEVINIT(wdc) has cases for MACPPC,
HPCMIPS and IQ80321 only.

AND THE REGISTERED WINDOW IS DEAD: the -v run counts ZERO "read from ALTSTATUS"
and ZERO "write to ALT. CTRL" for the whole boot.  So today the handler that
exists is never reached, and the twelve writes that should reach it are discarded
0x1da bytes lower down.

================================================================================
A TRAP WORTH RECORDING -- IT PRODUCED A CLEAN, CONFIDENT, HALF-EMPTY CENSUS
================================================================================
The first version of the census hook tested its window on paddr at do_return_ok.
It reported 12 unmapped writes and ZERO command-block accesses, with no error and
no missing denominator -- and that was wrong.  The device arm of
MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE does "paddr -= mem->devices[i].baseaddr" BEFORE calling the
handler, so a device access reaches do_return_ok carrying the RELATIVE offset
(0x00..0x0f), never 0x14000000+.  A window predicate written on paddr is
therefore blind to exactly the mapped half -- which is the half that answers Q3.
The fix is the GXC_P0 latch, taken immediately after address translation.

*** THIS IS PRECISELY HOW A REPORT COULD COME BACK WITH THE UNMAPPED WRITES AND
NO COMMAND-BLOCK CONTROL AT ALL.  A censusing instrument must be shown to see the
POSITIVE case before its negatives are believed; here the 2560/2560 DATA
agreement against the tree's own -v output is that proof. ***

================================================================================
METHOD, AND HOW TO RE-RUN IT
================================================================================
Two builds, neither of them GXEMUL-SEC, neither of them ../build/, and neither
under /tmp/gxwit* (another seat's trees):

  BUILD A  /tmp/gxcensusA   pristine copy of GXEMUL-SEC.  Verified with cmp
                            against SEC file-by-file and [ binary -nt source ]
                            after the build (logs/buildA.log,
                            verify_buildA.sh).  Every number attributed to
                            "pristine" comes from this.
  BUILD B  /tmp/gxcensusB   same tree + a census hook in memory_rw.c ONLY.
                            The complete diff is buildB_census_hook.diff (3
                            hunks); MAKE_RC=0 and FRESH_OK are both recorded in
                            logs/buildB.log.

The emulator is run on the UNMODIFIED -E landisk machine description, no
device_add, no address overrides:

  gxemul -E landisk -M 64 -d iR:_images/wdc-scratch-64m.img \
         _images/openbsd76-landisk-bsd.rd

iR: = IDE, read-only with a pid-unique discard overlay, so runs cannot inherit
state.  Each run boots to the OpenBSD installer prompt ("(I)nstall, (U)pgrade,
(A)utoinstall or (S)hell?") and is then killed; that is the whole probe/attach
phase, and no install is performed.  SCOPE OF EVERY COUNT HERE IS THAT WINDOW.

  run_boot.sh        one boot, output captured (no pty needed; the SCIF console
                     works on a plain pipe)
  census.py          tallies non-existant accesses by paddr, with denominators
  analyse_hook.py    the build-B window census, values and bit decomposition
  tally_runs.sh      cross-run agreement table
  tally_verbose.sh   the -v instrument counts
  watch_2c.py        pristine-build watchpoint capture (pty plumbing is
                     gxprobe.Emu's; install_stubs() is overridden away because
                     that layer is MIPS-specific and this rig is SH4)
  devlist.py         device-list dump on the constructed machine
  verify_buildA.sh   freshness + cmp-against-SEC for build A
  mk_censusB.py      generates the build-B hook (refuses to patch on a
                     non-unique anchor)
  collect.sh         copies every log here

WITNESS LADDER: rung 4 (boot).  A real OpenBSD 7.6 driver reaches the path
unaided on a committed, unmodified machine description, with a liveness control
(the command block answers and the guest completes autoconf) and a device-
signature control (the IDENTIFY data carries the host image's name and geometry,
which RAM cannot produce).

NOT SETTLED, stated rather than guessed:
  * whether delivering the reset changes the boot outcome (a fix-side
    measurement, deliberately not taken here);
  * CD-ROM rig behaviour, where COMMAND_RESET's two cdrom-gated consumers would
    fire;
  * anything past the installer prompt -- no install, no post-attach I/O;
  * other landisk models (machine_landisk.c's own TODO 2) and other guest OS
    versions.  Everything above is OpenBSD 7.6 RAMDISK #70 only.


================================================================================
*** CORRECTION APPENDED 2026-08-28.  THE TWO-INSTRUMENT VALIDATION CLAIM ABOVE
*** IS NOT RECONSTRUCTIBLE FROM THE PRESERVED LOGS.  THE CONCLUSIONS SURVIVE;
*** THE STATED PROOF DOES NOT.
================================================================================

Found by the opus seat on this row's pass-1 panel, and re-derived here before
recording.

This file rests its credibility on one methodological sentence -- "a censusing
instrument must be shown to see the POSITIVE case before its negatives are
believed; here the 2560/2560 DATA agreement against the tree's own -v output is
that proof."  Measured against the logs preserved beside it:

    logs/B1_hook_full_window.log        "read from DATA"  ->     0
                                        non-existant ...2c ->   12
    logs/A6_pristine_verbose_TRIMMED.log "read from DATA" ->    24
                                        non-existant ...2c ->    6

The hook's 2560 appears only in THIS FILE'S OWN TABLE, not as a grep-able count
in B1; and the -v instrument the claim compares it against shows 24, not 2560.
**A6 IS A SHORT RUN -- its own filename says TRIMMED -- and it is the only -v
artifact preserved.**  So the "two independent instruments agreed" proof cannot
be re-run by a later reader.  The -v run also shows 3 rather than 5 at
0x10000003, for the same reason.

WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS ENOUGH FOR EVERY CONCLUSION THIS FILE DRAWS: B1's
12,718 mapped accesses across all eight command-block registers, and the
`wd0 at wdc0 ... 131 cyl, 16 head, 63 sec` attach visible in A1, discharge the
positive-case obligation ON THEIR OWN.  The instrument demonstrably sees the
device.  Nothing in the twelve-write histogram depends on the 2560 comparison.

*** WHY THIS IS RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DELETED: this row's panel gate was
*** "ship, but not on this packet" PRECISELY BECAUSE the previous warrant rested
*** on an unpreserved artifact.  The re-take preserved everything -- and then the
*** write-up described something the preserved artifact does not show.  That is
*** the same class one step further in: the evidence is there and does not say
*** what the prose says.  Preservation is necessary and not sufficient. ***

OWED, either one: re-take the -v instrument on the disk rig TO COMPLETION and
preserve it, or leave this correction standing as the caption.  Do not let a
round block cite the 2560 agreement until one of those is done.

ALSO CORRECTED: this file reports 0x1400002c as the unmapped site.  It is one of
FIVE per boot, and the other four are real queue items -- 0xfe080000 (5/boot) and
0xfe080040 (1/boot), SH7751R extended-INTC registers reached from intc_init and
intpri_intr_priority; and 0x10000003 (5/boot, rtc_ce) and 0x10000005 (3/boot,
intr_init / extintr_establish), landisk board registers.  census.py PRINTS an
"ALL paddrs" section; this write-up presented only the 0x14000000..0x14000040
window.  Consequence worth stating: fixing 0x1400002c does NOT make `-T` usable
on a landisk boot -- four other sites still halt it -- so any `-T`-based row is a
PROBE row, not a boot row.
"""wdcaltoffset -- RUNG-3 WITNESS AND DETECTOR.  Real guest instructions, real address
decode, real memory_rw, on an UNMODIFIED in-tree machine description (evbmips -e malta).

THE DEFECT.  DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus) in src/devices/dev_wdc.c reads relative_addr
ZERO times, while dev_wdc_init registers its region TWO BYTES WIDE.  The durable keys are
grep anchors, not line numbers:

    grep -n 'DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus)'   src/devices/dev_wdc.c
    grep -n '"wdc_altstatus",'               src/devices/dev_wdc.c

        memory_device_register(..., "wdc_altstatus", alt_status_addr, 2,
            dev_wdc_altstatus_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

    offset 0  ->  0x3f6  Device Control      (write: nIEN, SRST, 4BIT)
    offset 1  ->  0x3f7  Drive Address Reg   READ-ONLY on real ATA hardware

Both offsets land in the same handler body, so a guest STORE to 0x3f7 is executed as a
store to Device Control.  Since #481 corrected the arm's bit to WDCTL_RST and wired in
wdc_new_command_reset(), that store now performs a full soft reset: write_in_progress is
cleared and the input ring is purged.  A write to a register the guest is not even
allowed to write aborts a live ATA WRITE SECTORS and destroys its staged bytes.

THE MACHINE IS NOT MODIFIED AND NO DEVICE IS ADDED.  malta carries a wdc through its own
committed path: machine_evbmips.c bus_pci_add(..., "piix4_ide") -> bus_pci.c
PCIINIT(piix4_ide), which does device_add(machine, "wdc addr=<isa_portbase+0x1f0> ...")
whenever an IDE image is attached; isa_portbase = 0x18000000.  Attaching a disk image is
ordinary operation, not a machine edit.

    wdc registers      physical 0x180001f0 + N  ->  KSEG1 0xffffffffb80001f0 + N
    wdc_altstatus      physical 0x180003f6      ->  KSEG1 0xffffffffb80003f6
    the read-only reg  physical 0x180003f7      ->  KSEG1 0xffffffffb80003f7

THE SHAPE OF THE WITNESS -- three phases, IDENTICAL except for the address of one store.

    PHASE 1  poke 0x06 (nIEN|SRST) at 0x3f6, the REAL Device Control register.
             A legitimate soft reset.  BOTH builds must abort the armed write.
             This is the ACCEPT control the de-escalation clause demands: it proves the
             fix leg does not disable the register it is supposed to leave alone, and it
             proves the reset machinery this probe observes is alive.

    PHASE 2  poke the SAME 0x06 at 0x3f7, the READ-ONLY Drive Address Register.
             *** THE DEFECT.  Committed build: identical to phase 1.  Fix leg: nothing
             happens. ***

    PHASE 3  poke 0x02 (nIEN alone, WDCTL_RST clear) at 0x3f7.
             Neither build resets.  This is the discriminator that says the defect is
             the RESET BIT reaching a register that has none, rather than "any store to
             0x3f7 does something".

PHASE 1 AND PHASE 2 ARE THE ANCHOR (vacuity-floor clause (f)).  They are the same
stimulus at ADJACENT addresses, so the pair pins WHERE the store lands, not merely that
two addresses behave differently.  On the committed build the two phases are
indistinguishable -- which IS the defect, stated as a measurement.

THE TWO CONTROLS THE WITNESS LADDER DEMANDS FOR AN ACCESS WITNESS:

    LIVENESS          three of them.
                      (a) RAM: the same sb/lbu instruction shapes round-trip 0x55
                          through plain RAM, so a device row reading 0x00 could not be
                          blamed on a bad encoding.
                      (b) DEVICE REGISTER: 0x55 -> cyl_lo and 0x66 -> cyl_hi read back
                          as 0x6655 through the wdc's own decode.  This is #481's
                          preserved liveness=0x6655 idiom, kept deliberately so this
                          witness and that one are comparable.
                      (c) per phase: the ARMED status read, which must show DRQ before
                          the poke or the phase measured nothing.

    DEVICE SIGNATURE  two of them.
                      (a) FIFO: push 0x5a then 0xa5 to the data port and read the SAME
                          address twice.  A memory cell returns 0xa5 both times; only a
                          FIFO returns them in push order.
                      (b) STATUS: the byte at +7 is COMPUTED by status_byte() out of
                          diskimage_exist(), d->error and d->write_in_progress.  The
                          guest never wrote it.  RAM would answer with what was stored.

WHY THIS IS ALSO A DETECTOR, NOT ONLY A WITNESS.  Every row carries a want for BOTH
legs.  Phase 2's two rows are the only ones whose want DIFFERS between the legs; every
other row is an identity row that must hold on both.  So the file fails on the committed
build, passes once the handler decodes its offset, and still fails if a future fix
over-reaches: a build that refuses offset 0 as well reds PHASE 1, and a build that keeps
the decode but nulls its effect reds PHASE 2 again.

BYTE ORDER.  evbmips -e malta is LITTLE-endian.  Every access here is a single byte, so
byte order cannot silently change what a row means -- stated because a sibling detector
in this tree was measured testing the wrong endianness.

Usage:  wdcaltoff_rung3_probe.py <base-binary> <fix-binary> <disk-image> [outdir]

BOTH LEGS RUN INSIDE ONE PYTHON INVOCATION, SO THE INSTRUMENT IS IDENTICAL BY
CONSTRUCTION.  A driver shaped "for t in base fix; do python3 probe.py $t; done" gives
the two legs different instruments if the probe is edited between them, and the
difference lands in the very output being diffed.

NOTHING IN THE REPO IS WRITTEN.  Both trees under measurement are copies under /tmp, and
the disk image is attached with the iR: prefix, whose overlay is pid-unique.
"""
import os
import pty
import re
import select
import sys
import time

BASE_BIN = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "/tmp/gxaltoff/base/gxemul"
FIX_BIN = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "/tmp/gxaltoff/fix/gxemul"
DISK = sys.argv[3] if len(sys.argv) > 3 else "/tmp/gxaltoff/altoff-scratch.img"
OUTDIR = sys.argv[4] if len(sys.argv) > 4 else "/tmp/gxaltoff/run"

BASE = 0xffffffff80100000          # KSEG0, physical 0x00100000, inside malta's 128 MB
RAWBIN = None                      # set in main(), lives under OUTDIR

WDC_HI, WDC_LO = 0xb800, 0x01f0    # wdc register file, KSEG1
ALT_HI, ALT_LO = 0xb800, 0x03f6    # wdc_altstatus region, KSEG1
RAM_HI = 0x8020                    # KSEG0 scratch for the RAM liveness control

#  Register offsets from the wdc base -- src/include/thirdparty/wdcreg.h
R_DATA, R_SECCNT, R_SECTOR = 0, 2, 3
R_CYL_LO, R_CYL_HI, R_SDH = 4, 5, 6
R_STATUS = 7                       # wd_command on write, wd_status on read

#  Status bits -- wdcreg.h
DRDY, DSC, DRQ, ERR = 0x40, 0x10, 0x08, 0x01

#  status_byte() ORs DRDY|DSC because diskimage_exist() is true for the attached IDE
#  image; DRQ iff (inbuf_head != inbuf_tail) OR write_in_progress; ERR iff d->error.
#
#  d->error IS SET AT DEVINIT.  Grep it as "->error" -- dev_wdc_init writes
#  "d->error      = 1;" with the field name padded, so a grep for "d->error = 1" with a
#  SINGLE space MATCHES NOTHING and reports that the field starts at 0.  It did, while
#  this file was being written, and the first run's measured 0x51 against a predicted
#  0x50 is what sent me back to look.  A pattern that matches nothing reports zero for
#  everything, which is this project's oldest apparatus trap -- and here the measurement
#  caught the reading rather than the other way round.
#
#  wdc_command() clears it ("d->error = 0" at the top), so the power-on ERR bit stands
#  only until the first phase issues WDCC_WRITE.
ST_POWERON = DRDY | DSC | ERR      # 0x51  before any command has been issued
ST_IDLE = DRDY | DSC               # 0x50  ring empty, no write armed, error cleared
ST_ARMED = DRDY | DSC | DRQ        # 0x58  write_in_progress set

WDCC_WRITE = 0x30                  # wdcreg.h
WDCTL_RST = 0x04
WDCTL_IDS = 0x02
SRST_POKE = WDCTL_IDS | WDCTL_RST  # 0x06 -- what older OpenBSD asserts; #481's value
NIEN_POKE = WDCTL_IDS              # 0x02 -- no reset bit at all

STAGE = [0x71, 0x72, 0x73, 0x74, 0x75, 0x76, 0x77, 0x78]

zero, v0, v1, a0, a1, a2, a3 = 0, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7
t0, t1, t2, t3 = 8, 9, 10, 11
s0, s1, s2, s3, s4, s5, s6, s7 = 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21, 22, 23
t9 = 25


def I(op, rs, rt, imm):
    return (op << 26) | (rs << 21) | (rt << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)


def lui(rt, imm):
    return I(0x0F, 0, rt, imm)


def ori(rt, rs, imm):
    return I(0x0D, rs, rt, imm)


def addiu(rt, rs, imm):
    return I(0x09, rs, rt, imm)


def sb(rt, off, base):
    return I(0x28, base, rt, off)


def lbu(rt, off, base):
    return I(0x24, base, rt, off)


def beq(rs, rt, off):
    return I(0x04, rs, rt, off)


NOP = 0
NAME = {zero: "zr", v0: "v0", v1: "v1", a0: "a0", a1: "a1", a2: "a2", a3: "a3",
        t0: "t0", t1: "t1", t2: "t2", t3: "t3", s0: "s0", s1: "s1", s2: "s2",
        s3: "s3", s4: "s4", s5: "s5", s6: "s6", s7: "s7", t9: "t9"}

prog = []


def emit(word, mn):
    """mn is checked against the EMULATOR'S OWN disassembly.

    A wrong register field silently yields 0, and 0 is a value several rows below would
    accept by accident -- this project has shipped that mistake into a committed gate.
    """
    prog.append((word, mn))


def e_li(rt, v):
    emit(addiu(rt, zero, v), "addiu %s,zr,%d" % (NAME[rt], v))


def e_sb(rt, off, base):
    emit(sb(rt, off, base), "sb %s,%d(%s)" % (NAME[rt], off, NAME[base]))


def e_lbu(rt, off, base):
    emit(lbu(rt, off, base), "lbu %s,%d(%s)" % (NAME[rt], off, NAME[base]))


def e_store(val, off, base):
    e_li(t3, val)
    e_sb(t3, off, base)


def arm_a_write():
    """Arm WDCC_WRITE and stage STAGE into the input ring.

    wdc_command() -> wdc__write() sets d->write_in_progress = d->cur_command, which is
    what makes status_byte() raise DRQ.  wdc_command() also calls
    wdc_new_command_reset(), so each phase starts from a clean ring on BOTH legs
    regardless of what the previous phase left -- that is what keeps the phases
    independent, and it is why phase 3 measures the same thing on both legs even though
    phase 2 left them in different states.

    len(STAGE) is 8, deliberately NOT a multiple of 512: the sector drain inside
    DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc) fires on "inbuf_len % 512 == 0", so nothing reaches the disk
    image and the staged bytes simply sit in the ring where the reset can destroy them.
    """
    e_store(0xa0, R_SDH, t0)       # drive 0, head 0 (bits 7 and 5 are the obsolete 1s)
    e_store(0x01, R_SECCNT, t0)
    e_store(0x01, R_SECTOR, t0)
    e_store(0x00, R_CYL_LO, t0)
    e_store(0x00, R_CYL_HI, t0)
    e_store(WDCC_WRITE, R_STATUS, t0)
    for b in STAGE:
        e_store(b, R_DATA, t0)


#  ---------------------------------------------------------------- setup
emit(lui(t0, WDC_HI), "lui t0,0xb800")
emit(ori(t0, t0, WDC_LO), "ori t0,t0,0x01f0")
emit(lui(t1, ALT_HI), "lui t1,0xb800")
emit(ori(t1, t1, ALT_LO), "ori t1,t1,0x03f6")
emit(lui(t2, RAM_HI), "lui t2,0x8020")

#  A -- RAM LIVENESS: the same instruction shapes, through plain RAM.
e_store(0x55, 0, t2)
e_lbu(v0, 0, t2)

#  B -- DEVICE-REGISTER LIVENESS: #481's 0x6655, through the wdc's own decode.
e_store(0x55, R_CYL_LO, t0)
e_store(0x66, R_CYL_HI, t0)
e_lbu(v1, R_CYL_LO, t0)
e_lbu(a0, R_CYL_HI, t0)

#  C -- DEVICE SIGNATURE: the data port is a FIFO, not a cell.
e_store(0x5a, R_DATA, t0)
e_store(0xa5, R_DATA, t0)
e_lbu(a1, R_DATA, t0)
e_lbu(a2, R_DATA, t0)

#  D -- DEVICE SIGNATURE: a status byte the guest never wrote.
e_lbu(a3, R_STATUS, t0)

#  ------------------------------------------------- PHASE 1: 0x3f6, a real SRST
arm_a_write()
e_lbu(s0, R_STATUS, t0)            # armed -> DRQ set
e_store(SRST_POKE, 0, t1)          # <<< 0x3f6, the Device Control register
e_lbu(s1, R_STATUS, t0)            # both legs: DRQ CLEAR
e_lbu(s2, R_DATA, t0)              # both legs: 0xff, the ring is empty

#  ------------------------------------- PHASE 2: 0x3f7, THE READ-ONLY REGISTER
arm_a_write()
e_lbu(s3, R_STATUS, t0)            # armed -> DRQ set
e_store(SRST_POKE, 1, t1)          # <<< 0x3f7, the Drive Address Register
e_lbu(s4, R_STATUS, t0)            # base: DRQ CLEAR   fix: DRQ still SET
e_lbu(s5, R_DATA, t0)              # base: 0xff        fix: 0x71, the staged byte

#  ------------------------- PHASE 3: 0x3f7 again, with the RESET BIT NOT SET
arm_a_write()
e_lbu(s6, R_STATUS, t0)            # armed -> DRQ set
e_store(NIEN_POKE, 1, t1)          # <<< 0x3f7 with WDCTL_RST clear
e_lbu(s7, R_STATUS, t0)            # both legs: DRQ still SET
e_lbu(t9, R_DATA, t0)              # both legs: 0x71

HALT = len(prog)
emit(beq(zero, zero, -1), "b 0x")
emit(NOP, "nop")
HALT_ADDR = BASE + HALT * 4

REGS = ("v0", "v1", "a0", "a1", "a2", "a3",
        "s0", "s1", "s2", "s3", "s4", "s5", "s6", "s7", "t9")

FF = 0xff       # wdc_get_inbuf() returns (uint64_t)-1 on an empty ring; lbu takes 0xff

#  label, register, want on the COMMITTED build, want on the FIX leg, kind
ROWS = [
    ("A   liveness   RAM round-trip, same sb/lbu shapes",
     "v0", 0x55, 0x55, "control"),
    ("B   liveness   cyl_lo readback (0x6655 low byte)",
     "v1", 0x55, 0x55, "control"),
    ("B   liveness   cyl_hi readback (0x6655 high byte)",
     "a0", 0x66, 0x66, "control"),
    ("C   devsig     data port 1st read -- a FIFO",
     "a1", 0x5a, 0x5a, "control"),
    ("C   devsig     data port 2nd read -- not a cell",
     "a2", 0xa5, 0xa5, "control"),
    ("D   devsig     status byte computed by the device",
     "a3", ST_POWERON, ST_POWERON, "control"),
    ("P1  liveness   armed for the 0x3f6 phase: DRQ SET",
     "s0", ST_ARMED, ST_ARMED, "control"),
    ("P1  accept     0x3f6 SRST aborts the write: DRQ CLEAR",
     "s1", ST_IDLE, ST_IDLE, "accept"),
    ("P1  accept     0x3f6 SRST purged the ring: 0xff",
     "s2", FF, FF, "accept"),
    ("P2  liveness   armed for the 0x3f7 phase: DRQ SET",
     "s3", ST_ARMED, ST_ARMED, "control"),
    ("P2  *DEFECT*   0x3f7 store aborts the live write",
     "s4", ST_IDLE, ST_ARMED, "defect"),
    ("P2  *DEFECT*   0x3f7 store purged the staged bytes",
     "s5", FF, 0x71, "defect"),
    ("P3  liveness   armed for the nIEN phase: DRQ SET",
     "s6", ST_ARMED, ST_ARMED, "control"),
    ("P3  control    0x3f7 without WDCTL_RST: DRQ still SET",
     "s7", ST_ARMED, ST_ARMED, "control"),
    ("P3  control    0x3f7 without WDCTL_RST: bytes intact",
     "t9", 0x71, 0x71, "control"),
]


class Emu(object):
    def __init__(self, argv, boot_wait=5.0):
        self.buf = bytearray()
        self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
        if self.pid == 0:
            try:
                os.execvp(argv[0], argv)
            finally:
                os._exit(127)
        self.drain(boot_wait)

    def drain(self, seconds):
        end = time.time() + seconds
        while time.time() < end:
            r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], 0.05)
            if self.fd in r:
                try:
                    d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
                except OSError:
                    return
                if not d:
                    return
                self.buf.extend(d)

    def send(self, cmd, wait=0.4):
        start = len(self.buf)
        data = (cmd + "\n").encode()
        view = memoryview(data)
        while len(view):
            n = os.write(self.fd, view)
            view = view[n:]
            if len(view):
                self.drain(0.05)
        self.drain(wait)
        return bytes(self.buf[start:]).decode("latin1", "replace")

    def close(self, log):
        try:
            os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
            self.drain(0.4)
        except OSError:
            pass
        for fn in (lambda: os.close(self.fd),
                   lambda: os.kill(self.pid, 9),
                   lambda: os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)):
            try:
                fn()
            except OSError:
                pass
        with open(log, "w") as f:
            f.write(bytes(self.buf).decode("latin1", "replace"))


def reg(emu, name):
    for _ in range(5):
        out = emu.send("print %s" % name, wait=0.35)
        m = re.findall(r"0x[0-9a-fA-F]+", out)
        if m:
            return int(m[-1], 16)
        emu.drain(0.25)
    return None


def run_one(binary, tag, out):
    argv = [binary, "-V", "-E", "evbmips", "-e", "malta",
            "-d", "iR:" + DISK, "0x%x:%s" % (BASE, RAWBIN)]
    out("  command   " + " ".join(argv))
    emu = Emu(argv)

    #  CONTROL 0 -- every hand-encoded word checked against the emulator's own
    #  disassembler.  Addresses are printed WITHOUT an 0x prefix.  The WORD is the
    #  authoritative check; the mnemonic is a rendering and is counted separately so a
    #  spelling this file guessed wrong cannot masquerade as a bad encoding.
    dis = emu.send("unassemble 0x%x 0x%x" % (BASE, BASE + len(prog) * 4), wait=3.0)
    seen = {}
    for line in dis.splitlines():
        m = re.match(r"^\s*([0-9a-f]{8,16}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+(?:<-)?\s*(.*)$", line)
        if m:
            seen[int(m.group(1), 16) & 0xffffffff] = (m.group(2), m.group(3).strip())

    w_ok = m_ok = 0
    bad = []
    for i, (w, want_mn) in enumerate(prog):
        addr = (BASE + i * 4) & 0xffffffff
        hit = seen.get(addr)
        if hit is None:
            bad.append("  BAD  0x%08x  no disassembly line" % addr)
            continue
        gotw, gotmn = hit
        norm = " ".join(gotmn.replace("\t", " ").split())
        if gotw == "%08x" % w:
            w_ok += 1
        else:
            bad.append("  BAD  0x%08x  word %s vs %08x" % (addr, gotw, w))
        if norm.startswith(" ".join(want_mn.split())):
            m_ok += 1
        else:
            bad.append("  MNEMONIC 0x%08x  got %r want %r" % (addr, norm, want_mn))
    out("  CONTROL 0 encodings: %d of %d WORDS and %d of %d mnemonics agree with the"
        " emulator's own disassembler" % (w_ok, len(prog), m_ok, len(prog)))
    for l in bad:
        out(l)

    pc0 = reg(emu, "pc")
    out("  pc at entry = %s (want 0x%016x)"
        % (("0x%016x" % pc0) if pc0 is not None else "UNREADABLE", BASE))

    emu.send("breakpoint add 0x%x" % HALT_ADDR, wait=0.5)
    t = time.time()
    emu.send("continue", wait=1.0)
    deadline = time.time() + 120
    hit = False
    while time.time() < deadline:
        emu.drain(0.5)
        tail = bytes(emu.buf[-4000:]).decode("latin1", "replace")
        if "BREAKPOINT" in tail.upper():
            hit = True
            break
    elapsed = time.time() - t
    out("  parked at the halt breakpoint: %s   (%.2f s)"
        % ("yes" if hit else
           "*** NO -- the guest never reached 0x%016x ***" % HALT_ADDR, elapsed))

    got = {}
    for nm in REGS:
        v = reg(emu, nm)
        got[nm] = None if v is None else (v & 0xff)

    whole = bytes(emu.buf).decode("latin1", "replace")
    diags = {n: whole.count(n) for n in
             ("write to DATA, but not", "reading too much", "wdc inbuf overrun",
              "wdc inbuf full", "write to ALT. CTRL", "DRIVE ADDRESS")}
    emu.close(os.path.join(OUTDIR, "transcript_%s.log" % tag))
    return got, w_ok, m_ok, hit, diags


def main():
    global RAWBIN
    os.makedirs(OUTDIR, exist_ok=True)
    RAWBIN = os.path.join(OUTDIR, "wdcaltoff.bin")
    with open(RAWBIN, "wb") as f:
        for w, _ in prog:
            f.write(bytes([w & 0xff, (w >> 8) & 0xff,
                           (w >> 16) & 0xff, (w >> 24) & 0xff]))

    lines = []

    def out(s=""):
        lines.append(s)
        print(s)
        sys.stdout.flush()

    out("=" * 94)
    out("wdcaltoffset -- RUNG-3 WITNESS + DETECTOR on unmodified `evbmips -e malta`")
    out("=" * 94)
    out("date      %s" % time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"))
    out("machine   evbmips -e malta   5Kc, LITTLE endian   (machine desc UNMODIFIED)")
    out("wdc       physical 0x180001f0 via bus_pci piix4_ide; KSEG1 0xffffffffb80001f0")
    out("altstat   physical 0x180003f6 len 2; offset 1 = 0x180003f7 = the READ-ONLY")
    out("          Drive Address Register, KSEG1 0xffffffffb80003f7")
    out("program   %d guest instructions at 0x%016x, parks at 0x%016x"
        % (len(prog), BASE, HALT_ADDR))
    out("staged    %d bytes per phase (%s) -- NOT a multiple of 512, so the sector"
        % (len(STAGE), " ".join("%02x" % b for b in STAGE)))
    out("          drain never fires and nothing reaches the disk image")
    out("base bin  %s" % BASE_BIN)
    out("fix bin   %s" % FIX_BIN)
    out()

    out("-" * 94)
    out("LEG 1 -- COMMITTED build (source-identical to GXEMUL-SEC at the named baseline)")
    out("-" * 94)
    b_got, b_w, b_m, b_hit, b_dg = run_one(BASE_BIN, "base", out)
    out()

    out("-" * 94)
    out("LEG 2 -- FIX LEG (the handler decodes relative_addr; 0x3f7 declines the write)")
    out("-" * 94)
    f_got, f_w, f_m, f_hit, f_dg = run_one(FIX_BIN, "fix", out)
    out()

    out("-" * 94)
    out("RESULTS -- every value below was produced by a GUEST LOAD through real decode")
    out("-" * 94)
    out("  %-56s %-4s %-14s %-14s" % ("", "reg", "COMMITTED", "FIX LEG"))
    npass = nfail = 0
    fails = []
    changed = []
    for label, rn, wb, wf, kind in ROWS:
        gb, gf = b_got.get(rn), f_got.get(rn)
        okb, okf = (gb == wb), (gf == wf)
        npass += okb + okf
        nfail += (not okb) + (not okf)
        if not (okb and okf):
            fails.append(label)
        if wb != wf:
            changed.append((label, rn, wb, wf, gb, gf))
        out("  %-56s %-4s want %02x got %-5s want %02x got %-5s  %s"
            % (label, rn, wb, ("%02x" % gb) if gb is not None else "??",
               wf, ("%02x" % gf) if gf is not None else "??",
               "ok" if (okb and okf) else "FAIL"))
    out()
    out("  %d of %d predictions correct across both legs" % (npass, npass + nfail))
    for l in fails:
        out("  FAIL  %s" % l)
    out()

    out("-" * 94)
    out("THE DIFFERENTIAL -- the rows the defect owns.  Same script, opposite colour.")
    out("-" * 94)
    for label, rn, wb, wf, gb, gf in changed:
        out("  %-56s %-4s  %02x -> %02x   %s"
            % (label, rn, gb if gb is not None else 0, gf if gf is not None else 0,
               "AS PREDICTED" if (gb == wb and gf == wf) else "MISMATCH"))
    out()

    def hx(d, k):
        return ("%02x" % d[k]) if d.get(k) is not None else "??"

    out("  On the COMMITTED build phase 1 and phase 2 are BYTE-IDENTICAL:")
    out("    0x3f6 -> status %s, data %s   (the Device Control register)"
        % (hx(b_got, "s1"), hx(b_got, "s2")))
    out("    0x3f7 -> status %s, data %s   <-- a register the guest may not write"
        % (hx(b_got, "s4"), hx(b_got, "s5")))
    out("  On the FIX LEG they separate:")
    out("    0x3f6 -> status %s, data %s   (UNCHANGED -- the accept row)"
        % (hx(f_got, "s1"), hx(f_got, "s2")))
    out("    0x3f7 -> status %s, data %s   (the write is declined)"
        % (hx(f_got, "s4"), hx(f_got, "s5")))
    out()

    out("-" * 94)
    out("WHY THIS IS THE DEVICE AND NOT RAM  (device-signature controls)")
    out("-" * 94)
    out("  The data port was read TWICE at the SAME address and returned 0x%s then 0x%s."
        % (hx(b_got, "a1"), hx(b_got, "a2")))
    out("  A memory cell returns the last value written (0xa5) both times; only a FIFO")
    out("  returns them in push order.  This is the wdc input ring, not a memory word.")
    out("  The status byte at +7 read 0x%s at power-on and 0x%s armed.  DRDY|DSC = 0x50"
        % (hx(b_got, "a3"), hx(b_got, "s0")))
    out("  is computed by status_byte() out of diskimage_exist(), the ERR bit out of the")
    out("  d->error that dev_wdc_init sets, and DRQ out of d->write_in_progress.  The")
    out("  guest wrote none of those three, and RAM would return what was stored.")
    out("  RAM liveness (0x%s) and register liveness (0x%s%s -- #481's 0x6655 idiom)"
        % (hx(b_got, "v0"), hx(b_got, "a0"), hx(b_got, "v1")))
    out("  prove the sb/lbu shapes and the wdc decode both work, so a device row")
    out("  reading 0x00 could not have been blamed on a bad encoding.")
    out()

    out("-" * 94)
    out("EMULATOR DIAGNOSTICS DURING EACH RUN -- INFORMATIONAL, NOT EVIDENCE")
    out("-" * 94)
    out("  debug() RETURNS SILENTLY WHEN FREE-RUNNING at default verbosity (it subtracts")
    out("  1 from verbose while emul_executing and returns on v < 0), and this probe")
    out("  breakpoints and continues rather than stepping.  So every debug()-only line")
    out("  below reads 0 on BOTH legs and a zero here means nothing; only fatal() lines")
    out("  can appear.  The rows above are the measurement.")
    for n in sorted(set(list(b_dg) + list(f_dg))):
        out("  %-28s committed x%d   fix x%d" % (n, b_dg.get(n, 0), f_dg.get(n, 0)))
    out()
    out("  transcripts: %s/transcript_base.log , transcript_fix.log" % OUTDIR)
    out("  raw guest program: %s (%d bytes)" % (RAWBIN, 4 * len(prog)))
    out()

    ok = (nfail == 0 and b_hit and f_hit
          and b_w == len(prog) and f_w == len(prog))
    out("VERDICT   %s"
        % ("WITNESS CONFIRMED -- the committed build treats 0x3f7 as 0x3f6 and the fix"
           " leg separates them" if ok else
           "*** SOMETHING DID NOT HOLD -- read the FAIL lines above ***"))
    out("          %d of %d row predictions; encodings %d/%d and %d/%d; both legs"
        " parked: %s" % (npass, npass + nfail, b_w, len(prog), f_w, len(prog),
                         b_hit and f_hit))
    out("ALTOFF_DONE")

    with open(os.path.join(OUTDIR, "RESULTS.txt"), "w") as f:
        f.write("\n".join(lines) + "\n")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    0x3f7 -> status 58, data 71   (the write is declined)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY THIS IS THE DEVICE AND NOT RAM  (device-signature controls)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The data port was read TWICE at the SAME address and returned 0x5a then 0xa5.
  A memory cell returns the last value written (0xa5) both times; only a FIFO
  returns them in push order.  This is the wdc input ring, not a memory word.
  The status byte at +7 read 0x51 at power-on and 0x58 armed.  DRDY|DSC = 0x50
  is computed by status_byte() out of diskimage_exist(), the ERR bit out of the
  d->error that dev_wdc_init sets, and DRQ out of d->write_in_progress.  The
  guest wrote none of those three, and RAM would return what was stored.
  RAM liveness (0x55) and register liveness (0x6655 -- #481's 0x6655 idiom)
  prove the sb/lbu shapes and the wdc decode both work, so a device row
  reading 0x00 could not have been blamed on a bad encoding.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  debug() RETURNS SILENTLY WHEN FREE-RUNNING at default verbosity (it subtracts
  1 from verbose while emul_executing and returns on v < 0), and this probe
  breakpoints and continues rather than stepping.  So every debug()-only line
  below reads 0 on BOTH legs and a zero here means nothing; only fatal() lines
  can appear.  The rows above are the measurement.

  wdc inbuf full               committed x0   fix x0

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""census.py <logfile> -- tally 'non-existant' accesses by paddr.

COUNTS OCCURRENCES, NOT LINES, deliberately.  The emulator's warning lines
interleave with the guest console stream -- a warning is emitted mid-word,
"wd0 at wdc0 chan[ memory WRITE ... ]nel 0 drive 0" -- so a line-based count is
not obviously safe here.

HONEST SCOPE: in the four runs taken for this census the two counts AGREED at
12 (no log line carried two warnings), so the occurrence form is a structural
precaution and NOT a measured correction.  tally_runs.sh prints both forms, so
any future divergence shows up instead of being assumed either way.
"""
import re, sys, collections

pat = re.compile(
    r"\[ memory (READ|WRITE): (?:from|to) non-existant "
    r"paddr=0x([0-9a-f]+) len=(\d+) pc=0x([0-9a-f]+)(?: <([^>]*)>)? \]")

log = open(sys.argv[1], "rb").read().decode("latin-1")
hits = pat.findall(log)

by_addr = collections.Counter()
detail = collections.Counter()
for d, paddr, ln, pc, sym in hits:
    by_addr[int(paddr, 16)] += 1
    detail[(int(paddr, 16), d, int(ln), pc, sym)] += 1

print("TOTAL non-existant accesses in log: %d" % len(hits))
print()
print("--- ALL paddrs ---")
for a in sorted(by_addr):
    print("  0x%08x  %d" % (a, by_addr[a]))
print()
WLO, WHI = 0x14000000, 0x14000040
print("--- WINDOW 0x%08x .. 0x%08x (unmapped only; mapped accesses print nothing) ---"
      % (WLO, WHI))
win = [a for a in sorted(by_addr) if WLO <= a <= WHI]
if not win:
    print("  (none)")
for a in win:
    print("  0x%08x  %d" % (a, by_addr[a]))
print()
print("--- window detail (paddr, dir, len, pc, symbol) x count ---")
for k in sorted(detail):
    if WLO <= k[0] <= WHI:
        print("  0x%08x %-5s len=%d pc=0x%s <%s>  x%d" %
              (k[0], k[1], k[2], k[3], k[4], detail[k]))
#  denominators, so a zero is distinguishable from a broken pattern
print()
print("DENOMINATORS: log bytes=%d, 'non-existant' substring occurrences=%d, regex hits=%d"
      % (len(log), log.count("non-existant"), len(hits)))
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""analyse_hook.py <build-B log> -- the 0x14000000..0x14000040 window census.

Register numbering: landisk registers the wdc with addr_mult=2, so device
register N sits at paddr 0x14000000 + 2N (machine_landisk.c; DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc)
does relative_addr /= d->addr_mult).
"""
import re, sys, collections

REG = {0x00: "0 data", 0x02: "1 error/features", 0x04: "2 seccnt",
       0x06: "3 sector", 0x08: "4 cyl_lo", 0x0a: "5 cyl_hi",
       0x0c: "6 sdh", 0x0e: "7 status/command"}
CTL = {0x08: "WDCTL_4BIT", 0x04: "WDCTL_RST", 0x02: "WDCTL_IDS"}

pat = re.compile(r"GXCENSUS ([RW]) paddr=0x([0-9a-f]+) len=(\d+) nx=(\d+) "
                 r"val=0x([0-9a-f]+) pc=0x([0-9a-f]+)")
log = open(sys.argv[1], "rb").read().decode("latin-1")
hits = pat.findall(log)
print("DENOMINATORS: log bytes=%d, 'GXCENSUS' occurrences=%d, regex hits=%d"
      % (len(log), log.count("GXCENSUS"), len(hits)))
print()

tally = collections.Counter()
nx = collections.Counter()
for d, pa, ln, n, val, pc in hits:
    tally[(int(pa, 16), d, int(ln))] += 1
    nx[int(n)] += 1

print("--- WINDOW CENSUS 0x14000000..0x1400003f ---")
print("%-12s %-20s %-3s %-4s %s" % ("paddr", "wdc register", "dir", "len", "count"))
for (pa, d, ln) in sorted(tally):
    off = pa - 0x14000000
    name = REG.get(off, "*** NOT MAPPED ***")
    print("0x%08x  %-20s %-3s %-4d %d" % (pa, name, d, ln, tally[(pa, d, ln)]))
print()
print("no_exceptions flag on those accesses: %s  (0 = real guest access)" % dict(nx))
print()

#  ---- the +0x2c writes, in order, with their bit decomposition ----
tc = [h for h in hits if int(h[1], 16) == 0x1400002c]
print("--- EVERY ACCESS AT 0x1400002c, IN ORDER ---")
vals = collections.Counter()
for i, (d, pa, ln, n, val, pc) in enumerate(tc, 1):
    v = int(val, 16)
    bits = " | ".join(CTL[b] for b in (0x08, 0x04, 0x02) if v & b) or "(no bits set)"
    print("%-3d %s len=%s val=0x%02x  = %-32s pc=0x%s" % (i, d, ln, v, bits, pc))
    vals[v] += 1
print()
print("VALUE HISTOGRAM at 0x1400002c (%d accesses):" % len(tc))
for v in sorted(vals):
    bits = " | ".join(CTL[b] for b in (0x08, 0x04, 0x02) if v & b) or "(none)"
    print("   0x%02x  x%-3d  %s" % (v, vals[v], bits))
print()
print("WRITES=%d  READS=%d  carrying WDCTL_RST(0x04)=%d"
      % (sum(1 for h in tc if h[0] == "W"), sum(1 for h in tc if h[0] == "R"),
         sum(1 for h in tc if int(h[4], 16) & 0x04)))

#  ---- where in the probe does the RST write land? ----
print()
print("--- CONTEXT: the 12 window accesses immediately before/after each 0x1400002c write ---")
idx = [i for i, h in enumerate(hits) if int(h[1], 16) == 0x1400002c]
for k, i in enumerate(idx[:2] + idx[-1:], 0):
    lo, hi = max(0, i - 4), min(len(hits), i + 5)
    print("  ... around hit index %d (val 0x%s):" % (i, hits[i][4]))
    for j in range(lo, hi):
        d, pa, ln, n, val, pc = hits[j]
        off = int(pa, 16) - 0x14000000
        mark = "  <<<" if j == i else ""
        print("      %-6s %-20s val=0x%-4s%s"
              % (d, REG.get(off, "UNMAPPED +0x%02x" % off), val, mark))

7670: * **`idesync`** �?" lowest value of the four. `0xE7` is defined at `wdcreg.h:130` and never
7671:   referenced; no OpenBSD 7.6 path issues it, and a `dd`-through-`/dev/rwd0c` attempt failed
7672:   because the ramdisk has no node. Reachability stays where the earlier seat left it.
7673: 
7674: ### `landiskaux` �?" open, NEW, and adjacent to `wdcnoirq`
7675: 
7676: **The wdc device-control register is unmapped on landisk.** `dev_wdc.c:1056` places
7677: alt-status/device-control at `addr + 0x206` under a machine switch covering MACPPC, HPCMIPS
7678: and IQ80321 �?" **not landisk**. OpenBSD writes `0x1400002c` via `obio_iomem_write_1` and it
7679: lands nowhere: 6 unmapped writes diskless, 12 with a disk. **So the guest's nIEN and SRST
7680: are no-ops today**, which bears directly on `wdcnoirq`'s interrupt behaviour.
7681: 
7682: ### `imagesmd` �?" records only
7683: 
7684: `regress/images.md`'s landisk section still says *"the rig deliberately sends no input"*,
7685: which `#293` fixed; `gate_crossfamily.sh:186-192` already annotates it as having been untrue
7686: for far longer than it was true.
7687: 
7688: ### `sh4prio0` �?" ~~open~~ **FIXED as #455 (`dc0c9d5`)**. One guest store, unpreventable.
7689: 
7690: *Kept in full rather than trimmed, because the design recorded below was REFUTED before it was built and a future reader needs to see that it was caught. The state line is corrected here rather than left to the ledger alone: a row reading `open` on shipped, witnessed, gated work tells a future reader it was never done.*
7691: 
7692: `src/cpus/cpu_sh.c:312-316` ends the host process when any interrupt is asserted at priority 0:
7693: 
7694: ```c
7695: 	if (prio == 0) {
7696: 		/*  Interrupt not implemented? Hm.  */
7697: 		fatal("[ SH interrupt 0x%x, prio 0 (?), aborting ]\n", irq_nr);
7698: 		exit(1);
7699: 	}
7700: ```
7701: 
7702: **The comment is a wrong record in its own right: nothing is unimplemented.** Priority 0 is a
7703: documented, guest-selectable, RESET-DEFAULT state. Quoted verbatim, and corroborated four
7704: separate ways in the same manual:
7705: 
7706: > "Each interrupt has a priority level (16-0) ... **When level 0 is set, the interrupt is
7707: > masked and interrupt requests are ignored.**" �?" A19.2, `_scratchpad/sh7750_hardware.txt:39887-39888`
7708: 
7709: > "**IPRA to IPRC are initialized to H'0000** and IPRD is to H'DA74 by a reset."
7710: > �?" A19.3.1, `:40193-40194`
7711: 
7712: So **every on-chip source is priority 0 out of reset**, and the model's own init agrees:
7713: `cpu_sh.c:204-218` sets `intc_iprd`, the INTPRI/INTMSK words and comments *"All others are
7714: zero"*, then bakes that into the array at `:220`.
7715: 
7716: #### TWO ROUTES, and the second is the one that changes the ranking
7717: 
7718: **Route A �?" `-E landisk`, ordinary driver ordering.** Enable the TMU0 underflow interrupt
7719: (`TCR0 = 0x0020` �?" and note it PASSES `#447`'s guard, because `TCR_UNIMPLEMENTED` is `0x02DC`
7720: and `TCR_UNIE` is `0x0020`, not in the mask), set TCOR0/TCNT0, start it with `TSTR = 1`, and
7721: never touch IPRA. The host timer underflows, `dev_sh4.c:585` asserts, `prio = (0 >> 12) & 0xf
7722: = 0`, and the process dies. A guest that enables before prioritising is doing something
7723: perfectly ordinary.
7724: 
7725: *** Route B �?" `-E testsh`, ONE STORE, AND NO GUEST WRITE CAN PREVENT IT. ***
7726: `machine_test.c:98-99` wires the **entire test-machine interrupt tree** to
7727: `SH4_INTEVT_IRQ15` (`0x3e0`, array index 31). `sh_update_interrupt_priorities()`'s IRQ loop
7728: (`cpu_sh.c:255`) is bounded `i <= SH4_INTEVT_IRQ14`, so index 31 is **never assigned** �?" and
7729: extending the loop would not help, because the formula `15 - ((i - IRQ0)/0x20)` gives
7730: `15 - 15 = 0` for that very line. `dev_irqc.c:185` defaults to all interrupts enabled, so one
7731: store to `0x110000a0` (`DEV_MP_IPI_ONE`) reaches `dev_mp.c:206` �+' `dev_irqc.c:63` �+' the prio-0
7732: kill, synchronously, inside the store instruction.
7733: 
7734: **In practice: the SH test machine's interrupt controller cannot be used at all** �?" any
7735: interrupt it delivers ends the emulator. All three line numbers checked by hand.
7736: 
7737: #### *** THE WITNESS IS CHEAP, AND THE REASON CORRECTS THIS PROJECT'S OWN RULE ***
7738: 
7739: The carrier said a stepping probe cannot observe interrupts. **The exact word is DELIVERY.**
7740: `cpu_dyntrans.c:204-206` guards the exception DISPATCH; this `exit(1)` sits in the **assert**
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""`#451` DETECTOR: the two command-byte validators in sh4_sci_cmd() decline instead of
ending the host process -- and, more importantly, DECLINE MEANS DECLINE.

This is the detector, not the witness.  sh4_sci_witness.py asserts the PRE-FIX symptom
(three guest-clocked bytes that killed the host) and is RED now that they do not; this
file asserts the post-fix property and must stay green.

*** WHY SURVIVAL IS NOT THE PROPERTY. ***  #451 deleted two exit(1) calls, and with them
the accidental tripwire that made every mistake in this function LOUD.  Before the fix, a
guard that grew or a `return` that went missing announced itself by ending the host on the
first offending byte.  After it, the same mistakes are SILENT: a missing `return` performs
an invented transfer against the RS5C313 and says nothing, and a guard that grew declines a
legal command and says nothing.  A row that checks only "the host is still alive" cannot
see either, and would have passed every mutant the pass-1 panel named.

So the oracle here is the TRANSFER, observed through the same bit-banged protocol a guest
uses.  Two facts make that possible, both measured before this file was written:

  *  a legal pair -- 0xa5 (set address 5) then 0x9c (write data c) -- prints
     `[ SCI: write addr=5 data=c ]` from dev_sh4.c:685.  The address and the data are
     values THIS FILE chose, so the line is a device signature and not merely output.
  *  a declined byte prints its own `command declined` line and NO transfer line at all.

The debug() lines are visible because these sessions SINGLE-STEP.  debug() early-outs at
debugmsg.c:375 on `(quiet_mode && !ss) || v < 0`, and stepping sets `ss`.  A free-running
variant of this probe would go quiet WITHOUT SAYING SO -- do not convert these rows to a
free run without re-establishing the oracle.

THE ROWS, and which mutant each one exists to kill.  Every one of these was named by a
pass-1 seat, and the ones marked (*) survive a detector built the obvious way.

  R1  byte 0x10   site 1 declines, and NO transfer follows.                          (*)
                  0x10 has bit 7 CLEAR but a LEGAL AD/DT field, so if site 1 latches
                  and then falls through -- the one-line mutant -- 0x10 reaches the
                  data-write arm and really writes.  Byte 0x00 CANNOT see this: its
                  0x30 field is also invalid, so site 2 catches what site 1 dropped and
                  masks the defect.  Three seats converged on this independently.
  R2  byte 0x80   site 2 declines (encoding 0), no transfer.
  R3  0x80 then 0xb0 IN ONE SESSION -- 0xb0 must ALSO print.                         (*)
                  Both bytes reach the same else arm, so a latch keyed per SITE reports
                  the first and swallows the second forever.  This row is the whole
                  argument for keying on the field.  It is also vacuity-floor clause (b)
                  for the 0x30 value, which had no witness until the pass-1 panel.
  X   all THREE fault categories in ONE session -- all three must print.            (*)
                  R3 catches a collision between the two SITE-2 values; nothing caught a
                  collision ACROSS the sites.  TWO pass-2 seats keyed site 1 to collide
                  and picked DIFFERENT bits, so a two-byte row catches one and misses the
                  other; driving 0x10, 0x80 and 0xb0 together catches both.  Measured.
  RPT1 a SITE-1 offender TWICE -- one line, no transfer.                             (*)
                  THE ROOT CAUSE THREE SEATS FOUND SEPARATELY: every repeat and cross row
                  drove site 2, so site 1 ran exactly once in a fresh session.  Three
                  real-defect mutants walked through that gap, and the measuring seat
                  scored one of them 10/10 against the shipped file.
  N   0xa5, 0x20, 0x20, 0x9c -- a declined byte must not re-latch sci_cur_addr.      (*)
  N2  0xa5, 0x80, 0x9c -- the SITE-2 twin of N.                                      (*)
                  Site 2 can be made to fall through with address_transfer = 1 instead
                  of 0.  1 pokes sci_cur_addr WITHOUT reaching the write arm, so it
                  emits no line and every `transfers == []` row stays green.  Measured
                  at 12/12 before this row existed.
  CEIL 0x80 then 0xc0 -- two DIFFERENT bytes of ONE category, still ONE line.        (*)
                  RPT repeats the SAME byte, which is a weaker claim.  Widening the key
                  mask 0x30 -> 0xf0 keys 0x80 and 0xc0 apart and draws two lines,
                  silently breaking the stated three-line ceiling.  Measured at 12/12.
                  0x20 is declined but its field is the ADDRESS arm, which emits no
                  transfer line, so `transfers == []` looks clean while the address latch
                  is corrupt.  The oracle is structurally blind without this shape.
  RPT the SAME offender twice in one session: exactly ONE line, still declined.      (*)
                  Kills the sharpest mutant the panel found -- moving the `return`
                  INSIDE the latch guard, which is the natural typo since the braces are
                  already there.  It declines the first bad byte and lets every later
                  one fall through, and a detector that drives each offender once in a
                  fresh emulator scores it perfect.  The same row kills an unlatched
                  fatal() from the other side, by counting.
  A1  byte 0xa5   ACCEPT: a legal address transfer is silent.
  A2  0xa5, 0x9c  ACCEPT: a legal data write LANDS, with the address and data this file
                  chose.  C1 in the witness covers one valid command shape only; the
                  0x10 arm had no accept row before this one.
  J   0x80 then 0xa5, 0x9c -- a legal pair still works AFTER a decline.
                  dev_sh4.c:731 zeroes sci_bits_outputed in the CALLER, so a decline
                  re-arms the shifter.  Moving that line into the callee below the new
                  returns is a realistic tidy-up that jams the shift register at 8
                  forever, and nothing else here would notice.
  O   the transfer oracle is LIVE (A2 saw its line) -- without this, every "no transfer"
                  row above is unfalsifiable rather than true.
  I   identity: row count.

usage:  sh4_sci_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <landisk-kernel>
"""
import os
import re
import sys

import sh4_pcic_probe as P

SHREG_SCSPTR = 0xffe0001c       # thirdparty/sh4_scireg.h:64  (byte-wide)
SPB1IO, SPB1DT = 0x08, 0x04     # :83, :84
SPB0IO, SPB0DT = 0x02, 0x01     # :85, :86

#  dev_sh4.c:664 and :685.  Matching "SCI: read"/"SCI: write" would also match the
#  DECLINE lines, which contain "SCI: command byte ..." -- anchor on the word that only
#  a real transfer prints.
XFER_RE = re.compile(r"\[ SCI: (read|write) addr=([0-9a-f]+) data=([0-9a-f]+) \]")
DECLINE_RE = re.compile(r"sh4: SCI: command byte 0x([0-9a-f]{2}) ")

EXPECT_ROWS = 14

rows = []


def row(name, ok, got, want):
    rows.append((name, bool(ok)))
    print("  %-4s %s" % ("ok" if ok else "FAIL", name))
    if not ok:
        print("       got  %s\n       want %s" % (got, want))


def clock_byte(value):
    """Opcodes that clock `value` in, most-significant bit first.  See the witness."""
    ops = []

    def store(v):
        ops.append(0xE000 | (v & 0xff))     # mov #v,r0
        ops.append(0x2100)                  # mov.b r0,@r1
    store(SPB1IO | SPB0IO)                  # prime: SPB0IO must be in the OLD value
    for i in range(7, -1, -1):
        d = SPB1DT if (value >> i) & 1 else 0
        store(SPB1IO | SPB0IO | d)
        store(SPB1IO | SPB0IO | SPB0DT | d)
    return ops


def drive(seq, label, kw):
    """Clock every byte in `seq` through one session.  Returns (transfers, declines)."""
    ops = []

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""#443 DETECTOR: an unsupported SH-4 PCIC transaction is DIAGNOSED AND SURVIVED
instead of calling exit(1) -- and, just as importantly, is not silently absorbed.

Rung 3.  Real SH-4 guest instructions through real address decode, real
`memory_rw` and real device dispatch, on an UNMODIFIED in-tree `-E landisk`.
No source is edited, no `device_add` of our own, no boot.

WHAT THIS IS AND IS NOT.  The pre-fix WITNESS (`_scratchpad/sh4pcic_witness.py`)
asserted the SYMPTOM -- a MEASURED census of all 137 word offsets in both
directions found 116 killing the host on a plain 32-bit READ and 125 on a write
-- so it goes red once the defect is gone, which is what a witness should do.
This file is the DETECTOR: it asserts the FIXED behaviour, fails on the pre-fix
build, and is built to fail on the mutants listed at the foot of this docstring.
Grading one by the other's clauses is a category error in either direction.

*** THE ROW THAT MATTERS MOST IS P3, NOT P1. ***  Deleting `exit(1)` and nothing
else does not fix this defect -- it makes it worse.  The store into `pcic_reg[]`
at dev_sh4.c:875-879 is UPSTREAM of every guard, so a rejected value has ALREADY
landed by the time the guard runs, and `exit(1)` was the only thing that stopped
a guest reading it back.  P3 writes an accepted value, then a rejected one, then
READS THE REGISTER BACK through the guest; the naive fix turns a host kill into
silent state corruption, and P3 is the only row in this file that sees it.

P3b is its sibling and covers the `default:` arm, whose restore is otherwise
UNOBSERVABLE: nothing else in the tree reads `pcic_reg[]`, and a `default:` read
answers 0 regardless.  It becomes observable through ADDRESS ALIASING -- the
switch dispatches on the FULL address, but PCIC_REG() indexes on the WORD, so
0xfe200015 (a byte inside PCICONF5) reaches `default:` while sharing PCICONF5's
array slot.  A byte store there clobbers the whole word on the way in; only the
restore puts it back.

P2b uses the same aliasing in the other direction, and it is what gives "reads
answer 0" any teeth at all: on a fresh device every unimplemented slot already
holds 0, and writes to unimplemented offsets are dropped, so `odata = 0` and
`odata = pcic_reg[i]` agree EVERYWHERE until a labelled register has put a
non-zero word into a slot that `default:` can reach.

*** SEVEN MUTANTS PASSED THE FIRST 29 ROWS.  P14-P20 ARE WHAT CLOSES THEM. ***
The 29-row file measured ONE access width, observed TWO of the ten `pcic_old`
restores, never offered any arm a SECOND rejected write, and never wrote
PCICONF0 before reading it.  Each of those blanks was a family of survivors,
not a single mutant:

  P14/P15  the WIDTH AXIS.  `if (len == 2) exit(1);` -- the original host kill
           narrowed to one width -- passed 29/29, because len=2 was issued
           NOWHERE in the file and len=1 at only two fixed addresses.
  P16      one read-back per RESTORE.  Deleting a single restore (PCIMBR's,
           say) passed 29/29 and let a guest read its own rejected word back.
  P17/P18  the SECOND OFFENCE.  Bracing the restore INSIDE the latch -- a
           brace slip, the shape a tidy-up produces -- restores the first
           offence and silently corrupts every one after it.  29/29, ten arms.
  P19      the MUTUALLY-MASKING PAIR in the PCICONF0 arm.  Each of the two
           statements is void alone, so single-line mutation is blind to both;
           delete both and the guest gets its own word back.
  P20      the latch keyed on (offset, FAULT CLASS).  A per-offset latch merges
           two different faults that share one array slot and drops the second
           diagnostic entirely.

P4 counts the LATCHED SUFFIX, never a register name.  Under a cold debugger
`single_step` is true, so `debug()`'s quiet_mode early-out never fires and a
probe run this way CANNOT distinguish `fatal()` from `debug()` by presence -- a
#441 detector row was passed by a two-character mutant for exactly that reason.
P5 is P4's other half: a PER-DEVICE latch passes P4 and fails P5.

NO SH-4 MANUAL IS CITED ANYWHERE IN THIS FILE, and every constant below is read
out of a header in this repository -- with the file and line named -- or measured.

*** THE REASON GIVEN FOR THAT CHANGED ON 2026-08-22, AND THE PRACTICE DID NOT. ***
This paragraph used to say there was no SH-4 manual in the tree, which was true
when it was written (`_scratchpad/sh4_manual.txt` is one byte) and is FALSE now:
a real SH7750 Hardware Manual (Rev.7.00, REJ09B0366-0700) and SH-4 Software
Manual were obtained by owner directive and extracted to
`_scratchpad/sh7750_hardware.txt` and `_scratchpad/sh4_programming.txt`.

The file still cites none, deliberately.  A probe's constants should come from
the headers the CODE actually compiles against, because a probe that agrees with
the manual while the code disagrees with both would be green and useless.  The
manual is now available for deciding what the code OUGHT to do; the headers
remain the authority on what it DOES.

usage:  sh4_pcic_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <landisk-kernel> [--jobs N] [--fast]

    *** KEEP THE "./" ON THE BINARY. ***  `os.execvp` on a bare name searches
    PATH; when it misses, EVERY arm silently reports alive=False and a probe
    written the naive way "passes" having measured nothing.  P0 catches that,
    and the existence check in main() catches it sooner.
"""
import argparse
import os
import pty
import re
import select
import sys
import time
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor

# ----------------------------------------------------------------- constants
#  All READ from this tree.  Every one was opened.
SH4_PCIC = 0xfe200000               # src/include/thirdparty/sh4_pcicreg.h:39
N_PCIC_REGS = 0x224 // 4            # src/devices/dev_sh4.c:72        -> 137

#  The 21 offsets carrying a `case` label in DEVICE_ACCESS(sh4_pcic),
#  dev_sh4.c:885..1036 (`default:` at :1041).  Names from sh4_pcicreg.h:48..110.
LABELLED = {
    0x000: "PCICONF0", 0x004: "PCICONF1", 0x008: "PCICONF2",
    0x014: "PCICONF5", 0x018: "PCICONF6",
    0x100: "PCICR",    0x104: "PCILSR0",  0x108: "PCILSR1",
    0x10c: "PCILAR0",  0x110: "PCILAR1",
    0x1c0: "PCIPAR",   0x1c4: "PCIMBR",   0x1c8: "PCIIOBR",
    0x1e0: "PCIBCR1",  0x1e4: "PCIBCR2",  0x1e8: "PCIWCR1", 0x1ec: "PCIWCR2",
    0x1f0: "PCIWCR3",  0x1f4: "PCIMCR",   0x1f8: "PCIBCR3",
    0x220: "PCIPDR",
}
ALL_OFFS = list(range(0, N_PCIC_REGS * 4, 4))
UNLABELLED = sorted(o for o in ALL_OFFS if o not in LABELLED)

#  The nine labelled offsets whose WRITE arm carries a value guard, each with
#  the substring its diagnostic prints.  dev_sh4.c:885, 929, 941, 953, 965,
#  977, 989, 1001, 1013.
GUARD_MSG = {
    0x000: "write to SH4_PCICONF0",
    0x014: "SH4_PCICONF5 unknown value",
    0x018: "SH4_PCICONF6 unknown value",
    0x104: "SH4_PCILSR0 unknown value",
    0x108: "SH4_PCILSR1 unknown value",
    0x10c: "SH4_PCILAR0 unknown value",
    0x110: "SH4_PCILAR1 unknown value",
    0x1c4: "PCIMBR set to",
    0x1c8: "PCIIOBR set to",
}

#  The EIGHT labelled arms that carry BOTH a value guard and a restore of the
#  previous word, with the value each one accepts.  Read out of the guard
#  expressions in DEVICE_ACCESS(sh4_pcic); the two symbolic ones are
#  SH4_PCIC_MEM (sh4_pcicreg.h:44) and SH4_PCIC_IO (sh4_pcicreg.h:41).
#
#  There are TEN restores in that function -- these eight, plus PCICONF0's
#  (unobservable by design; see P19) and `default:`'s (P3b, P18).  Before P16
#  only TWO of the ten were observed by this file, and deleting any of the
#  other eight was MEASURED to pass all 29 of its rows.
RESTORE_ARMS = {
    0x014: ("PCICONF5", 0xac000000),
    0x018: ("PCICONF6", 0x8c000000),
    0x104: ("PCILSR0", (64 - 1) << 20),
    0x108: ("PCILSR1", (64 - 1) << 20),
    0x10c: ("PCILAR0", 0xac000000),
    0x110: ("PCILAR1", 0xac000000),
    0x1c4: ("PCIMBR", 0xfd000000),
    0x1c8: ("PCIIOBR", 0xfe240000),
}

#  One word every one of those eight arms rejects, and which no arm accepts.
REJECT = 0x12345678

#  The load/store pair for each ACCESS WIDTH, keyed by `len` as the device sees
#  it.  Without this the census was one-dimensional -- see P14/P15.
LDST = {
    1: (0x6210, 0x2100),        # mov.b @r1,r2  /  mov.b r0,@r1
    2: (0x6211, 0x2101),        # mov.w @r1,r2  /  mov.w r0,@r1
    4: (0x6212, 0x2102),        # mov.l @r1,r2  /  mov.l r0,@r1
}

#  Guest scratch.  landisk RAM is 64 MB at 0x0c000000 (machine_landisk.c:84);
#  0x8c010000 is its P1 (cached, unmapped) alias, past the 0x8c002000 entry
#  point and inside the loaded image -- the same scratch #441's probe uses.
CODE = 0x8c010000
DEST = 0x8c010100
RAMSRC = 0x8c010200

#  Instruction halfwords.  EVERY ONE IS CHECKED against the emulator's own
#  disassembler in a session that executed it -- row P11.  A wrong register
#  field is silent: it yields 0, which every zero-valued oracle accepts by
#  accident, which is why P7c also insists on a small NON-ZERO value.
OPS = {
    0x0009: "nop",
    0x6210: "mov.b @r1,r2",
    0x6211: "mov.w @r1,r2",
    0x6212: "mov.l @r1,r2",
    0x6252: "mov.l @r5,r2",
    0x2100: "mov.b r0,@r1",
    0x2101: "mov.w r0,@r1",
    0x2102: "mov.l r0,@r1",
    0x2142: "mov.l r4,@r1",
    0x2322: "mov.l r2,@r3",
    0x2312: "mov.l r1,@r3",
    0x2540: "mov.b r4,@r5",
    0x6250: "mov.b @r5,r2",
    0x7104: "add #4,r1",
    0x7304: "add #4,r3",
    0x3510: "cmp/eq r1,r5",
    0x8bfa: "bf 0x%x" % CODE,
}

#  Two values the DEVICE synthesises.  Neither is stored anywhere this probe
#  writes, so neither can be echoed back to us by RAM.
PCICONF0_ID = 0x350e1054        # dev_sh4.c:894-896, PCI_ID_CODE(0x1054,0x350e)
PCICONF2_CLASS = 0x06000000     # dev_sh4.c:2205, PCI_CLASS_CODE(0x06,0x00,0)

LATCHED = "(once per offset)"
2685: 		    "a second device is still told", other);
2686: 	}
2687: 
2688: 	free(e.inbuf);
2689: 	free(d.inbuf);
2690: }
2691: 
2692: 
2693: /*  THE DEVICE-CONTROL ACCEPT ROW.
2694:  *
2695:  *  A pending sector write must SURVIVE an ordinary Device Control write.
2696:  *
2697:  *  *** THE WARRANT FIRST WRITTEN HERE WAS WRONG, and it was wrong in the
2698:  *  reassuring direction.  It said this row is "the only instrument in the tree
2699:  *  that can see the regression it guards against: MEASURED, no gate arms a CDROM,
2700:  *  so the ALT-CTRL/COMMAND_RESET path is never exercised by any rig."  Corrected
2701:  *  2026-08-27 after a seat refuted it. ***
2702:  *
2703:  *  The ALT-CTRL arm NEEDS NO CDROM.  `if (idata & WDCTL_4BIT) d->cur_command =
2704:  *  COMMAND_RESET;` is unconditional -- only the SIGNATURE BYTES read back at
2705:  *  wd_cyl_lo/wd_cyl_hi are CDROM-gated.  So "no CDROM" never implied "not
2706:  *  exercised", and the row's own justification did not follow.
2707:  *
2708:  *  The defensible statement is narrower and still supports the row: no RIG writes
2709:  *  Device Control, and on the fork's only SH4 wdc machine it CANNOT -- the driver
2710:  *  writes addr+0x2c while GXemul registers alt-status at addr+0x206, so twelve
2711:  *  probe writes land on unmapped memory (filed separately as `landiskaux`).  A
2712:  *  PROBE can write it, and a seat built one at rung 3.
2713:  *
2714:  *  *** THAT MAKES THIS ROW'S WARRANT REST ON A SECOND DEFECT. ***  Fix the
2715:  *  mapping and the ALT-CTRL arm goes live on that machine too.  A row justified by
2716:  *  another bug's existence is worth keeping and worth SAYING SO, because the
2717:  *  justification expires the day that bug is fixed.
2718:  *
2719:  *  0x08 is not an exotic value.  From cited driver source: Linux libata holds
2720:  *  ATA_DEVCTL_OBS (0x08) as `ap->ctl`, the STEADY STATE between commands, and older
2721:  *  OpenBSD resets with 0x06 (bit 3 clear) and then writes 0x08 separately.  A guest
2722:  *  may write it at any moment, including mid-transfer.
2723:  *
2724:  *  So a future round that hangs a ring-flush or a writer-cancel off the ALT-CTRL arm
2725:  *  -- while it still triggered on WDCTL_4BIT (0x08) rather than WDCTL_RST (0x04) --
2726:  *  would cancel a legal transfer on a routine register write.  This row reds on that
2727:  *  edit.  It is the de-escalation clause's accept side.
2728:  *
2729:  *  *** THAT FIX IS `#481`, AND THE PREDICTION HELD -- BUT ONLY BECAUSE THE ROUND DID
2730:  *  THE PREREQUISITE FIRST.  `#481` hung exactly this writer-cancel off the ALT-CTRL
2731:  *  arm, and this row is GREEN, because the same change corrected the bit: a routine
2732:  *  0x08 never reaches the cancel.  The row DOES red under `Md_bitswap` -- which is
2733:  *  that same edit WITHOUT the prerequisite.  So this row measured the ordering
2734:  *  argument rather than merely asserting it, which is what an accept row is for. ***
2735:  */
2736: static void row_devctl_preserves_write(void)
2737: {
2738: 	struct wdc_data d;
2739: 	unsigned char buf[8];
2740: 	int i;

215: def session(cmds, nstep, label="", extra=None, disasm_upto=0,
216:             binary=None, kernel=None, timeout=90):
217:     """One cold-debugger session.  Returns (buf, alive, started).
218: 
219:     `started` is kept separate from `alive` ON PURPOSE.  A session that never
220:     reached the debugger prompt ALSO reports alive=False, and scoring that as
221:     "the guest access killed the host" would let a broken invocation -- missing
222:     binary, missing image, host out of memory -- masquerade as a measurement.
223:     P0 requires started of every session this run ever opened.
224:     """
225:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
226:     if pid == 0:
227:         argv = [binary, "-V", "-E", "landisk", "-M", "64"] + (extra or []) \
228:                + [kernel]
229:         try:
230:             os.execvp(binary, argv)
231:         except Exception:
232:             pass
233:         os._exit(127)
234:     buf = ""
235:     died = [False]
236: 
237:     def rd(t=0.4):
238:         nonlocal buf
239:         r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], t)
240:         if fd not in r:
241:             return True
242:         try:
243:             d = os.read(fd, 65536)
244:         except OSError:
245:             died[0] = True
246:             return False
247:         if not d:
248:             died[0] = True
249:             return False
250:         buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
251:         return True
252: 
253:     def wait(mark=0, echo=None, timeout=timeout):
254:         t = time.time()
255:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
256:             if not rd():
257:                 return False
258:             resp = buf[mark:]
259:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
260:                 continue
261:             if len(buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
262:                 return True
263:         return False
264: 
265:     def send(s):
266:         #  `_mark`, not `mark`: gate 6's fresh-mark census keys on that exact
267:         #  spelling, and every other pty probe in the tree writes it that way.
268:         b = (s + "\n").encode("latin1")
269:         _mark = len(buf)
270:         n = 0
271:         while n < len(b):
272:             try:
273:                 n += os.write(fd, b[n:])
274:             except OSError:
275:                 died[0] = True
276:                 return False
277:         return wait(mark=_mark, echo=s if s else None)
278: 
279:     started = wait(timeout=120)
280:     STARTS.append((label, started))

332: def dwords(buf):
333:     """The words the LAST `dump` printed, in ADDRESS order, exactly as printed.
334: 
335:     `dump` renders raw bytes, so a little-endian 32-bit 0xac000000 stored at
336:     DEST prints as 000000ac.  Anchored on the TWO spaces `dump` puts after the
337:     address, which `put`'s echo (`0x8c010100: 11111111`) does not have.
338:     """
339:     m = re.findall(r"^\s*0x[0-9a-f]+  ((?:[0-9a-f]{8} )*[0-9a-f]{8})",
340:                    (buf or "").replace("\r", ""), re.M)
341:     return m[-1].split() if m else []
342:

344: def dumped(buf):
345:     """The word at DEST itself -- w[0], not the last word on the line."""
346:     w = dwords(buf)
347:     return w[0] if w else None

350: def le(v):
351:     """The byte string `dump` prints for a 32-bit value stored little-endian."""
352:     return "".join("%02x" % ((v >> (8 * i)) & 0xff) for i in range(4))

355: def poke(hws, at=CODE):
356:     return ["put h 0x%x, 0x%04x" % (at + 2 * i, h) for i, h in enumerate(hws)]

359: def read_arm(addr, label, kw, extra=None):
360:     """Guest 32-bit LOAD from `addr`, then STORE the result to DEST.
361: 
362:     Two instructions, so a row can check the VALUE and not merely survival.
363:     """

584: static int status_byte(struct wdc_data *d, struct cpu *cpu)
585: {
