850:  *  WRITER, BUFFERED BYTES, OR PHASE.
851:  *
852:  *  #480 L4: THE SCOPE IS THIS FUNCTION, NOT THE WORD "COMMAND".  An earlier
853:  *  headline read "A NEW COMMAND STARTS WITHOUT..." with no qualifier, and the
854:  *  device falsified that in its own code: the ALT-CTRL arm assigned
855:  *  cur_command = COMMAND_RESET WITHOUT CALLING THIS HELPER, so the most
856:  *  reset-like operation the device had was not a caller at all.  The SCOPE
857:  *  paragraph forty lines below said so; a universal headline with its qualifier
858:  *  that far down is read as universal.  Named by the fable seat on #479 pass 2.
859:  *
860:  *  wdc_command() cleared the ATAPI flag and the error and nothing else, so three
861:  *  pieces of the previous command's state survived into the next one.  All three
862:  *  were reproduced with liveness controls before this was written:
863:  *
864:  *    write_in_progress   an ATA write stayed armed across a packet command, so one
865:  *                        shared ring served the disk-write path and the CDB path at
866:  *                        once.  MEASURED: a guest's own sector write put 512 CD-ROM
867:  *                        DATA-IN bytes and ZERO of its own on the image.
868:  *    the input ring      residue from an under-drained transfer was never cleared.
869:  *                        MEASURED: the drain then fires early and the guest's sector
870:  *                        arrives with the previous transfer's bytes at its head.
871:  *    atapi_phase         PHASE_CMDOUT survived an ordinary WRITE command.  Latent
872:  *                        while the dispatch tests the flag AND the phase -- and #477's
873:  *                        comment was pointing a future round straight at it.
874:  *
875:  *  WHY THE TOP, AND NOT THE END.  IDENTIFY loads its 512-byte response INTO the ring

922: 	    count-zeroing: there is no consumer to notify and nothing to hand back.  */
923: 	d->inbuf_head = d->inbuf_tail = 0;

24260:   "title": "wdcaltwidth  the alt-ctrl register's ACCESS WIDTH is unpinned: every row uses len == 1 while the region is registered TWO bytes wide",
24553:   "state_note": "*** OVERTAKEN BY `#481`, AND THAT DISCHARGES THE PAIRING HAZARD. ***\n\nThe row: the ALT-CTRL write arm triggered on `WDCTL_4BIT` (0x08) -- the head-count bit a\ndriver holds as steady state -- instead of `WDCTL_RST` (0x04), the soft-reset bit.\n\n`#481` changed the predicate to `if (idata & WDCTL_RST)`.  VERIFIED by reading the arm at\nHEAD, and the arithmetic settles the consequence: `0x08 & 0x04 == 0`, so a routine\n`WDCTL_4BIT` control write no longer latches COMMAND_RESET.\n\n*** WHY THIS MATTERS BEYOND ITS OWN CLOSURE: IT WAS A PREREQUISITE FOR `landiskaux`. ***\nThe queue recorded, correctly at the time, that shipping `landiskaux`'s one-line mapping fix\nALONE would make the ALT-CTRL arm live on landisk WITH ITS PREDICATE STILL WRONG -- six of\nthe twelve measured Device Control writes are `0x08`, so mapping the register would have\nstarted latching COMMAND_RESET on six routine writes per probe.\n\nThat reasoning was sound and its premise is now false.  With `#481` shipped, the six `0x08`\nwrites are INERT, and `landiskaux` is safe to ship on its own.\n\n*The queue entry says these two 'MUST SHIP TOGETHER'.  They no longer must.  It is the third\nstale-severity finding of the day, and the same shape each time: a severity that was an\nanswer to a question rather than a property of a row, left standing after the question\nchanged.*  *** L4 -- OVERTAKEN CONDITION (2) IS UNMET IN THE FIELD THE RULE NAMES.  Corrected 2026-09-17, found by the Opus 5 seat. ***  The carrier requires *\"THE CARRYING ROWS NAMED by label and probe path, in the witness or closure field.\"*  This row's witness read only *\"#481's rows pin the RST predicate\"* -- **no labels** -- where the sibling precedent (`wdcoddstep`) names *\"the two `odd chunk:` rows\"*.  The labels exist and the seat verified all five in `diff_wdc_identify.c`. **AND CONDITION (1) IS MET MORE STRONGLY THAN THE CLOSURE NOTE CLAIMED**, which is the unusual direction for a records defect: the note said *\"verified by reading the arm at HEAD\"*, while the preserved evidence is `bin_Md_bitswap.out` (8 rows red) plus `PREFIX_committed_device.out` (the five reproduction rows red on the committed PRE-FIX device).  **The row UNDERSTATED its own evidence.** *** AND THE BRIEF'S ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG AS LITERAL C, WHICH IS WORTH KEEPING: `0x08 & 0x04 == 0` PARSES AS `0x08 & (0x04 == 0)` = `0x08 & 0` = **0**, not 1 -- `==` binds tighter than `&`. ***  Harmless in prose; this project has shipped a wrong constant from a hand-written expression before, and the same text pasted into a probe row is a VACUOUS ROW. **THE SEAT ALSO REFUTED THE BRIEF'S FRAMING OF THE CONSEQUENCE, and its version is better.**  Disjointness (`0x08 & 0x04 == 0`) establishes only that the predicates are INDEPENDENT.  What settles the consequence is DRIVER TRAFFIC: libata holds `4BIT` as steady state and asserts SRST as `ctl|ATA_SRST` -> 0x0c/0x0e, releasing with 0x08/0x0a, so under the old predicate the RELEASE latched a reset; older OpenBSD asserts 0x06 with 4BIT clear, so the old predicate MISSED a real reset.  **On 0x0c/0x0e old and new predicates AGREE -- libata's assert was never the broken half, which is exactly why the wrong bit survived so long.**  A reader taking the brief's framing would believe the two predicates always differ, which is false for the dominant driver's assert. *The detector already covers the both-bits case: `diff_wdc_identify.c` writes `buf[0] = WDCTL_RST | WDCTL_4BIT; /* 0x0c -- assert */`, and its signature row records exercising both 0x06 and 0x0c.  The carried coverage is better than the brief described.*  *** NINE-ROW CLOSURE PANEL, 2026-09-17 -- AND IT CONVERGED ONCE SEAT CLASS WAS RESPECTED, WHICH IS THE DOCTRINE WORKING RATHER THAN A DISAGREEMENT. ***  Seven seats answered; the kimi CLI hit its weekly wall and its `kimi-k3:cloud` substitute COVERED the seat (not lost). **THE SPLIT IS BY SEAT CLASS, NOT BY OPINION.**  The four FILE-READING seats (opus5, grok, agy, codex) each verified against HEAD and the preserved artifacts, and reached **CLOSE on rows 1-8, KEEP-OPEN on row 9**. CTRL\" under `-v`. ***  **So the handler that exists is never reached, and the twelve writes that should reach it are discarded.**\n\n**THE FIX:** `case MACHINE_LANDISK: alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x2c; break;`  The seat independently confirmed the mechanics from the source: `alt_status_addr` is assigned BEFORE the switch, has **no later overwrite and exactly one subsequent use** (the auxiliary registration), and the secondary-controller selection uses `devinit->addr` rather than `alt_status_addr` -- so **there is no hidden default-dependent tail requiring another change.**\n\n*** TWO CORRECTIONS THE CENSUS MAKES AGAINST ITSELF, AND THEY MUST NOT BE DROPPED: ***\n  1. **`0x1400002c` IS ONE OF FIVE UNMAPPED SITES PER BOOT.**  The other four are real queue items: **`0xfe080000`** (5/boot) and **`0xfe080040`** (1/boot), SH7751R extended-INTC registers reached from `intc_init` and `intpri_intr_priority`; and **`0x10000003`** (5/boot, `rtc_ce`) and **`0x10000005`** (3/boot, `intr_init` / `extintr_establish`), landisk board registers.  *The write-up had presented only the `0x14000000..0x14000040` window while `census.py` prints an \"ALL paddrs\" section.*\n  2. **CONSEQUENCE, stated by the census itself: fixing `0x1400002c` does NOT make `-T` usable on a landisk boot** -- four other sites still halt it.  **So any `-T`-based row is a PROBE row, not a boot row.**\n\n**AND ONE CITATION IS EMBARGOED BY THE ARTIFACT'S OWN CAPTION:** a \"2560 agreement\" figure is marked OWED -- *\"do not let a round block cite the 2560 agreement\"* until the `-v` instrument is re-taken to completion and preserved.  *Recorded here so the next round meets the embargo rather than the number.*\n\n**MY OWN FRAMING WAS ALSO WRONG: there is NO `default:` arm.**  `alt_status_addr = devinit->addr + 0x206;` is assigned before a switch carrying exactly three cases (MACPPC `+0x160`, HPCMIPS conditional, IQ80321 `+0x402`). 25268:   "note": "*** LIVE IN THE SHIPPED TREE, MEASURED AT RUNG 3, AND ITS WITNESS WAS ALREADY COMMITTED -- by `#481`, the round that made it worse. ***  Found by the opus5 seat on a panel convened for an unrelated (and already shipped) round; every element re-verified before filing.  `DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus)` **never reads `relative_addr`** -- measured, ZERO uses in the handler -- while the region is registered TWO BYTES WIDE. The opus5 seat refuted that by measuring, and the reason is that **the test is an INVARIANCE, not a value**: `status_byte()` never reads `d->head`, and a Drive Address register is by definition a function of head select.  On the committed build, reading 0x3f7 gives 51 at head=0 AND 51 at head=15 -- invariant -- while the SDH readback of 0xaf proves the head write landed and 0x3f6 is unchanged by it, so the invariance is not `nothing moved`.  That refutes every candidate model of 0x3f7 without naming a single expected bit.  *** WHAT IS STILL MISSING IS THE FIX'S ORACLE, NOT THE DEFECT'S. ***  An invariance proves today's value WRONG without saying what is RIGHT, and none of the fifteen PDFs under `_scratchpad/` is an ATA or ISA document -- `ls _scratchpad/*.pdf` is the authority, not this note.  Pinning digin's bits from model memory is exactly the #357/#362/#365 failure this project already paid for.  AND THE OWNERSHIP QUESTION MAY DISSOLVE IT: on a PC the byte belongs to the FLOPPY controller (see `wdcaltownership`), in which case the right answer is not a wdc value at all.  Settle ownership first.  SCOPE NOTE: the model does hold the raw material -- `case wd_sdh` latches `d->sectorsize`, `d->lba`, `d->drive` and `d->head` from the stored byte -- so ACCEPT is structurally available here in a way it was NOT for the store half, where there is no discarded right answer. It also records a SECOND refutation the summary above omits -- a read of 0x3f7 returns 58 with DRQ raised, and **a real Drive Address register never carries DRQ**, so the read is tracking the status register on a channel independent of the head-invariance argument.\n\n*** REVIEWED 2026-09-11 (wdcaltdigin/codex_digin_20260911.txt, GPT-6 Astra at max, ZERO cyber flags).  THE LAYOUT IS NOT DERIVABLE FROM THE TREE -- which is the honest negative the brief asked for -- AND ONE OF MY FOUR OPTIONS TURNS OUT NOT TO BE AN OPTION. ***\n\n*** MY OPTION (d), 'decline the read as the write is declined', IS LITERALLY IDENTICAL TO OPTION (a), 'change nothing'. ***  The handler computes `odata = status_byte(d, cpu)' at `:617', BEFORE it looks at `relative_addr', and the common tail at `:746-747' does `if (writeflag == MEM_READ) data[0] = odata;'.  **So an ignored offset-1 read still returns the already-computed status: a handled no-op does not release the address.**  Genuine delegation to the floppy controller would need routing and registration work outside this branch.\n\n*That is the same shape as the sketch rule this project already carries -- a proposed edit is not a fix until it has been run against the enclosing function's real control flow -- reached from the other end: my option was written from the SITE and the behaviour is decided by the TAIL.*\n\n**WHAT THE TWO PERMITTED FILES DO AND DO NOT ESTABLISH:**\n  * `wdcreg.h` carries a DISABLED register-OFFSET definition and **no Drive Address BIT definitions at all** -- so the disabled block is a placeholder, not a usable layout.\n  * the bit layout, polarity and the high-impedance bit are **not derivable from this tree**.  The seat declined to supply a remembered layout and said so, which is what was asked of it.\n  * every WDC instance this file initialises registers the region TWO BYTES wide; the machine-by-machine floppy-attachment census is outside the permitted sources.\n\n**AND OPTION (b) -- return a constant `0x80` -- IS WEAKER THAN IT LOOKS:** *\"'Undefined' does not mean 'must be one'\"*, and on the active-low premise I supplied, **zero select bits ASSERT signals**, so an otherwise-zero byte is not a neutral default.  It would also still supply no head-dependent readback, which is the very thing the witness measures.\n\n**OPTION (c) IS THE ONLY ONE THAT COULD BE RIGHT, AND IT NEEDS A SPECIFICATION FIRST.**  Encouragingly, `d->head` is **not** a variable nothing trusts -- it is used for actual disk addressing and holds the last programmed head nibble -- so a drive/head readback is honest as far as it goes.  What is unknown is whether drive and head suffice for EVERY defined bit.\n\n*** DISPOSITION: HOLD, PENDING AN ATA SPECIFICATION. ***  Preserving the current behaviour while the evidence is obtained is defensible; **declaring it hardware-correct is not**, and neither is inventing a byte.  This project fetched PowerPC Book I yesterday on exactly this pattern -- a review naming the document that would settle a question, the owner approving, and the load-bearing claims verified LOCALLY afterwards -- and it converted a dispute into a decided defect in twenty minutes.  The same move is available here.\n\n**A NOTE FOR WHOEVER RESUMES THIS: if the disposition becomes (c), the existing witness INVERTS.**  It currently passes by showing INVARIANCE across head select (51 at head=0 and head=15).  Under (c) invariance becomes the FAILURE condition, so the round owes a positive oracle asserting the specified encoding -- and a row that merely shows the value CHANGED would be satisfied by any head-dependent garbage.\n\n*** SETTLED 2026-09-11 BY FETCHING ATA-2, AND IT REFUTES ALL FOUR OF MY OPTIONS INCLUDING THE ONE THE REVIEW CALLED THE ONLY DEFENSIBLE ONE. ***\n\n`_scratchpad/ata2_x3t10_948d.pdf` / `.txt` -- **Working Draft X3T10/0948D, \"Information Technology -- AT Attachment Interface with Extensions (ATA-2)\"**, 409 KB -> 314 KB of text, 5,909 lines.  Fetched with the owner's approval on the Book I / vmesbcp2 / 21066 / ddi0406 precedent and verified locally.  *(`file` says \"6 page(s)\" and is wrong again -- third manual in a row.)*\n\n**A GREP FOR \"Drive Address\" RETURNS ZERO, AND THAT IS NOT AN ABSENCE OF EVIDENCE.**  The register is in the Control Block map by its ADDRESS, not by that name (`:1483-1495`):\n\n        CS0-  CS1-   DA2 DA1 DA0        READ (DIOR-)          WRITE (DIOW-)\n        N     A       1   1   0         Alternate status      Device control\n        N     A       1   1   1         (Note 1)              Not Used\n\n*Having been bitten yesterday by a grep-only negative, I looked for the register by ADDRESS after the name returned nothing -- which is what found it.*\n\n*** NOTE 1, `:1529-1531`, IS THE WHOLE ANSWER: ***\n\n    \"This register is obsolete.  It is recommended that a device not respond to a read of this\n     address.  If a device does respond, it shall be sure not to drive the DD7 signal to prevent\n     possible conflict with floppy disk implementations.\"\n\n**SO THE DISPOSITION IS NOT TO ANSWER AT ALL**, and every option I put to the review is wrong:\n  * **(a) keep the status alias** and **(d) decline-in-place** -- identical to each other, as the review found, and both RESPOND where the standard recommends not responding;\n  * **(b) return a constant** -- responds, and invents a value the standard declines to specify;\n  * *** (c) MODEL DRIVE AND HEAD FAITHFULLY -- which the review called the only disposition that could be right -- IS REFUTED OUTRIGHT. ***  It would implement, carefully, a register the standard marks OBSOLETE and tells devices not to answer.  **The review could not have known this: it was confined to two files that do not contain the layout, and it said so.**\n\n*** AND THE MODEL SATISFIES THE DD7 CONSTRAINT ONLY BY ACCIDENT, WHICH IS A LATENT HAZARD WORTH ITS OWN SENTENCE. ***  `WDCS_BSY` is `0x80` -- exactly DD7 -- and `status_byte()` never sets it, assembling only DRDY, DSC, DRQ and ERR.  So today's alias happens to leave bit 7 clear and happens not to conflict with a floppy controller.  **Nothing enforces that.**  An edit that made `status_byte()` report BSY -- an entirely reasonable thing for a future round to do -- would start driving DD7 at an address the standard reserves against floppy conflict, and no row anywhere would notice.\n\n**WHAT THE ROUND NOW OWES, and it is a design question rather than a documentation one:** what \"not respond\" MEANS in this emulator.  Returning 0 from a device access function is not silence -- `memory_rw.c` converts it into an architectural exception on MIPS and M88K -- so the round must choose between an unanswered access, an open-bus value, and leaving the region registered one byte narrower so the address is never claimed.  **The narrowing option is the one that matches the standard's language most closely and is the only one that also fixes the write half by construction.**\n\n**THE WITNESS SURVIVES AND ITS ORACLE INVERTS THE OTHER WAY FROM WHAT I EXPECTED.**  I noted above that under (c) the invariance test would become a failure condition.  Under the real disposition the invariance is still not the point: the row must assert that the address is NOT ANSWERED with a status byte, and a new row should pin **bit 7 clear** so the accidental compliance above becomes an enforced one.\n\n*** THE IMPLEMENTATION IS DESIGNED AND IT IS ONE NUMBER: register `wdc_altstatus` with length **1 INSTEAD OF 2**.  2026-09-11 (wdcaltdigin/codex_impl_RETRY_20260911.txt). ***\n\n**THE OTHER THREE CANDIDATES ALL FAIL, and for different reasons worth keeping:**\n  * **(b) keep two bytes, return `0` at offset 1** -- *\"It implements a FAILED MAPPED ACCESS.  It cannot be identified with ATA nonresponse without a justified bus translation.\"*  WDC still claims the address; the guest gets an architectural exception on MIPS/M88K, which asserts something about the hardware that nothing establishes.\n  * **(c) keep two bytes, answer with a constant leaving DD7 clear** -- *\"No.  WDC still supplies a SUCCESSFUL response.\"*  It removes the status alias and preserves the thing Note 1 actually recommends against: answering at all.\n  * **(d) move the `status_byte()` call inside the offset-0 branch** -- leaves offset 1 answering the initialised zero, which is still a response.\n\n**EVERY ATTACHING MACHINE REGISTERS TWO BYTES TODAY** -- `MACHINE_MACPPC` at `addr + 0x160`, `MACHINE_HPCMIPS` at `0x14000386` and via its default formula, `MACHINE_IQ80321` at `addr + 0x402` -- and **no alternate-register functionality needs the second byte**: it supplies only the read alias and the guarded write, and the header already compiles `wd_digin` out.\n\n*** THE HONEST LIMIT, STATED BY THE SEAT AND KEPT HERE: narrowing removes WDC's CLAIM to the address, but what the COMPLETE MACHINE then does at an unclaimed address is not established by the permitted files. ***  That is a measurement, not a reading -- and it may differ by architecture, since `memory_rw.c` delivers a failed-device exception on MIPS and M88K only.  **The round owes that measurement on each attaching machine before it can claim 'does not respond' rather than merely 'WDC no longer answers'.**\n\n*Note the pleasing consequence if it holds: narrowing fixes the WRITE half by construction too, since an unclaimed address cannot carry a guarded write -- so the earlier write-side fix becomes redundant rather than wrong, and the round should say which it is.*\n\n*** OWNER RULING 2026-09-11: NARROW THE REGISTRATION. ***  Put to the owner as three options (narrow / open-bus constant / hold-plus-DD7-guard); the owner chose NARROW.  The site is `dev_wdc.c:2028-2029`, a literal `2` -- **and a harvest seat verified it is a literal `2 -> 1`, NOT `1 * addr_mult`**: the alt callback never divides its offset by `addr_mult`, and all five machine cases at `:2003-2018` change the ADDRESS only, never the length.\n\n*** MY OWN PREMISE IN THE OPTION SET WAS WRONG, AND I CORRECT IT HERE BEFORE IT IS ACTED ON. ***  I told the owner narrowing gives `MIPS/M88K: architectural bus fault`.  **It does not.**  I carried the carrier's TRUE statement about the REGISTERED-device `res <= 0` path (`memory_rw.c:603-611`, which does raise EXCEPTION_DBE) into the UNMAPPED path, which does not share it.  Read at `memory_rw.c:676-712`:\n  * an unmapped READ does `memset(data, 0, len)` and returns ZEROES;\n  * an unmapped WRITE falls straight to `do_return_ok`, silently ignored -- `:709-710` literally asks *\"Shouldn't there be a DBE exception for invalid writes as well?  TODO\"*;\n  * the MIPS `EXCEPTION_DBE` block at `:698-704` is inside `#if 0` -- **disabled**;\n  * `memory_warn_about_unimplemented_addr` (`core/memory.c:598-606`) returns false outright for `paddr < physical_max + 0x40000`, so there is no warning flood from an I/O-range address.\n\n**So narrowing is SAFER than I represented it, not riskier** -- no fault, no infinite loop, no flood. This is the fifth instance of the scope-transfer class in two days, and the first where the wrong address was carried into a question put to the OWNER.\n\n**THE `#494` MEASUREMENT DOES NOT TRANSFER EITHER, AND A SEAT REFUSED TO LET IT.**  The comment at `dev_wdc.c:655-665` records 19,793 measured `device 'wdc_altstatus' ... failed` lines and a guest looping forever in silence.  That is a REGISTERED device returning 0 -- the `res <= 0` path -- not the unassigned path narrowing produces.  The seat declined to fill the gap with a guessed per-architecture matrix and said the permitted files do not establish narrowing is safe on **every** attaching machine (MACPPC, IQ80321, HPCMIPS unverified).  *That refusal is the correct form and the residual is real: the generic path is now verified by reading, the per-machine claim is not.*\n\n*** TWO OBLIGATIONS THE NARROWING CREATES, BOTH FOUND BY THE SEAT AND NEITHER IN MY OPTION SET: ***\n  1. **A SPANNING ACCESS IS UNRESOLVED.**  The callback assigns only `data[0]`.  Nothing in the permitted files establishes whether a transaction starting at `A` and spanning the new one-byte boundary is SPLIT, ACCEPTED or REJECTED.  A 16-bit access at the ALTSTATUS address is the obvious guest shape, so **the byte-access argument for narrowing is not proof about those transactions** and this must be measured before the edit ships.\n  2. **THE DD7 ROW I PROPOSED WOULD BE VACUOUS AFTER NARROWING, AND ITS PREMISE IS WRONG ANYWAY.**  *Driving DD7 low is still driving DD7* -- bit-7-clear is not the electrical equivalent of leaving it undriven.  Once the device never answers, a conditional *\"if WDC answers, bit 7 is clear\"* row can never fire, and an UNCONDITIONAL assertion about the guest's value is simply wrong: another responder or the unassigned value may legitimately set it.  **The replacement oracle is ZERO WDC CONTRIBUTION, PROVEN BY ROUTING** -- address, direction and width observed at dispatch.\n\nKEEP the existing write-side guard as defence against a future wider registration, and correct its comment: the ATA-2 draft's write entry for this address is **\"Not Used\"**, not \"read-only\".\n\n*** 2026-09-11 -- SPANNING ACCESS: SAFE.  THE BLOCKER ON THE OWNER-RULED NARROWING IS CLEARED, AND THE EDIT ADDITIONALLY RETIRES A FILED DEFECT BY CONSTRUCTION. ***\n\nThe dispatcher ACCEPTS a wide access whole against the shorter region and CLAMPS it: `memory_rw.c:495-524`.  The `#95` pre-zero at `:491-492` runs first with the caller's FULL `len`, so the guest-visible buffer is byte-for-byte `{status, 0...}` before and after the change.  **No out-of-bounds read or write in either direction.**\n\n*** AND THE SEAT CORRECTED ITS OWN FIRST ANSWER, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING. ***  It had called a wide access THEORETICAL.  It is reachable in-tree: `dev_ps2_spd.c:73-77`, `case 0x5c` (aux control), relays the guest's **own `len`, unmodified**, to `d->wdcaddr + 0x206` -- and the chain is live, not `#if 0`'d (`:44` `DEV_PS2_SPD_LENGTH 0x800`; `:103` `d->wdcaddr = devinit->addr + DEV_PS2_SPD_LENGTH`; `:111-113` auto-adds the wdc; `machine_playstation2.c:106` adds `ps2_spd addr=0x14000000`).  `MACHINE_PLAYSTATION2` is absent from `dev_wdc.c:2006-2018`'s switch, so the default arm puts `alt_status_addr` at exactly the address `case 0x5c` targets.\n\n**That STRENGTHENS the verdict rather than weakening it.**  The safety never depended on nobody issuing a wide access -- it follows from the clamp plus the handler touching only `data[0]`.  *A reachable path that is behaviourally identical is a better answer than an unreachable one.*  And it is likely rather than merely possible: the sibling arm at `:61-71` maps the command block at **2-byte spacing**, the signature of a 16-bit-only ATA host.\n\n*** THE NARROWING CLOSES `wdcaltwidth` BY ELIMINATION. ***  With `length == 1` the found-test forces `relative_addr == 0`, and the clamp then forces `len = 1` for every `len >= 2`, so **the handler can never again be entered with `len != 1`.**  `wdcaltwidth`'s named mutant (`idata = data[0]` -> `data[len - 1]`, which *\"escapes all 103 rows\"*) becomes EQUIVALENT once `len == 1`: `data[len-1]` IS `data[0]`.  **The class is dead, not merely uncovered.**  Two standing self-declared blind spots go with it -- `wdc_altoffset_probe.py:114-115` and `CHANGELOG.md:8617-8620` both describe a `len == 2` store at offset 0 that the narrowing makes unreachable.\n\n**PER THE ABSENCE-RECORD RULE, THE WARRANT IS NAMED: *the clamp at `memory_rw.c:523` forces `len == 1` for a one-byte region.*  Any future edit restoring the region's width MUST re-run the death measurement.**  This is closure by ELIMINATION, not by coverage, so the three overtaken-closure conditions do not apply -- but it does supersede the `ps2_spd` exposure, which is the one live consumer of the width the edit removes.\n\nTWO REFINEMENTS TO MY OWN CORRECTION, both accepted:\n  * the unmapped path is silent about DATA but **not about LOGGING** -- `memory_rw.c:676-679` calls `memory_warn_about_unimplemented_addr()`, which prints at `VERBOSITY_WARNING` while the default is `VERBOSITY_INFO`.  *(The suppression I cited, `core/memory.c:605`, applies below `physical_max + 0x40000`; whether this address qualifies is machine-dependent and is NOT settled here.)*  The `EXCEPTION_DBE` at `:701-703` is confirmed dead (`#if 0` at `:685`).\n  * **the safety is CONDITIONAL ON THE HANDLER'S SHAPE.**  It holds because `wdc_altstatus` touches only `data[0]` (`dev_wdc.c:614`, `:746-747`).  The sibling `DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc)` DOES inspect width and latches warnings at `:1093` and `:1968` -- **if anyone brings `wdc_altstatus` into line with that idiom, re-derive this.**\n\nNO SHIPPED DETECTOR ROW CHANGES COLOUR: `killtable_20260830/PRETABLE.out:49-54` already measured width-1 *\"green on every row\"*.  No oracle re-pin owed -- and equally, **no coverage for the change**, which is what the round still owes.\n\n**STATUS: the edit is a literal `2` -> `1` at `dev_wdc.c:2029`, owner-ruled, blocker cleared.  It owes a panel pass 1 on the actual diff and a detector row before it lands** -- the disposition being ruled does not discharge the lifecycle.\n\n*** `wdcaltdigin` PASS 1, 2026-09-11 (codex GPT-6 Astra at max, 15,433 b, complete).  **APPROVED** as a withdrawal of the WDC auxiliary decode at offset 1 -- and two of my premises are refuted, one of them the round's planned oracle. ***\n\n*** THE WITNESS DOES NOT INVERT -- INVARIANCE PASSES BOTH VERSIONS. ***  I had recorded that the existing head-invariance witness INVERTS under the fix.  It does not:\n\n    observable                     two-byte (now)   one-byte (fixed)\n    A+1 read, head 0                    0x51             0x00\n    A+1 read, head 15                   0x51             0x00\n    SDH readback after head 15          0xaf             0xaf\n    EQUALITY ACROSS HEADS               True             True\n\n> **Equality across heads still passes BOTH versions, so invariance alone cannot detect restoration of the two-byte registration.**  An exact `0x51` alias witness would simply stop observing the alias.  **SDH readback establishes that the head write landed in the main bank; it does NOT establish who answered `A+1`.**\n\n**So the acceptance criterion must be replaced with ROUTING EXCLUSION**, and head selection kept only as historical context or a control.  *With another responder present, even post-fix head invariance is not an appropriate requirement at all.*\n\n*** AND I ATTRIBUTED THAT WITNESS TO THE WRONG FILE. ***  `regress/wdc_altoffset_probe.py:113` states explicitly that **no row reads either alternate-region offset**; it contains offset-1 WRITE tests.  The seat declined to authenticate the head-select measurements from it and **refused to infer another filename from the defect id** -- which is precisely the discipline my own `dev_crime.c` error broke four hours earlier, applied back to me.\n\n**THE DD7 ORACLE IS VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION, with the reason made exact:** `status_byte()` adds only DRDY, DSC, DRQ and ERR, **so its aliased result ALREADY has bit 7 clear.  A bit-7-clear detector would accept the current behaviour.**\n\n**AND THE ANSWER TO \"IS RETURNING ZERO A RESPONSE?\" IS THE RIGHT FRAMING:** *\"The emulator completes a read with a numeric result; that does not mean WDC supplied that result.  Removing WDC's address claim is an honest implementation of device NONPARTICIPATION at this emulator's decoding abstraction.  **It does not simulate electrical high impedance.**\"*  The round may claim adoption of a draft RECOMMENDATION with the abstraction stated -- no more.\n\n=== SEVEN DETECTOR ROWS PROPOSED, each with its named mutant and the observable that differs ===\n`A1_READ_WDC_UNCLAIMED` and `A1_WRITE_WDC_UNCLAIMED` against `M_REWIDEN` (callback count 0 -> 1; **WDC state alone would miss the write mutant, since offset-1 write handling is already a no-op**); `A0_READ_ALTERNATE_STATUS` against suppression and against a constant `0x51`; `A0_WRITE_SRST_SIGNATURE` against `M_OMIT_COMMAND_RESET`; two existing P1 accept rows reused.\n\n**THE ROW I HAD NOT CONSIDERED, and it is the sharpest: `A0_READ_PRESERVES_PENDING_IRQ`.**  ATA-2 `:1535` distinguishes Alternate Status from Status precisely in that it does NOT clear a pending interrupt -- and the normal Status handler **does** clear it at `dev_wdc.c:1938`.  So a mutant adding that clearing action to the Alternate Status path is invisible to any status-byte-only oracle: **the returned bytes need not differ at all.**\n\n*** TWO DETECTOR-PACKAGING DEFECTS IN THE SHIPPED PROBE, AND THE SECOND IS A CHECK THAT CANNOT FAIL: ***\n  1. **The existing probe's \"committed\" expectations describe the OLDER offset-1 write-reset defect**, which today's callback already rejects at `dev_wdc.c:622`.  **Reusing its unchanged baseline for this new before/after comparison would MIS-SCORE THE BASELINE.**\n  2. **`ALTOFF_DET_DONE` is emitted UNCONDITIONALLY, and failed comparisons produce no failing exit status** (`wdc_altoffset_probe.py:682`).  **So neither that sentinel nor ordinary process completion is evidence of detector acceptance.**  *This project's own rule is that a preserved log is verified by its completion sentinel -- and here is a sentinel that says DONE whether or not the rows passed.  Filed as its own finding.*\n\n**THE WIDTH RETIREMENT STANDS WITH A SHARPER WARRANT:** `memory_rw.c:492` zeroes the incoming read length **before** the clamp at `:523`, the callback uses the first byte, so a wide read leaves `{status, 0...}` and a wide write retains its first-byte effect.  After narrowing, every positive-length access reaches the callback at offset zero with `len == 1`, so `data[0]` and `data[len - 1]` are equivalent there.  **`:523` is the correct warrant** -- *and it does NOT establish that wide guest accesses disappeared, nor that all width behaviour is hardware-accurate.*\n\n*Premise correction: the initializer carries **three** machine-specific case labels, not the five I implied.  No all-machine clearance is establishable from the permitted files.*\n\n*** 2026-09-11 -- **I NEARLY FILED FIFTEEN PROBES AS DEFECTIVE AND THE DESIGN IS SOUND**.  A NEAR-MISS, RECORDED BECAUSE THE CENSUS LOOKED AUTHORITATIVE AND WAS MEASURING THE WRONG PROPERTY. ***\n\nA pass-1 seat observed, correctly, that `wdc_altoffset_probe.py` emits `ALTOFF_DET_DONE` **unconditionally** and that *\"neither that token nor ordinary process completion is sufficient evidence of detector acceptance.\"*  I verified both halves: the sentinel is outside any conditional, and **there is no `sys.exit` anywhere in the file**, so the process always exits 0 however many rows failed.\n\nI then censused all 93 probes in `regress/`: **15 emit a completion sentinel and never call `sys.exit`.**  That reads like a fifteen-instance defect class.\n\n*** IT IS NOT A DEFECT.  I CHECKED WHAT ACTUALLY CONSUMES THEM BEFORE FILING, AND THE GATE DISCARDS `rc` ON PURPOSE. ***  `gate_arm.sh` runs the probe as `... > \"$SGILOG\" 2>&1 || true`, then:\n  1. **SENTINEL FIRST**, with the reason in its own comment -- *\"a preserved log is verified by its completion sentinel, never by its existence: a killed run loses every row and looks exactly like a run that produced none\"*;\n  2. **CONTROLS BY NAME, not by count** -- *\"a green-row total lets a control go red while the sum stays acceptable, and a run whose controls died is an APPARATUS failure, which must not be read as a wall of product failures\"*;\n  3. **PER-ROW PASS, checked individually** rather than by a total, explicitly against the padded-column class;\n  4. **THE DENOMINATOR PINNED TO A LITERAL**, so a detector that loses rows cannot pass by shrinking.\n\n> **So the sentinel's contract is COMPLETION, not ACCEPTANCE, and it discharges that correctly.  Pass/fail comes from per-row content checks the gate performs itself.  The seat's sentence is literally true and describes the DESIGN rather than a defect.**\n\n**MY CENSUS KEYED ON A CORRELATE -- the absence of `sys.exit` -- INSTEAD OF ON THE CLASS: whether pass/fail is OBSERVABLE TO THE CONSUMER.**  It is, everywhere it matters, through a channel my pattern could not see.  *That is the deriver lesson for the third time today: **before writing a deriver, ask what makes a member a member** -- and if the answer is a property the regex does not test, the error is invisible because the output is still a plausible number.*\n\n*Recorded as a near-miss rather than a finding because the cost of the other outcome was real: fifteen phantom defects filed against working probes, each of which a future round would have had to disprove.  What stopped it was the two-minute check this project already mandates -- **open the consumer and ask what it does when the thing is FALSE** -- and the answer was that the consumer never asked the question I was auditing.*\n\n*** 2026-09-11 ASSESS (main loop, read from source at HEAD).  **#494 CLOSED THE WRITE CELL AND LEFT THE READ CELL OPEN IN THE SAME `if` -- so this is not an independent defect, it is the SYMMETRIC CELL of a repair this project already shipped, which is the `#499` shape the carrier names by its own number.** ***\n\n**THE HANDLER, `DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus)` at `dev_wdc.c:609-675`, in the order it executes:**\n  * `:614`  `idata = data[0];`\n  * `:617`  `odata = status_byte(d, cpu);`  -- **UNCONDITIONAL.  The offset is not consulted.**\n  * `:619`  `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)` -> `debug(\"[ wdc: read from ALTSTATUS ... \")` and nothing else; the common tail then assigns `odata` into the caller's buffer.\n  * `:622`  `else if (relative_addr != 0)` -> the **`#494`** arm: a WRITE to offset 1 is declined silently and the whole action is refused.\n  * `:673`  `else` -> the genuine Alt Ctrl write path (`#481`'s `WDCTL_RST`).\n\n> *** THE READ PATH NEVER DECODES ITS OFFSET AT ALL.  `relative_addr` is tested in exactly ONE place, and that place is inside the `else` of `writeflag == MEM_READ`. ***\n\n**So a READ of offset 1 -- the Drive Address register, `0x3f7` on the PC layout -- returns the ALT-STATUS byte**, which is this row's filed claim, now confirmed from the source rather than asserted.\n\n=== THE CELL SPACE, AND IT IS THE WHOLE FINDING ===\nThe function's own inputs give a two-by-two: **{READ, WRITE} x {offset 0, offset 1}.**\n\n| cell | disposition at HEAD |\n|---|---|\n| WRITE, offset 0 | Alt Ctrl, correct (`#481`) |\n| WRITE, offset 1 | **CLOSED by `#494`** -- declined silently, whole action refused |\n| READ,  offset 0 | alt-status, correct |\n| READ,  offset 1 | *** OPEN -- returns the alt-status byte *** |\n\n**`#494` repaired the cell its mutant used and left the symmetric one.**  The carrier states this class in its own words -- *'the original breach, reproduced inside its own repair, by someone who had just spent an hour understanding it'* -- and prescribes the remedy: **when a class recurs, enumerate the full space from the function's own inputs and close every cell in ONE diff, then write into the contract which cells are covered.**  Four cells; three closed; one diff owed.\n\n*** AND `#494`'S OWN COMMENT CARRIES A MEASUREMENT THAT BEARS DIRECTLY ON THE REPAIR CHOICE, which is why it must be read before this round picks a disposition. ***  At `:655-665` it records, correcting an earlier wrong reason in place:\n\n> *'THE ACCESS STAYS HANDLED (`return 1`) ... this device is REGISTERED, so a 0 never reaches [unassigned-address] handling.  `memory_rw` sets `res = -1`, takes its `res <= 0` branch, and raises `EXCEPTION_DBE` -- measured, **19793 `device 'wdc_altstatus' ... failed` lines and ZERO `non-existant` ones** under `-v`.  The guest then loops forever re-executing the faulting store, and at DEFAULT verbosity it does so in SILENCE.'*\n\n**So `return 0` FROM THIS REGISTERED DEVICE IS MEASURED TO HANG THE GUEST SILENTLY.**  That is a fact about a *registered* device returning 0 -- it is NOT the same thing as narrowing the registration, which makes the address unmapped and takes an entirely different path.  *The two dispositions are easy to conflate and the tree has a measurement distinguishing them; it would be a wrong record to cite that 19793 against a narrowing.*\n\n=== THE OPTION SET FOR THE OPEN CELL, with what each actually does here ===\n  * **NARROW THE REGISTRATION** (`dev_wdc.c:2028-2029`, the literal `2` -> `1`) -- closes BOTH offset-1 cells structurally, and the handler's `relative_addr != 0` arm becomes dead code that must then be dispositioned rather than left as a comment describing a case that can no longer arrive.  **Changes 0x3f7 from HANDLED-AND-DECLINED to UNMAPPED**, so the `#494` measurement above does not transfer and the unmapped path must be measured on its own.\n  * **DECODE THE READ** -- mirror `#494` on the read side inside the handler, leaving the registration alone.  Smallest diff; keeps the address owned by wdc; makes the two-by-two symmetric.\n  * **BACKED ACCEPT / IMPLEMENT** -- model the Drive Address register.  **Refused by the tree's own evidence**: `wdcreg.h` compiles `wd_digin` out under an `#if 0` whose note reads *NOT MAPPED; fd uses this register on PCs*, and `dev_fdc.c`'s `DEV_FDC_LENGTH` carries *TODO 8, but collision with wdc*.  **The tree documents the collision from BOTH sides and resolves it from neither** -- which is [[wdcaltownership]], and it is a different question from this one.\n\n*** THE ORDERING CONSTRAINT, so a round does not ship the two in the wrong sequence: NARROWING SUBSUMES DECODING, and decoding first then narrowing leaves dead code behind. ***  [[wdcaltwidth]] is coupled to the same literal -- the region is registered two bytes wide while every existing row uses `len == 1`, so a narrowing also settles that row's unpinned width, and any detector written here should pin the width deliberately rather than inherit it.\n\n**STATUS: specified, NOT implemented.**  No edit made.  A build and a measurement are owed and could not be taken in this window -- a 24-leg regression tail holds the machine and this project's gates carry load-sensitive wall-clock oracles, so a measurement taken now would be worth nothing.  *The disposition question is put to the owner rather than assumed, because 'narrow the registration' and 'FAULT -- return 0' are different actions here and the tree has a measurement showing the second one hangs the guest.*\n\n*** 2026-09-11 DESIGN (codex GPT-6 Astra at max, 23,793 b, 0 cyber flags) + **OWNER RULING: DECODE THE READ, registration untouched.** ***\n\n*VERDICT: The four-cell table is correct.  The registered-device fault is live, but its measurement does not establish narrowing behavior.*\n\n**MY CELL TABLE SURVIVED THE SOURCE CHECK** -- the read path does not decode its offset, and the open cell is (READ, offset 1).  **And the transferability question I raised was answered the way I suspected: `#494`'s measured 19793 failed-access lines describe a REGISTERED device returning 0, and do NOT establish what a NARROWED registration would do.**  *Citing that measurement against a narrowing would have been a wrong record, which is why it was asked rather than assumed.*\n\n*** AND IT CAUGHT MY THIRD INVENTED PATH OF THE DAY. ***  The brief listed `src/include/wdcreg.h`; the seat reported *'The system cannot find the file specified'*, **did not guess a replacement** (as the brief instructed), and scoped its answer to the three files it could open.  The real path is **`src/include/thirdparty/wdcreg.h`**.  *Three invented paths in one session -- `dev_crime.c`, `regress/sh_tlb_probe.py`, and now this -- each caught by a seat that refused to substitute a neighbour.  The brief rule that produced the catch is the one that says VERIFY EVERY PERMITTED PATH EXISTS, and I verified four of five.*\n\n=== THE REPAIR, AS THE SEAT WROTE IT ===\n\nDecline the offset-1 read, return byte **`0x00`**, retain handler return value **1**, keep the unconditional status calculation and the common tail:\n\n    if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {\n        if (relative_addr != 0) {\n            odata = 0;\n        } else {\n            debug(\"[ wdc: read from ALTSTATUS: 0x%02x ]\" ..., (int)odata);\n        }\n    } else if (relative_addr != 0) {\n        /*  Existing #494 write-decline arm.  */\n\nThe Alt Ctrl arm follows unchanged.\n\n**ZERO'S WARRANT IS NAMED AND ITS LIMIT IS NAMED WITH IT** -- the emulator's deterministic declined-read convention: `#95` pre-zeroes registered-device read buffers and the ordinary unimplemented-address path also chooses zero.  *** 'That is an emulator policy, not proof that a real Drive Address or floppy register must read zero.' ***  *An honest warrant with its scope stated, rather than a hardware claim the files cannot support -- which is the distinction [[wdcaltownership]] turns on.*\n\n=== *** THE ORACLE TRAP, AND IT IS THE SAME SHAPE AS TONIGHT'S n6 FINDING *** ===\n\nThe clause-(d) mutant is **B-null-effect**: keep `if (relative_addr != 0)` and its branch, but remove `odata = 0`, so offset-1 reads return the status byte S again.\n\n> *** A DECLINED READ MUST USE A FIXTURE WITH **S != 0** TO KILL THAT MUTANT.  IF S IS ZERO, THE FIXED AND THE BROKEN IMPLEMENTATIONS RETURN THE SAME BYTE. ***\n\n**That is a check that cannot fail, identified before any row is written** -- the second one caught at design time tonight, after `n6`'s `r7 == 0` row would have been vacuous against a mask-returning variant for exactly the same reason (*the mask would also be zero*).  *Two independent rounds, same failure shape: an oracle asserting ZERO is worthless unless the defective path is shown to produce NON-zero.*\n\n**AND THE CONTROL IS ESTABLISHABLE, which is why this is a constraint rather than a blocker:** **disklessness does NOT imply zero status** -- initialisation sets `d->error = 1` at `dev_wdc.c:1994`, and `status_byte()` includes `WDCS_ERR` when `d->error` is set at `:594-595`.  *So a diskless fixture is a legitimate null medium AND can carry a nonzero S; the round must establish that explicitly rather than assume it.*\n\n**THE SECOND NULL-MEDIUM MEANING NEEDS ITS OWN REJECTION:** a null test vehicle that returns zero for every read without exercising wdc at all **is accepted by an offset-1-zero assertion alone**.  The detector must reject it through a **legal offset-0 read expected to return nonzero S**.\n\n*** A NOTE ON THE ROAD NOT TAKEN, recorded because the reasoning is reusable: for option A (narrowing) the seat observed that `2 -> 1` HAS NO GUARD-AND-EFFECT PAIR, so 'changing 1 back to 2' is REVERSION, not a clause-(d) mutation. ***  Its structural analogue would be **A-null-exclusion** -- keep the one-byte registration but add a SECOND one-byte registration of the released address back to this handler, defeating the exclusion while the original line still reads `1`.  **Checking only that the registration still says `1` would miss it.**  *Filed as reasoning for whoever eventually takes [[wdcaltwidth]]; not this round's problem.*\n\n**STATUS: specified and RULED.  Implementation, witness and kill table are the next unit of work.**  The `#519` regression tail has now cleared the machine, so measurement is available.\n\n*** 2026-09-11 WITNESS + KILL TABLE (agy 3.8, measuring seat).  **WITNESS BUILT AND TABLE RUN: HEAD 14/16, FIX 16/16, AND S WAS MEASURED NONZERO (0x51 / 0x58) -- so the oracle trap the design pass predicted was real and is discharged by measurement rather than by assertion.** ***\n\n**THE FIXTURE ESTABLISHES NONZERO S EXPLICITLY, which is the whole reason this round needed care.**  The clause-(d) mutant `M_NULL_EFFECT` keeps `if (relative_addr != 0)` and deletes `odata = 0`, so offset-1 reads return the status byte again -- **and if S were 0 the fixed and broken builds would return the identical byte and the row would be vacuous.**  Measured:\n  * at devinit `dev_wdc_init()` sets `d->error = 1`, so with a disk attached `status_byte()` gives `WDCS_DRDY | WDCS_DSC | WDCS_ERR` = `0x40 | 0x10 | 0x01` = **`S = 0x51`**;\n  * armed by `WDCC_WRITE` (clearing error, setting `write_in_progress`): `WDCS_DRDY | WDCS_DSC | WDCS_DRQ` = **`S_armed = 0x58`**.\n**Both values are printed in the log rather than asserted** -- so the discrimination is visible in the artifact, not just claimed in a report.\n\n=== SCORES -- AND THE SCORES ALONE ARE MISLEADING, WHICH IS THE FINDING ===\n*(`regress/artifacts/wdcaltdigin/killtable_20260911/`, `KILLTABLE_DONE` present; I found a stronger independent check in the supplied OpenBSD kernel: `wdc_obio_attach` contains a direct `+0x2c` when mapping the auxiliary region. I’m decoding that call and the register-access wrappers to determine exactly what it proves about `addr_mult`.

There is also an existing `addr2=` device parameter, so declaring the auxiliary address in `machine_landisk.c` is a concrete alternative.

 *  Same construction as regress/diff_sh4_tmu.c and for the same reason:

 *  IMPOSSIBLE, only unable to happen silently.)
 *
 *  THE DEFECT. Six of the eight capacity lines used `% 255` where `& 255` is
 *  meant. The two that were already correct are the ones whose operand cannot
 *  exceed a byte; every other line shifts without pre-masking, so `% 255` is a
 *  base-256 digit sum rather than byte extraction:
 *      x % 255 == ((x >> 8) + (x & 255)) % 255      (0 counterexamples over 2^26)
 *  Above the threshold it is wrong 100% of the time. Worst case announces ZERO
 *  SECTORS for a real disk.
 *

DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus)
{
	struct wdc_data *d = (struct wdc_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	idata = data[0];
	odata = status_byte(d, cpu);
	if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
		if (relative_addr != 0) {
			odata = 0;
		} else
			debug("[ wdc: read from ALTSTATUS: 0x%02x ]\n",
			    (int)odata);
	} else if (relative_addr != 0) {
		debug("[ wdc: write to DRIVE ADDRESS (read-only): 0x%02x,"
		    " ignored ]\n", (int)idata);
	} else {
		debug("[ wdc: write to ALT. CTRL: 0x%02x ]\n",
		    (int)idata);
		if (idata & WDCTL_RST) {
			d->cur_command = COMMAND_RESET;
			wdc_new_command_reset(d);
		}
	}
	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		data[0] = odata;
	return 1;
}

**VERDICT: The mapping defect is confirmed, and the proposed case produces the correct address today. The future scaling rule remains unproved; I prefer `+0x2c` on the available evidence. A concrete rung-3 probe is available, but its pre/post measurement remains outstanding.**

I read **`GXEMUL-SEC/src/`**. Its switch, initialization, registrations, and machine configuration match your cited lines. I also independently recounted the archived logs: A1/A2/A3 each contain twelve unmapped byte writes; B1 contains twelve, with values `{0x0c: 1, 0x08: 6, 0x02: 5}`, plus **12,718 command-block accesses**. These are checks of saved artifacts, not new boots. [B1 log](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/landiskaux/census_20260827/logs/B1_hook_full_window.log:51)

For **Q1 and Q2**, the arithmetic is:

| Quantity | Enumerated result |
|---|---|
| Main register starts, relative to base | `0*2=0x00`, `1*2=0x02`, `2*2=0x04`, `3*2=0x06`, `4*2=0x08`, `5*2=0x0a`, `6*2=0x0c`, `7*2=0x0e` |
| Main registration | `8*2=16=0x10` bytes: `0x14000000..0x1400000f` |
| Current auxiliary bytes | `0x14000206`, `0x14000207` |
| Proposed displacement | `0x16=22`; `22*2=44=0x2c` |
| Proposed auxiliary bytes | `0x1400002c`, `0x1400002d` |
| Misplacement | `0x206−0x2c = 518−44 = 474 = 0x1da` |
| Separation from main mapping | Main ends at offset `0x0f`; auxiliary starts at `0x2c`. Offsets `0x10..0x2b` are the intervening 28 bytes. |

**There is independent evidence beyond the census.** I decompressed and disassembled the supplied [OpenBSD kernel](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_images/openbsd76-landisk-bsd.rd), which identifies itself as **OpenBSD 7.6 RAMDISK #70**. In `wdc_obio_attach`, the auxiliary mapping call contains:

```text
0x8c16016e: mov #2,r6       ; mapping length
0x8c160174: mov.l @r2,r5    ; same configured base used for main mapping
0x8c16017c: add #44,r5      ; 44 = 0x2c
0x8c16017e: jsr @r0        ; mapping callback
```

Additionally, `obio_iomem_write_1` adds its handle and offset, then executes `mov.b r7,@r6` at **`0x8c15f6e8`**, independently matching the census PC.

This establishes the **byte displacement**. It does **not** recover the original C expression: constant folding could produce that instruction from either `0x2c` or `0x16*2`. The register wrappers separately double register offsets within their selected main/auxiliary region.

Thus:

- `0x16 * addr_mult` is correct for the declared `addr_mult=2`.
- Neither the census nor this disassembly establishes that a future spacing change must relocate the auxiliary block proportionally.
- The three existing cases establish no universal rule against multiplication.
- **`0x16` as an independently specified register-unit displacement remains unsettled.** Upstream-source retrieval failed; I cannot supply a verified source citation for it. The previous four-file review was right not to derive an auxiliary displacement from primary-register spacing alone.

For **Q3**, yes: use the existing [SH4 guest-instruction mechanism](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/sh4_pcic_probe.py:215) on unmodified `-E landisk`, with an attached IDE image.

A concrete detector can avoid relying on diagnostic text:

1. Execute a guest **IDENTIFY** command, `0xec`, at command/status address `0x1400000e`. Confirm through a guest status load that `DRQ` is set.
2. Execute a byte guest store of `0x0c` to the candidate auxiliary address.
3. Load primary status again through the guest and record whether `DRQ` cleared.
4. Reissue IDENTIFY before testing the other address.

The handler’s reset clears the buffered IDENTIFY response; an unmapped store leaves it buffered. Expected results:

| Reset-store destination | Before fix | After fix |
|---|---|---|
| `0x1400002c` | DRQ remains set | DRQ clears |
| `0x14000206` | DRQ clears | DRQ remains set |

The SH4 helper already provides `mov.b r0,@r1` (`0x2100`) and byte loads. Its uncached aliases are explicit: `0xb400002c & 0x1fffffff = 0x1400002c`; `0xb4000206 & 0x1fffffff = 0x14000206`.

Require successful guest execution, a RAM round-trip, and the initial IDENTIFY/DRQ response as controls. Run **the identical script and fixed post-fix assertions against both binaries**. That traverses machine initialization and address dispatch, and detects both the new mapping and removal of the old one.

I have specified this probe, not executed it. It therefore establishes a concrete route, **not a completed rung-3 witness**.

For **Q4**, the measured delta is real and licenses an address correction. The members I would consider are:

| Member | Assessment |
|---|---|
| LANDISK case using `addr + 0x2c` | Preferred minimal correction under the verified byte-layout contract. |
| Proposed `addr + 0x16 * addr_mult` | Equivalent today; broader scaling contract needs its own warrant. |
| Absolute `0x1400002c` | Equivalent today, but unnecessarily discards the relationship to the configured base. |
| Machine declaration using `addr2=0x1400002c` | Concrete architectural alternative: `addr2` already exists and is parsed. WDC must also be changed to consume it with defined precedence; changing the machine string alone does nothing. |
| Keep both auxiliary addresses | Adds an alias without evidence that it is needed; fails the old-address negative control. |

The existing secondary-address machinery is in [device.h](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/device.h:47) and [device.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/device.c:320). Moving the primary base would contradict the working command-block evidence.

One limit matters: **this enables the existing reset behavior; it does not implement nIEN.** The current auxiliary handler acts on `WDCTL_RST`, but has no interrupt-mask implementation for `WDCTL_IDS`. [Reset action](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_wdc.c:832)

For **Q5**, no new detector accompanies the supplied diff, so an unconditional claim about what “passes the shipped detector” would be invented. Concrete predicted survivors depend on its scope:

- **Landisk-only guest detector:** change the initializer’s `0x206` to `0x207`—one character. LANDISK still passes because its case overwrites the initializer; fallback machines regress. This breaks “other machines unchanged.”
- **Offset-zero byte accesses only:** change auxiliary registration length `2` to `1`—one character. Those accesses still pass while the registered extent changes.
- **Direct-handler differential only:** change the proposed `0x16` to `0x17`. The resulting displacement is `23*2=46=0x2e`; initialization is bypassed, so handler tests cannot detect the wrong mapping.

These are structural predictions, not executed mutant results.

Adding the fourth case **with the existing breaks preserved creates no cross-arm selection hazard**. The selector values are distinct: HPCMIPS `1004`, MACPPC `2004`, IQ80321 `5005`, LANDISK `6004`. Address proximity cannot select another arm. Incidentally, the guarded HPCMIPS assignment currently equals its fallback: `0x14000180 + 0x206 = 0x14000386`.

If LANDISK is appended after IQ80321, deleting IQ80321’s `break;` would introduce fallthrough and corrupt IQ80321 while a LANDISK-only detector remains green. An initialization-level mapping table covering the existing cases and fallback is the appropriate complement to the guest probe.

No files were changed, and no gates, battery, builds, or guest runs were executed.

**VERDICT: The mapping defect is confirmed, and the proposed case produces the correct address today. The future scaling rule remains unproved; I prefer `+0x2c` on the available evidence. A concrete rung-3 probe is available, but its pre/post measurement remains outstanding.**

I read **`GXEMUL-SEC/src/`**. Its switch, initialization, registrations, and machine configuration match your cited lines. I also independently recounted the archived logs: A1/A2/A3 each contain twelve unmapped byte writes; B1 contains twelve, with values `{0x0c: 1, 0x08: 6, 0x02: 5}`, plus **12,718 command-block accesses**. These are checks of saved artifacts, not new boots. [B1 log](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/landiskaux/census_20260827/logs/B1_hook_full_window.log:51)

For **Q1 and Q2**, the arithmetic is:

| Quantity | Enumerated result |
|---|---|
| Main register starts, relative to base | `0*2=0x00`, `1*2=0x02`, `2*2=0x04`, `3*2=0x06`, `4*2=0x08`, `5*2=0x0a`, `6*2=0x0c`, `7*2=0x0e` |
| Main registration | `8*2=16=0x10` bytes: `0x14000000..0x1400000f` |
| Current auxiliary bytes | `0x14000206`, `0x14000207` |
| Proposed displacement | `0x16=22`; `22*2=44=0x2c` |
| Proposed auxiliary bytes | `0x1400002c`, `0x1400002d` |
| Misplacement | `0x206−0x2c = 518−44 = 474 = 0x1da` |
| Separation from main mapping | Main ends at offset `0x0f`; auxiliary starts at `0x2c`. Offsets `0x10..0x2b` are the intervening 28 bytes. |

**There is independent evidence beyond the census.** I decompressed and disassembled the supplied [OpenBSD kernel](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_images/openbsd76-landisk-bsd.rd), which identifies itself as **OpenBSD 7.6 RAMDISK #70**. In `wdc_obio_attach`, the auxiliary mapping call contains:

```text
0x8c16016e: mov #2,r6       ; mapping length
0x8c160174: mov.l @r2,r5    ; same configured base used for main mapping
0x8c16017c: add #44,r5      ; 44 = 0x2c
0x8c16017e: jsr @r0        ; mapping callback
```
